   - **First run.** The 2004 launcher launches `SinglePlayer\Basic Training`; retail data has no such object (its first tournament is `Training`) and retail Core has no `LaunchSession`.
128. **Descriptions and their panels** [Core `DE_CDescription::ObjInitContents` 0x701f11f0, `ObjProcessContents` 0x701f14e0, `ObjProcessReferences` 0x701f17a0; `DE_CControlCreator::ObjProcessContents` 0x7021c270, `CreateNewControl` 0x7021c520; OMF `DE_CPilotDescription::NewLargeRepresentation` 0x3504f930; `GatherAllObjectsDerivedFromCaller` 0x7014d350]:
   - **Contents.** One chunk: [name][the resource's passive reference: BOOL, size 0x20, {file, object} GUIDs], v2+ [BOOL listed +0xbc], v3 [f32 +0xd4][BOOL locked by default +0xc4][BOOL visible when locked +0xc8][BOOL +0xd8][BOOL +0xcc][BOOL +0xd0]; the chunk carries trailing bytes after these. Defaults: listed 1, the rest 0. On a fresh install the single-player masters open are Training, Practice and North American Open; Ultimate Warrior, IFL Cup and World Open are locked but listed; the others are hidden until unlocked; Cinematic is not listed.
   - **ControlCreator.** Contents v1 [name][i32 layout +0x44][i32 +0x40][i32 text height +0x48 in virtual pixels][f32 +0x4c]; references chunk 1 lists its items. A `Value\String` becomes a `DE_CTextDisplayControl3d` of that height (+0x40 clear turns its wrapping off), an image a control of the image's size, a ControlCreator its own control; layouts 1 and 3 size across (horizontal auto-size), others downwards; 2 and 3 group runs of the same kind. The top level is a page viewer. Session masters' "creators" are a title creator (a line break: the title is drawn into the image), the image and the description text.
   - **Pilots' panel.** `DE_CPilotDescription::NewLargeRepresentation` builds its own: it records the pilot as `LastSelectedPilot` (the fighter chooser paints with it), the name (20 virtual pixels) along the top, the pilot's photo in a framed, white-filled panel (0.1..0.65 high, 0.58 wide), and from 0.6 across a vertical bar of four stat rows (`DE_CPilotStatsRobot`: icon, label, a down-frame bar (0.2, 0.3, 0.3) filled (0.5, 1, 1) by the stat / 100).
   - **GatherAllObjectsDerivedFrom** asks the transfer agent (`FindObjectsDerivedFrom` + `FindMasterObject`) for every master object of the class in the data, each added once; not the scene's objects. With `IsMemberOf` the session scripts' creature chooser (`SetupUIForChooseCreature`) gets the eight `PlayerCreatureCast` robots this way.

129. **What an actor draws** [Direct3D `DE_CD3DRenderActor::GetValidActorType` 0x20023560; Core `DE_CActor::ObjProcessReferences` 0x7008ff90]: an actor is a render actor when one of its components (+0x20, filled only from its references' component list) is a mesh; the type is 1 or 3 by actor flag 0x10 (+0x110). A `Mesh\Standard` that is only a hierarchy child is never drawn: mesh affectors (Blade Pit's `CollisionMesh` cylinder, the jump pads' collision shapes in Desert, Iceberg and Volcano, Canyon's gun trigger) keep their shapes as children. A particle's light is the `Light` child the particle system makes (with the particle's intensity and colour); the effect affector's `Light` property is its template, not a second light.

130. **Collision tester sources** [Core `DE_CCollisionTester::AddSource` 0x7006a090, `AddSourceBranch` 0x7006a0c0]: `AddSource` adds that one affector; only `AddSourceBranch` walks the actor's children (+0xfc/+0x100), adding each affector in it. Scripts' "Will add this's actor tree" comments are copied boilerplate: Blade Pit's blades and spikes add their own mesh/cone affector, and the speaker sphere their noise hangs under them is not a hit source.

131. **Faces without a material; stacked first stages** [Core `DE_CMultiStageTextureProcess::Process` 0x7011a5f0]: a mesh's render elements are its materials' stages, so faces no material covers (the GUI's `TextBoxNext Mesh`, SpaceStation's torus caps) are not drawn. `Process` hands every enabled stage to `CStage::Process`; the stacked flag (+0x2c) only pairs a stage with the one before it (skipping both when it is a reflection the mesh cannot take), so a material whose first stage is flagged stacked (SpaceStation's `Girders`, SemiTranslucent) still draws it.

132. **Compiling classes shipped without VMC2** [Core `DE_CClass::PrepareForExecution` 0x7013abb0, `PCParser::yyparse`, `PCScanner::GetToken`; `DE_CPCodeMethod::ProcessContents`; `DE_CConstantList::NewConstant` in the factories' `Init`]: `vm/compiler.cpp` compiles them; checked method by method against classes that do ship VMC2 (the North American Open's sub-session: 94 of 95 methods identical in opcodes, call targets and literals; Redemption's, PCarManager, PSpinningBlade, PGunTrigger, PAirStrikeMissile alike).
    - **Method elements.** After a method element's source come two bytes: the first is 0 for a virtual method (its signature takes a vtable slot, slots numbered in order of appearance), the second set for an excluded element (Templates, Setup) whose subtree is not compiled. The leading u32 is 0 for a state, 1 for an instant method, 2+ for templates and settings (not compiled). A behavior's compiled methods are its method elements depth first, each followed by those nested in it; interface calls (op 37) number them in that order. Behaviors whose elements name method templates (the robots', the session masters') compile more methods than they have elements.
    - **Names.** A bare call goes to the methods nested in the running state and its enclosing states, then to the most-derived behavior's top-level method (a later behavior's `SetupMatch` replaces an earlier one's though neither is virtual); `.A.B()` paths name nested states. Overloads are chosen by argument types. Members are laid out from the last behavior to the first; object-reference members (non-value native classes) are ref members (ops 62/64, their classes listed in VMC2's ref-member table) and such locals in states local refs (ops 20-22, counted in the method's second count). Finals are inlined; class-typed finals (`final CNamedHandle g_X="..."`) are final instances (op 61). `<Return>` in a string literal becomes a newline.
    - **Code.** Expressions evaluate into frame slots: a call's receiver, then its arguments 4 bytes apart (a script method's return slot first); a binary operator's right operand 8 bytes after the left, or a numeric local used in place; the left operand's type decides and the right is converted (literals folded to it). `&&` short-circuits, `||` does not. Compound assignment to a numeric local works in place. Primitive array elements are written through `<ArrayElementPosition>` and op 7, reference arrays through `<ArrayElementSet>`; native fields with ops 47/50, embedded native values (CAttackInfo's vectors, a coordinate system's axes) with op 229. A native value passed where a primitive is wanted goes through its `Conversion()` operator; an instance passed to a state method is copied (op 42).
    - **Native constants.** 79 script constants (`cgroupAttackable`, `CPilot::sexMale`, `effectStandardFan`...) come from `NewConstant`; native classes' intrinsics come from every shipped VMC2's intrinsic table.
    - **Version skew.** Training's, Practice's and Trophy Room's sub-session classes see `BSinglePlayerSubSession`, which uses the tournaments' `m_TournamentAIDifficulty` that their own masters do not declare; PBoulder's `BPickup` uses a `BOMFEvents` newer than the one it sees. The original compiler reports such a name as "Unknown Identifier"; PSlidingText calls a native (`IsFlatTextEx`) no shipped class uses. Nothing references PSlidingText or Flyers' PCarManager, and Canyon's `BoulderSpawner` (the only user of PBoulder) is a parentless object outside the arena's tree that nothing adds, so boulders never spawn: of the six, only the three sub-sessions run.
    - **First run** [OMFBG.exe `DE_CGameLauncherWindow::ProcessEvent`, REPORT §5e]: with `DE_GLOBAL::FirstRun` (no registry yet) and no `simulatefullscreen`, the first control event 0x3a shows a "Welcome" user information (its text is not in the report) and launches `SinglePlayer\Basic Training` if the data has it; the retail data does not, so only the greeting shows.

133. **The theme's sound** (against a recording of the shipped game's main menu): the menu theme (`theme_short`, styles `1-menu` and `2-stadium`, groove 1 for 26 measures then 2, the stadium style from measure 124) relies on three things the DLS1-era player lacked. Its instruments choose samples by velocity (prog 8: four sample sets for velocities 127, 126, 125 and 0-124, as DLS2 `rgn2` regions); its lead, pads and bass set a 12-semitone pitch-bend range in the band (`nPitchBendRange`), so the lead's per-note sine bends 8192 -> 7168 dip 1.5 semitones; and its parts shape each note with instant or one-tick CC7 curves (127 at the note, 64 two grid steps later). Its drum channel (pchannel 9, from 16 s) selects the GM drum kit (patch 0x80000000); the kit and a melodic instrument share bank 0 program 0, and only the drum flag (bit 31) and bank MSB tell them apart, so matching on bank LSB and program alone played the melodic samples, pitched, as the drums. The strings (pchannels 45-47, from about 1:58) and some drums (keys 61 and 70) are stereo waves: two regions over the same keys and velocities, `wlnk` multi-channel with channels 1 (left) and 2 (right). Each half sounds on its own side only, at full level and unpanned (FINDINGS 135).
134. **Rules display without the frame** [OMF `DE_CRulesDisplay::SetupControls` 0x35063140, `DE_CRulesDisplayRobot` 0x35063520]: on the SinglePlayerLoading folder the page clears its main-frame flag (+0xac) and positions MatchScreenShot, CommentaryText, NextTextBox, MatchName and Progress; the robot's text controls are 14 (commentary, colour 1/0.75/0.8), 19 (match name, 1/1/0.65) and 16 (next match, 0.8 grey) virtual pixels high. Other folders use InfoWindow with the frame. GUI text advances each letter by its font quad's width (`CLetter::FixUp` +0x90), as the overlay does. The theme also has no chord track, so its chord-relative parts play against DirectMusic's default chord, C major with chord root 12: against a zero chord (root 0) the pad on pchannels 29-31 (from 16 s) sounded an octave low; with the default it plays G4-D#5, as the recording does.
135. **The synth the game ran on** [`dmsynth.dll` 5.3, DirectX 9.0b, Dec 2002, from `DirectX/DirectX.cab` on the game disc; static reading of its Ghidra decompile, no execution]: note-on starts a voice for every region whose key and velocity ranges hold the note, all tagged with one note-on serial (`DAT_77498b64`); note-off releases the first playing voice of the key and every voice with its serial (DDKSynth released only the first, so the second half of a stereo or layered note sounded on: the theme's strings piled up from 1:58). A region whose wave link is not channel 1 loads only with the multi-channel flag (`wlnk` options & 2); such a voice skips pan and sends its full level to each output bus whose bit is in the region's channel mask (1 left, 2 right), -96 dB to the rest; the reverb and chorus send buses get the full level either way. Waves themselves must be mono. Its MIDI-to-attenuation table equals DDKSynth's, and its pan table (built at load: `1000*log10(i/127)`, -2500 at 0) equals ours.
136. **Choose Creature's View** [OMF `DE_CChooseFighterUIManager::SetupControls` 0x35056720, `DE_CChooseFighterUIManagerRobot::ProcessButtonClickEvent` 0x35057270, `DE_CChooseFighterUIManager::Begin` 0x35056b20, `DE_CChooseFighterModelPickerUIManager::ObjInitContents` 0x35053fb0 / `SetupControls` 0x35054070, its robot 0x350551b0 / 0x35055890 / 0x350559f0, `DE_CFighterModelDescription::NewStrippedFighterModel` 0x35038940; Core `DE_CCamera::ObjProcessContents` 0x70187a70, `FindCamera` 0x70187c60]: the chooser's Window holds a strip from 0.93 down: the Model choice to 0.7 across and View from 0.73 to 0.97. View does nothing for a fighter without models; otherwise it clears the chooser's begun flag (+0x9c) and pushes the picker (+0x154). The picker (folder LoadFrame; transition on, no main frame, no backdrop, not auto-destroyed) builds a new scene with `LoadScene\ChooseFighterModelPickerEnvironment`, a scene viewer control over the robot's whole control area looking through the environment's `EnvironmentCamera`, and a copy of `LoadScene\FighterTemplate` holding the last selected pilot (`DE_CPilotDescription::LastSelectedPilot`) and the model's stripped model (a new creature model with an instance of the description's model, +0x5c, its texture managers' images replaced by name from +0xe4), placed under `RobotPedistal`; idle events pass that scene's time. In the LoadFrame rectangle: a name label (bottom 0.3, 0.05-0.95), arrows with the `VCRFastBackward` / `VCRFastForward` images (0.25-0.45 and 0.55-0.75 across, 0.35-0.65 down) that step the chooser's model index (+0x184, wrapping), Back (from 0.7 down, 0.05-0.45, Esc) and Next (0.55-0.95, Enter). Next sets the picker's +0x118 and returns; the chooser's Begin then chooses its highlighted fighter at once, with no door transition (+0xa8 cleared). A Camera's contents: v3 [name][field of view +0x2c], v4 adding [near +0x30][far +0x34]; defaults 80, 10, 100000. `ChosenModel` (ChosenModelCaller 0x35056500) is the current model description's +0xa8, the GUID its resource reference (+0x98) names: a `FighterModel` (each robot's Model1 file has Paint1-4), which `CFighterDescription::NewResource(GUID)` (0x35033080) constructs, parents to the fighter and installs as its creature model (vtable +0x118); with no GUID it takes the description's default (+0xdc). The pedestal turns by `BActorRotator` (SharedObjects): its render-tick `Tick` sets the Y rotation from `Scene().PreciseTime()` × `RotateYSpeed` / `RotateTime` (1000 by default) × 360, so it moves with the picker scene's own clock.
137. **Session recordings (replays)** [Core `DE_CSession::Begin` 0x701f9090 / `End` 0x701f9400 / `Update` 0x701f8ab0 / `Replay` 0x701f9900, `DE_CSessionRecord` 0x702025f0..0x70204390, `DE_CStateManager` / `DE_CStandardStateManager` (`CreateCurrentState` 0x701aeea0, `UpdateForReplay` 0x701af350, `BuildID` 0x701b1e00), `DE_CClassExecutorInstance::GenerateStatus` 0x70145930 / `ProcessStatus` 0x70145b50; Creature `DE_CCreature::ObjProcessContents` 0x15014820 / `BuildID` 0x15014c20 / `PackState` 0x150150b0; OMF `DE_CRecordingListUIManager` 0x350619e0..0x350626d0; full notes in `re/specs/replay-recording.md` and `re/specs/replay-playback.md`]: a recording holds no input or events, only the state history of every recordable state manager in the session. It starts in `DE_CSession::Begin` when the session is the server, `Gamepley\Record Session` is on and the master description's +0xfc (contents v1 BOOL) is set: Demolition, Last Man Standing, Team Demolition and Last Team Standing, so also the single-player matches, which are sub-sessions of them. A state is taken when a script calls `Register*State`: the owner's executor instances' status (their `replicate` members, then per frame a flag byte, the class method index and IP offset, and the frame's `replicate` locals; references as the target's state-manager ID), then for a creature its position and orientation when its contents' +0x300 / +0x304 say so (contents v3/v4: four BOOLs or a u32 of ID flags +0x1d0, then two squeezed values) and its physics. Managers match records by ID: a creature's is its component owner's, then its path (flag 0x40) or name (8), GUID (4) and creation time (2). Playback rebuilds the session from the master description and the recorded modifiers only, as a replaying server (+0xa0); every object whose manager matches a record is replay-driven (+0x78: no states of its own, `IsStateAlterable` false) and takes the newest recorded state at or before the scene time; the scripts re-create everything else, the fighters through their pickers' restored states; past the recorded end (+0x6c) the session ends. In our engine a restored state must also run at once for session nodes (their brains only advance when a hook switches state), the loading screen must run with no clients, and the replayed fighters run no AI commands; then a practice match replays to within a few units of its recording. The VCR panel (`DE_CVCRControls`, OMF 0x35064de0 / 0x35065760 / 0x35064ff0) only drives the scene clock (`TemporalMoveToScale`, `TemporalPassTime`): paused at first, a 50 ms step, slow 1/2..1/8, fast 2X..16X, rewind as a negative pass, the slider seeking; a seek or rewind back also brings back everything that measures time since it last ran (physics, AI timers).
138. **Teams** [OMF `DE_COMF2Fighter::ObjPrepare` (the block after the pilot lookup, +0x608), `TeamIndex` 0x3502a6e0, `IsEnemy` 0x3502a6f0, `IsEnemyFighter` 0x3502a740, `DE_CFaction::TeamIndex` 0x35052130]: a faction's team is its INT value "TeamIndex" (the team games' scripts set it on the factions they hand out). When a fighter is prepared, a pilot with a faction gives the fighter that team (+0x49c, -1 otherwise). `IsEnemyFighter` is false for itself and for a fighter whose team is set and equal; `IsEnemy` asks it for fighters and takes anything else as an enemy; `NumEnemies`/`Enemy` list the linked fighters that take this one as an enemy. With these the team matches (the IFL Cup, Team Demolition, Last Team Standing) put each pilot's faction's fighters on one side.
139. **Mesh normals** [Core `DE_CStandardMesh::CalculateNormals` 0x7012fb10, `AddNeighborFaces`]: a mesh's normals are computed at load, not stored. Each face (0x44 bytes: three vertex indices, three corner normals at +0x8, the face normal at +0x2c, smoothing groups at +0x38, three adjacent faces at +0x3c) gets the normalised cross product (a near-zero one gets z = 1); `AddNeighborFaces` gathers, recursively across the adjacent faces, every face sharing a vertex with it; each corner normal is the face's own normal plus the normals of those faces that share a smoothing-group bit and use that corner's vertex, normalised. The face flags in the data are these smoothing groups (1, 2, 4 ... bitmasks), and the adjacent faces always index the mesh's faces.
140. **Pilot panel and list look** [OMF `DE_CPilotDescription::NewLargeRepresentation` 0x3504f930, `GetPilotDescriptionText` 0x35050870; checked against screenshots of the original at 1024x768]: under the name (the top 0.08) a row from 0.1 to 0.65 holds the photo (to 0.58 across, in a down frame) and, from 0.6 across, a column whose top 0.73 is the four stat rows and the rest "Colors" (`$SG113626`) over a bar (spacing 4) of three up frames filled with the pilot description's colours (+0x118, +0x124, +0x130); below, from 0.66, a centred text display (margins 0.013) with `GetPilotDescriptionText`: the `PilotDescription` value, "BIO UNNAVAILABLE" when empty, then "\n\nRECON DATA\n\nObserved Preferences:\n\n" and the AI stat descriptors only where the unlock check (vtable +0x140, index 100 + k) passes. In the screenshots the list's selected row is (0, 0, 128), the classic Windows highlight `ColorSelectedItem` returns, and list rows are 20 pixels (`SetMinEntrySize(0, 0x14)`) with ~19-pixel text, no title above the list.
141. **Robot shadows** [Direct3D `DE_CShadowCaster` 0x20035cf0, `Update` 0x20036540, `CShadow::Setup` 0x20036000 / `DoneAddingLights` 0x20036aa0 / `AdjustShadowMapQuality` 0x20036920 / `UpdateShadowMap` 0x20036220; full notes in `re/specs/shadows.md`]: `Preferences\Renderer\Shadow Type` (default 0, the Video panel's None / Low / Medium / High, renderer +0x19c) with Lights on. Every render affector owning a 0x40 mesh (the robots, Warlord's weapons, the challenger truck) casts; nothing reads the 0x20 shadow-target flag, so the shadow lands on every projector-lit mesh its cone touches, never on robots. Each shadow is a private cone light of mode 2 (ZERO / INVSRCCOLOR, draw layer 2, after the additive projectors) whose image is a render target: the caster drawn flat grey = the strength I (TFACTOR) on black with a 1-pixel border, through a camera of twice the penumbra with half extent tan(fov) / 2 (about 6% wider than the projector). Low: one shadow from 4r straight above; Medium: one from the intensity-weighted mean of the static lights reaching it; High: that and one from the other lights. The light point is held 3r..6r from the caster, penumbra atan(r / L), near L - r, far 2L + 4r; I is floored at 0.3 (scaled by its share when two), capped at 0.6 except overhead. Map 256 >> level (128 >> level for Low/Medium), the level rising with distance (5q, q = 1 - 200 / (tan(fov / 2) depth)); refreshed every frame within 750 units, less often further, never behind the camera. A darkening projector multiplies each touched polygon once: our multi-pass materials must not be darkened once per pass.
142. **The observer camera** [Creature `DE_CObserveCommand` (`ObjInitContents` 0x15021e90, `Update` 0x15023400, `MajorMove` 0x15022780, `EvaluateCreaturesBeingWatched` 0x15022f80, `PositionWatchQuality` 0x150223f0), `DE_CCreature::CommandObserveInterestingCreatures` 0x15017bc0 / `CommandObserve` 0x15017960 / 0x15017a80, `DE_CCreature::DeltaTick` 0x15016700, `DE_CCommandManager::SetCreature` 0x1501d8c0 / `ReassignExecutors` 0x1501dd30 / `Execute` 0x1501de30 / `DeltaTick` 0x1501e1b0, `DE_CDigitalOrientationPropulsionExecutor::Tick` 0x15029500, `DE_CMouseAxisNavigationExecutor::DeltaTick` 0x1502a190 / `Point` 0x15029ea0, `DE_CTeleportExecutor::Tick` 0x1502c4b0, `DE_CCreature::SetAsUserControlled` 0x15016ca0, `DE_CMouseAxisController::RetrieveAndClearAnalogAxisMovement` 0x15028260; Session `DE_CStandardSession::SetCamera` 0x4500f400; Core `DE_CRenderer::SetCamera` 0x70184030, `IncrementMomentumFLOATCaller` 0x701d9f90; scripts `BObserverCamera`, `BStandardOMFSession`; OMF `DE_CVCRControls::ObjUpdate` 0x35064ff0]: the flying spectator is `Session.DEObjects` `ObserverCamera`, a creature the session copies (`StandardSessionAssureObserver`) and shows with `StandardSessionForceObserverCamera` whenever the action stops (before every round, between rounds, at the end), when the user's fighter is defeated in Last Man Standing / Last Team Standing, and for the awards and pilot quotes; `StandardSessionAssureCameraReadyForAction` (READY) and the user fighter's own `SetCamera` give the view back. `CSession::SetCamera` sets the renderer's camera, which sends a `RendererSetCamera` generic event (Object1 the renderer) to the scene's relays: `BObserverCamera` (de)activates on it, and the HUD's `BRenderActorIfCamInTree`, `BAnnouncer` and the environment effects follow the view. `Camera(0)` is the first actor of the creature's references 2.0 (the observer's `ObserverCamera` actor; a fighter's is its `FighterCamera` copy). The script flies the creature every rendered frame (thrust 0.0004/ms, drag 0.0001/ms, cap 0.2/ms, turns of -0.2 degrees per mouse unit) from what the command manager's executors leave it: the observe command (priority 2, type 8) owns a MoveTo (required distance 20, 10 when following) and a LookAt, both sub-commands the manager never updates; each controller info (one per direct `CreatureController` child: the gamepad [DigitalOrientationPropulsion, Teleport], the mouse axis [MouseAxisNavigation, Teleport]) takes the command its most confident executor handles (propulsion 1 for a MoveTo; mouse axis 1 for a LookAt, 0.5 for a MoveTo; teleport 0.01 x the distance to a MoveTo's destination, so beyond 100 it jumps), re-chosen only on a reassignment (after a sort, a teleport, a major move). Propulsion writes the local offset to the destination, less 1250 x the local momentum and 0.9 x the required distance, as the creature's FLOAT values `PropelOffsetX/Y/Z`; mouse-axis navigation sets the analog axes to the yaw and pitch error in degrees (the direction's y clamped to +-0.8); teleport writes the destination as the position and, on every other info that frame, turns Z to the LookAt target. The observe command watches the creature with the highest GlobalInterest and everything within min(2.1 x their mean distance, 0.4 x the teleport destinations' extent) of it (re-evaluated every 10 s of frame time), frames the box of their positions raised 15 (25 during pilot quotes), and on a major move picks the best `StationaryViewPoint*` (asked for with `GenericEventGatherTeleportDestinationActors`, which `BEnvironment` answers with the children of `ObserverTeleportDestinationParent`) by `PositionWatchQuality` ((d - ideal)/ideal + 1 beyond the ideal distance |box| x 0.9 + 40, twice that closer, x2 / x5 per blocked sight line), then orbits (0), sits at that vantage point (1) or follows the first watched (2, explicit targets) at random; major moves come when the summed interest jumps by more than 40% (2 s apart) and when the timed test fires; the field of view is atan(|box| / radius) x 57.29578 x framing x 1.2, eased at 0.0006..0.0015 per ms (60 while user-controlled). It uses MSVCRT `rand()`. The drama states (+0xb0 1..3) are unreachable: `g_DramaticTimeChanges` is 0 and nothing writes it. +0x124, the best sampled quality that gates the timed move, is never initialised. Manual Observer binds the observer's controllers to the user (`SetAsUserControlled(0)`): the keys thrust in the camera's frame, the relative mouse turns it, the autopilot stops and the FOV is 60; Auto gives it back (-1). `IncrementMomentum(float)` stops at zero speed instead of reversing.
143. **The notification dialog** [Core `DE_CNotification::Display` 0x7017fa70 → `DE_CDialog3d::UserInformation(title, text, g_IconUserInfo, 1)`; checked against a screenshot of the original at 2046x1424]: the title defaults to "User Information". The box is 0.737 screen heights wide: a light grey bevel (about 0.0056 high), a title bar 0.049 high shaded navy from dark on the left to bright on the right with a close box at its right end, a grey separator, and a black body with the core image `Not_UserInformation` top left, the text centred beside it and OK (0.29 of the width) bottom right in a blue frame. The dialog is modal: clicks elsewhere do nothing, OK and the close box dismiss it.
144. **Pilot RECON DATA** [OMF `GetPilotDescriptionText` 0x35050870, `UnlockStatDescriptions` 0x35050cf0, `DE_CPilot::StatByIndex` 0x35051330; Core `DE_CDescription::IsLocked` 0x701f2640, `Unlock` 0x70010aa0, `ObjFindChildByName` 0x7003dad0]: after the bio, for each of the 22 AI stats k whose description id 100 + k is unlocked, the text gets "\n\nRECON DATA\n\nObserved Preferences:\n\n" once, then "<name>: <level>\n" with the name from a fixed table (spellings as shipped: "Preimptive Attacks", "Grappls") and the level word (None, Abysimal, Low, Below Average, Average, Above Average, High, Huge, Massive) from the stat's `CVariedStat` +0x14, the level in the pilot's data. `IsLocked(id)` for id != 0 is true unless the description's DLock registry record lists the id; `Unlock(id)` adds it (the script's `Unlock(lockID...)` passes ids too). `UnlockStatDescriptions(n)` fills 0..21, swaps each slot from 21 down to 0 with slot `rand() % 22` (the CRT's), then walking from the end unlocks the first n still locked. `BSinglePlayerSession::SinglePlayerMatchComplete` calls it with 3 for each opponent's `Pilot().FindChildByName("PilotDescription")`, but `FindChildByName` looks only at direct children and a pilot's are its description (named after the pilot) and its image, so the call finds nothing: in the shipped game RECON DATA never unlocks in play, and ours does the same. (Wrong: the picker renames the description "PilotDescription", FINDINGS 258.)
145. **Billboard and ring particle renderers** [Particles `DE_CBillboardedQuadParticleRenderer::DE_CInstanceInfo::Process` 0x40010620 / `SetParticleVerticies` 0x400119b0, `DE_CRingParticleRenderer::DE_CInstanceInfo::Process` 0x40015c00; Core `DE_CCothType::GetValue` 0x7010a760; full specs in `re/specs/billboard-renderer.md` and `re/specs/ring-renderer.md`]: billboard +0x6c is the streak length (not a depth offset) and +0x84 a near limit for the camera-space modes. Streak modes 1–5 are kites in screen space at the particle's depth: sides at the particle, tip half a width past the end point, back half a width behind, half-width size × height × actor scale; directions towards the actor (1), the particle's momentum unrotated (3), the actor's momentum (4), the actor's Z (5); 2 reads an unset direction. Modes 14–23 are quads from the raw particle position in the generator's space (XY, XZ, YZ, actor-turned, or the particle's orientation); mode 20's fourth corner stores y = a'. The z offset (+0x80) changes only the depth, in modes 0 and 13. Cone mode 1 kills or fades a lens flare by the camera direction against the actor's Z (umbra / penumbra of a cone affector), cone mode 2 fades once |Z.z| > 0.9, cone mode 3 sizes by the glow value at the particle's age; the width / height scaling of modes 1–2 only feeds the bare-size test of modes 6–23. "Coth Type" is a bump, 1 − x/3 − x²/9 + 0.0486773x³ − 0.0081129x⁴ with x = (2t − 1)². Rings: the plane mode is +0xd4 (+0xe0 and +0xdc are never read), +0xd8 freezes the UVs, 20 fixed segments with sin on the frame's first row and cos on its second, U = detail × (rotation + i/20), mode 3 built in screen space with no heights, ages unclamped.
146. **Match snapshot** [`BSubSessionLauncher::SubSessionCaptureSnapshot`, `BStandardOMFSession::LocalPlayerCreatureProcessEvent`; Core `DE_CRenderTargetImage::Capture` 0x70193570; OMF `DE_CRulesDisplayRobot` 0x35063520]: when the local player's fighter is knocked out (`g_OMFFighterHealthExpended`) or lands a kill hit (`g_OMFAttackerKillHit`), the sub-session owner makes (once) a copy of Session.DEObjects' `SnapshotImage` (Image\RenderTarget, `g_FrameSnapshot`) as its own child, points its observer at the fighter (and the victim), ticks it by 1000000 ms to place it, and captures the observer's `Camera(0)`: the renderer draws the scene from that camera into the image, (n + 1) × 256 pixels square (+0x7c; n = 0 from the script). The rules display's MatchScreenShot control is filled white with that image when the owner session has a child named SnapshotImage; otherwise it gets the master description's medium representation (+0x124).
147. **Particle stepping, transparent order and attack trailers** [Core `DE_CParticleGeneratorInstance::SetTime` 0x701d5e70, `DE_CParticleRenderer::Process` 0x701c30a0; Direct3D `DE_CRendererDirect3D::Render` 0x20026e10, `DE_CClipVolume::DrawLayer` 0x2002a2a0, `DE_CGenericShader::ActivateAndDrawPatches` 0x20032ed0; OMF `DE_CAttackTrailer::Process` 0x35040250; full write-up in `re/specs/particle-stepping.md`]: the particle renderers step their instance from the render process (type 300000) once per rendered frame for each visible element, to the renderer's scene time, with the step clamped to 0..1000 ms (generator +0x7c, set only by ObjInitContents). "Visible" is the actor's Renderable flag and the element being active: no frustum or distance culling. Particles in an instance draw in birth order. Blended particle shaders sit in clip-volume layer 8 (6 with positive placement times), bubble-sorted highest first on a static key plus −(the actor's view z), only when a shader joins. The attack trailer ages its points in Process, once per draw: `life -= (t − last) × decay` with t the fighter's creature ticks; V = 1 − the life before; U 0 on the +width vertex unless ReverseUVs (no caller); blend SRCALPHA / ONE.
148. **Effects leftovers** [Particles `DE_CFourFaceTriangleParticleRenderer::DE_CInstanceInfo::Process` 0x400145d0, `SetParticleVerticies` 0x400119b0, ring `Process` 0x40015c00; Direct3D `SetupCameraView` 0x2000b540, `DE_CClipVolume::DrawLayer` 0x2002a2a0 / `SetupComplete` 0x2002a450, `DE_CShader::GetShader` 0x20032170, `CreateTextureFromImage` 0x2001bce0; OMF `DE_CSparkProcess::Tick` 0x3502f890; Core AVI frames 0x70189840; specs `re/specs/render-order.md`, `re/specs/particle-leftovers.md`, `re/specs/avi-images.md`]: Direct3D's camera space is left-handed (z forward, y up, focal y = focal x = ((ResX − 1) / 2) / tan(fovx / 2)). The billboard corner UVs put the image's top-left texel at the quad's top-right: sprites are mirrored left to right. Layer 8 (blended first passes: particles, trailers, blended mesh parts) sorts highest first on 1000·placement + 10·sub + G + shader index − view z, G = master object index × 1000 + a per-root counter for particle renderers only; the sort runs only when a shader is set up (a new element, or a texture / material change). Blended first passes of every mesh (arena glass, Gargoyle's wing membranes) sit in that same layer, so they draw after all opaque geometry, including fighters drawn later. Renderable is per actor, not inherited. FourFaceTriangle facings 1-3 use the mesh space's axes (world or actor), the third row crossed with that space's Y, and add ActorViewPosition to a world generator's already-world position. Colour mode 0 writes a slot's colour once, when the live count first passes it. Ring +0xdc / +0xe0 are never read. The spark process is a 16 ms temporal ticked once at creation; the sparks move per draw. Glow shaders are made per mesh, so moved actors keep them. AVI frames come from the codec's default output, copied into X8R8G8B8, with mip level k the frame's top-left (W >> k) × (H >> k) crop (SetMipMapLevel is not overridden), or one level when the image's mip flag (DE_CImage +0x4c, after type / width / height) is off.
149. **DirectSound setup and 3D** [AudioPlayer `DE_CDirectXAudioPlayerFactory::ProcessEvent` 0x10001350, `DE_CDirectXNoiseElement::CheckForReload` 0x100055d0, `DE_CDirectXSpeakerElement::CreateNewAudiopath` 0x10004ca0; DirectX 9.0b dsound.dll from the disc; `re/specs/directsound.md`]: DirectMusic's `InitAudio(DYNAMIC_STEREO, 128, ...)` runs the synth at 22050 Hz; the primary is set to 44100 Hz. Wave buffers are STATIC | CTRLVOLUME | CTRLFREQUENCY, plus CTRL3D only if `GetCaps` reports free hardware 3D buffers, with DS3DALG_DEFAULT, min = max distance 9999999 and nothing else set: no hardware 3D means every sound is centred. dsound's WDM software 3D (used when hardware creation fails) is a −500 mB base plus, per stereo channel, trunc(1000 log10(P)) with PR = |Z| / (|Z| + rh − X) (speakers at ±90°, y ignored, front / back symmetric, a blend toward the far side within 2.5 units); its emulated waveOut path gives the far side (1 − |s|)^1.5 instead. dsound's own mixer resamples linearly with a 12-bit fraction. `DE_CStreamNoise::Length` is the whole file's bytes × 1000 / nAvgBytesPerSec, in ms.
150. **Music bands and the DLS2 filter** [AudioPlayer `DE_CDirectXNoiseElement::CheckForReload` 0x100055d0, `DE_CDirectMusicSegment::GetDefaultBandInterface` 0x10009010, `Activate` 0x10004460; DirectX 9.0b dmsynth.dll `FUN_77486b10`, `FUN_77489d80`, `FUN_77491c20`; `re/specs/directmusic.md`]: only the theme's segment has a band track; for any other segment AudioPlayer takes the first band in the segment's container, makes a band segment of it and plays that before the music segment whenever it differs from the speaker's last band. dmsynth's filter: a DLS2 cutoff (abs cents) and Q (cB) give column (cutoff − 1200 log2(fs / 440) − 6900 + 10688) / 100 and row round(Q / 15) of three 16 × 89 Q30 tables (K, B1, B2; y = K x + B1 y1 − B2 y2, DC −0.75 row dB, peak +0.75 row dB), linear between columns, plus (CC74 − 64) × 100 cents hard-wired, recomputed at each control point and ramped like the volumes, after the sample interpolation and before the volume. The theme's pad2 (11921 cents, Q 40) and Snakeirons' seq (Q 0) use it, both swept by CC74 curves. Music audiopaths are dynamic stereo, so dmsynth's reverb is off.
151. **DirectMusic's style player** [DirectX 9.0b dmstyle.dll `FUN_7740b74f`, `FUN_77415eed`, `FUN_7741619d`; dmime.dll `FUN_773043bb`, `FUN_77304367`, `FUN_7730ac73`; `re/specs/directmusic.md`]: pattern selection tries criteria 2 → 5 (groove level in range, embellishment, ...) on the style's longest patterns first, one time-signature group at a time, and picks among the survivors with `rand() % n`; with none it falls back to the style's first pattern. GothicKit skipped one-measure groove patterns, which silenced about a dozen song segments. Curves follow four 193-step shape tables (0..19200) and send a value every max(16, dur × k / |Δ|) ticks (k = 100 for pitch bend, 3 for controllers); reset values go out only when a segment is stopped inside the curve's reset window. DX8 band items (`bd2h`) carry a physical time.
152. **One rand() stream** [Core `DE_GLOBAL::SystemInit` 0x70012b30, `MATH::Rand` 0x70025e70 / 0x70025ea0, `MATH::CenterRand` 0x70025ec0; Creature `DE_CObserveCommand`; OMF `DE_COMF2Fighter::GlobalInterest` 0x3502ae40]: Core, Creature, OMF and Particles all import `rand` from MSVCRT.DLL, so every main-thread draw (scripts' Random, particles, the observer, bounces, noise choice) shares one stream, seeded once with `srand(time(NULL))`. Rand(f) = rand() f / 32768, Rand(n) = (rand() n) >> 15, CenterRand(f) = rand() f / 32768 − f / 2. A fighter's GlobalInterest call recomputes its cached value when more than 300 ms (absolute) have passed since the last; the observer calls it every Auto frame, re-phasing what the fighters' target choice and IsAlive read. `DE_CObserveCommand` comes from MSVCRT `operator new` (not the debug CRT), so +0x124 holds whatever the heap gives, most likely 0.
153. **Gargoyle's wings** [Core `DE_CAnimationSequenceManager::Activate` 0x701c0070, `SetTime` 0x701bfdd0, `SetEmptyTime` 0x701c05e0, `ActivatePrelude` 0x701bff50, `ObjProcessReferences` 0x701bf750; scripts BWings, BGargoyle]: the body's AnimationSequenceManager also drives the eight wing actors, and 27 of the body bank's 126 sequences have wing streams; WingAnimationManager drives the same actors from WingAnimationBank (one constant sequence, WingsCurledUp). A manager writes actors' locals: SetTime with an active sequence writes only channels with a stream; ActivatePrelude captures the channels' current locals, writes the sequence at t, captures again and deactivates, after which SetTime blends each channel's local from the first capture to the second (unclamped). BWings' CurlUp is that prelude over 40 frames; BGargoyle calls ManualControl(5) every frame of a wing move, so the wings curl up 5 frames after it ends, from wherever the body left them. WingBase is identity and nothing writes it.
154. **Visibility for effects, and the output conversion** [Core `SetRenderedCaller` 0x7008e420 / `SetUnrenderedCaller` 0x7008e470; against the recording of the shipped game's menu theme]: `SetRendered` / `SetUnrendered` set only the actor's own Renderable flag (+0x108) and notify its renderers; children keep theirs, so an effect under a hidden parent goes on stepping and drawing. The game's DirectSound buffers and the synth's 22050 Hz output reach the device through a converter the disc does not hold; band levels of the recording against ours (aligned on its envelope) fit a 4-point Hermite interpolator best: flat within about 1.5 dB to 9 kHz with image energy above 14 kHz at the recording's level, where linear interpolation is 2-2.5 dB low at 6.4-9 kHz with 4-6 dB too much image energy and a windowed sinc leaves 25-65 dB too little.
155. **Per-camera HUD visibility** [Core `IsRenderedCaller` 0x7008e640; SharedObjects `BRenderActorIfCamInTree`, `BRenderIfCamInTree`, `BRenderIfCamIsParent`; Pilots `BDefaultHUD::Render`; Session `BComboDisplay`]: `IsRendered` returns the actor's +0x108. The render behaviours, set up per actor (the HUD, its targeting sight, the combo text), compare each attached renderer's `CameraActor().TopParent()` with the required actor's top parent (or the camera with the parent itself) and call `SetRendered` / `SetUnrendered(Renderer)`, again on every `g_SystemRendererSetCamera` event the scene relays. `BDefaultHUD::Render` returns early (and deactivates the target effect) while its HUD is not rendered. There is no camera or frustum test behind `IsRendered`.
156. **AVI colour conversion** [Windows XP SP3 avifil32 `CGetFrame::SetFormat` 0x73b5f4a0 / 0x73b5f73d, ir50_32.dll 0x738d41a0 (MMX) / 0x738d4cbd, ir41_32.ax 0x580d9780 / 0x580d95f0, iccvid.dll; run under emulation; `re/specs/avi-colour.md`]: with a NULL format, GetFrame asks the codec for its default (`ICM_DECOMPRESS_GET_FORMAT`), 24-bit BI_RGB from all three. Indeo 5 with MMX (every machine that has it; the switch is the registry's FloatingPointProcessor key) and a width that is a multiple of 8 (all shipped files): BT.601 studio range in 1/128 fixed point with saturating 16-bit arithmetic (149 (Y − 16), 204 V, 50 U + 104 V, 258 U), chroma interpolated per 2 × 2 pixels with floor averages, and a 4 × 4 ordered dither through per-pixel bias constants. Indeo 4.1 (and Indeo 5 without MMX): one chroma sample per 4 × 4 block, clamp tables indexed by Y + chroma offset + a 4 × 4 dither, with green taking a carry from the packed red and blue fields. Cinepak: R = Y + 2V, G = Y − trunc(U / 2) − V, B = Y + 2U, clamped. Ours matches all three exactly.
157. **Hotkeys** [Core `DE_CImpulseRobot` 0x7001f650..0x700209d0 (`TestKeyboardEvent` 0x70020020, `TestMouseEvent` 0x70020340, `Pulse` 0x70020450, `IsImpulseEvent` 0x700207e0 / 0x70020850), `KeyboardProc`, `DE_CMouseInput::TimerCallback`; OMF `DE_CInGameMenuUIManagerFactory::Init` 0x350590e0; scripts BVCRControls, BChatRelay, BStandardOMFSession, BTrophyController, BThrusterJet; `re/specs/impulses.md`]: one global robot holds named impulses, each with one key byte, one mouse button, required modifiers and attached targets; the first `SetDefaultHotkey` / `SetDefaultMousekey` for a name wins and nothing is saved, so hotkeys cannot be rebound (the Controls panel lists only the 12 described gameplay actions, Input2 controller bindings). A `WH_KEYBOARD` hook feeds every key message, autorepeat included; a key matches by the character it types (letters either case) or, for keys that type none, by its virtual key; required Shift / Ctrl must be held, others are ignored, and Alt is never recorded. A match pulses a CGenericEvent (Text = the name, INT1 = 1 on key up) to each target; `IsImpulseEvent(e, name)` is true for a generic event with that Text and INT1 0. The UI sees the same keys; neither consumes them. Impulses: In Game Menu (Esc), VCR Controls (V; the VCRControls slot modifier, in replays), Say to All (T), Network Monitor (Ctrl+N), Cycle Camera Forward / Backward ([ / ]) and Video Card Test (Ctrl+V) only when debugging, Switch Trophy Camera (left mouse), Fast / Slow ThrusterJet (left / right mouse).
158. **Loading, registry, first run, classes without code, descriptions' medium representation** [Session `BRulesDisplay`; Core `DE_CRegistry::DE_CRegistry` 0x7020db10, `GetResourceRegistryPrefix` 0x70041160, `DE_CDescription::Lock` 0x701f2460 / `IsLocked` 0x701f2640, `DE_CClass::Compile` 0x7013b7f0, `ObjGenerateReferences` ~0x70135d50, `DE_CDescription::ObjProcessReferences` 0x701f17a0, `NewMediumRepresentation` 0x701f2020; `re/specs/frontend-misc.md`]: no module loads session elements; the match sub-sessions' `BRulesDisplay` does (WaitingToBegin → IsLoadingNeeded → LoadingCoreElements, one `SessionLoadElements` per 6 session updates with the task "Loading <name>", → WaitingForSessionToBeSequenced → ActivatingSceneWatchers → Done, which closes the UI stack once the match has started). The registry is a `<name>Registry.DEObjects` transfer-agent file (default name "Diversions" + "Registry"), keys being folder paths to typed value objects; a description's unlocks are `ObjectInfo\<guid>\DLock`, a DataStream of u32 ids (GUID lower-case 8-4-4-16), `Lock(id, add)` removing the id and appending it when adding. The retail exe imports none of `FirstRun`, `FindMasterObject`, `LaunchSession`, so it has no first-run greeting. A class whose save found an error notification is written without VMC2; at load `Compile` marks it compiled but skips code generation after a link error, so such classes ran nothing; `CFlatTextEx` exists in no module. The description references chunk v6 is casts (BOOL-terminated), icon, large, components, replacements, medium, then 0x28 filler; every tournament and game type has an `Image\Standard` medium representation, which the rules display stretches over MatchScreenShot before a snapshot exists (the tournament's in single player, the game type's otherwise).
159. **GUI controls and the screens built from them** [Core `DE_CControl3d` family, `DE_CGUIStandardInterface`, `DE_CTopControl3d`, `DE_CDialog3d`; the 2004 development build's `DE_CGameLauncherInterface` (readable there); OMF `DE_COptionsUIManager` panels, `DE_CVCRControls`, the replays browser, `DE_CChooseFighterUIManager`; Core `DE_CSession::SetAsServer` 0x701f8110; `re/specs/controls.md`, `re/specs/screens.md`]: a control's edges are fractions of its parent plus pixel insets and lengths in physical pixels (code converts with ConvertToVPU when it wants 800 × 600 sizes; FINDINGS 126's "virtual pixels" is wrong). A frame is a 5-quad bevel whose corners take the interface's highlight / light / shadow / dark-shadow colours with a 0.2 face gradient, swapped when down. The launcher overrides ColorFace (grey 0.5, α 0.5), the active caption (blue, α 0.5), button text (white), window back ((0, 0, 0.1), α 0.7), tooltips, a 30-pixel title bar, 16-pixel list text and 24-pixel scroll bars drawn from the OMFBGGUI images CI.VertTile / HorTile / VertStatic, without arrow buttons; the rest are Windows system colours. Lists hard-code their selection (0, 0, 0.5). The Options page opens with no panel and applies the open one on every button click; the Video panel has no Full Screen check; resolution and bit depth apply at the next start. The session player limit is 20 in single player and `Network\Max Clients` for joinable sessions.
160. **Ending a match sub-session; the camera at the end of a match** [scripts `BSubSessionLauncher` (`SubSessionDestroySubSession`, `SubSessionCaptureSnapshot`), `BStandardOMFSession` (`LocalPlayerCreatureProcessEvent`, `StandardSessionForceObserverCamera`, `StandardSessionAssureCameraReadyForAction`, `LMSFighterDefeated`), `BObserverCamera` (`AssureActivated`, `SessionProcessEvent`), `BFighter` (`Ground.Death`, `FighterChangedState`, `FighterProcessEvent`), `BFighterStats` (`NotifyOfChange`)]: a tournament ends a finished match with `m_SubSession.DeleteThisLater()`, never `EndAndDie`; deleting the session ends it, its scene and environment going with it, before the next match's sub-session is made. When the local player lands a killing hit the owner points the observer at the player and the victim (`CommandObserve(player, victim)`, a 1000000 ms tick, then the snapshot); when the action stops, `StandardSessionForceObserverCamera` activates the observer, and an observer not already active then watches the interesting creatures (GlobalInterest over 0.1), the defeated ones having dropped to 0 (`InterestLevelDead`). An observer that is already active (the player was defeated earlier) keeps the player-and-victim command. `FighterChangedState` restores InterestLevelNormal whenever the fighter's status is not dead, and `Ground.Death` gets the fighter back up while it is not; the status becomes dead on `g_OMFFighterHealthExpended`, which `NotifyOfChange` sends whenever health is at or below 0, and becomes normal again on `g_OMFFighterRecoverFromStun`, which it sends after it when a stunned fighter's stun has recovered.
161. **Text size, InitGUI, the help tree, the session info panel, dialog dragging** [Core `CFontInfo::Update` 0x701caf70, `DE_CFlatText3::CalcScreenCoordinates` 0x701cdb30, `DE_CTextSizeRobot` 0x700c4c20, `DE_CListControl3dEx` (+0x124 tree), `DE_CHelpUIManager`, `GetEntriesWithParent` 0x7004c280, `NewSessionInfoControl` 0x70203d00, `ControlActor3d::ProcessEvent` 0x700ea330, `DE_CTitleBarRobot` 0x700db1b0; OMF `DE_CSessionInfoInGameRobot` 0x3501aae0; the 2004 development build's `InitGUI` 0x408b20]: a font's glyphs sit at their mesh coordinates plus the lowest letter's offset (+0x2c; -0.227 for Font_Ariel), one font unit drawn at the text size (controls: fs pixels, square; screen-space `DE_CFlatText3`: x scaled by the viewport width and y by its height), so dividing by the tallest extent (1.41) drew text 1.41x too small. InitGUI hangs a new UI parent actor, scaled (1, 4/3, 1), on the window-filling PostSceneView control with `AddCustomControl`: the GUI stretches to the window. Help is a `DE_CListControl3dEx` with its tree flag set; an entry lists under its direct parent object when that is a help entry too, with Minus / Plus icons (10 px at depth x 20 + 5), text at (depth + 1) x 20, the highlight around the text only, collapsed at first. The session info panel shows the master description's medium representation and one 45-pixel `NewIcon` per modifier, 50 apart (an icon-less modifier draws white); its second description is empty for recordings. A title bar's client area relays drag events 0x21 / 0x22 to its dialog, which moves by the mouse delta.
162. **Text primitives are screen space; status bar corners; read-only and ghosted controls; the GUI's scenes** [Core `DE_CPrimitiveGeneratorMesh::NewText` 0x7012de60, `CTextPrimitive::Setup` 0x7012df90, `TextSetPositionCaller` 0x7012ba00, `TextSetHeightCaller` 0x7012ba40, `DE_CFlatText3::SetScreenPosition` 0x701cddd0, `SetTextSize` 0x701cdd30, `Process` 0x701cd550, `UpdateText` 0x701ccf20, `CalcScreenCoordinates` 0x701cdb30; `CStatusBarPrimitive::UpdateBar` 0x7012d210, `SetColor` 0x7012e160, `NewStatusBar` 0x7012ce10, `CQuadPrimitive::PositionAsBox` 0x7012dba0, `StatusBarSetPositionCaller` 0x7012b840; `DE_CControl3d::ReadOnlyOn` 0x700e5760 / `ReadOnlyOff` 0x700e58b0, `GhostOn` 0x700e20b0, `IsAcceptInput` 0x700e2250; `DE_CScene::ObjInitContents` 0x7006e8d0, `TemporalUpdate` (autopause test), `DisableAutopause` 0x7000fa90, `IsPaused` 0x7000fa80, `DE_CTopControl3d::AssureRenderObjects` (its scene), Session `DE_CStandardSession::Begin`, OMF `DE_CChooseFighterModelPickerUIManagerRobot` ctor; scripts BText, BCrowdResponseSlot, BPilotQuote, BTimeAccelerate, BActorSpawner, BDoorMover]:
   - **Text primitives.** `NewText` gives each text primitive a `DE_CFlatText3` and calls its `SetScreenPosition` (0.5, 0.5, 100), which sets flag 8; `SetPosition` and `SetHeight` go to `SetScreenPosition` and `SetTextSize(float)` (flag 0x40: the size is not divided by the viewport height). `Process` makes the flat text's own render element screen space (mode 3) when flag 8 is set, and `UpdateText` always ends in `CalcScreenCoordinates`, so a text primitive is drawn in screen space whatever its mesh; `SetAsScreenSpace` (mesh +0x5c) only matters to quads and status bars (the branch in `CTextPrimitive::Setup` that reads it is dead: +0x28 is zeroed first). FINDINGS 116's "the crowd-response texts stay in world space" is wrong: BCrowdResponseSlot, BPilotQuote and BTimeAccelerate never call `SetAsScreenSpace` and still place their texts with screen positions.
   - **Status bars.** A status bar is two `CQuadPrimitive`s (body +0x44, cap +0x118); `SetPosition` sorts its corners; `SetColor` stores the colour (+0x1ec, alpha +0x1f8) and every corner of both quads gets it (the fourth corner's alpha is +0x1f8, the same alpha), so there is no per-vertex fade. Left to right (mode 0): h = bar height, p = x0 + (x1 − x0) × value; with p − h > x0 the body spans x0..p − h with UVs (0.1, 1) (0.9, 1) (0.9, 0.5) (0.1, 0.5) at (x0, y0) (p − h, y0) (p − h, y1) (x0, y1) and the cap p − h..p + h with (0.1, 0.5) (0.9, 0.5) (0.9, 0) (0.1, 0); otherwise the body is hidden and the cap spans x0..p + h with its left u 0.1 − (p − h − x0) / h × 0.5; hidden when p + h ≤ x0 (so value 0 still shows half a cap). Bottom to top (mode 3) is the same along y with the texture turned: body UVs (0.1, 1) (0.1, 0.5) (0.9, 0.5) (0.9, 1) and cap (0.1, 0.5) (0.1, 0) (0.9, 0) (0.9, 0.5) at (x0, lo) (x1, lo) (x1, hi) (x0, hi); `SetUVs` cuts the cap likewise. Modes 1 and 2 (right to left, top to bottom) are not updated at all. `SetValue` does not clamp.
   - **Read-only and ghosted controls.** `ReadOnlyOn` (flag 0x100000) adds a child control (+0x1c4) with its visual on and face black at alpha 0.5 over the control (*inference:* it takes the mouse in the control's place; ours refuses input); `ReadOnlyOff` deletes it. OMF uses it for Video's Apply, the connection robot's Bytes Per Second, the Controls panel's Assign / Remove, and the replays browser's Play (until a record is selected). `GhostOn` only sends control event 0x29 down the tree and nothing handles it, so a ghosted control looks and acts normal. The check box and radio button have no other look: their mark's alpha is 1 checked, 0 unchecked (`DE_CButtonRobot::SetUp` / `SetDown`), the `CheckMark` and `Circle` images are in COREImages.
   - **Scenes and autopause.** `DE_CScene::TemporalUpdate` passes no time while a UI page autopauses (or the app is inactive) only for a scene whose +0x1c0 and +0x1bc are both clear and that has no remote clients. `ObjInitContents` sets +0x1c0, so a scene autopauses only when its maker clears it: `DE_CStandardSession::Begin` does for the session's scene; the top control's scene (`AssureRenderObjects`: ambient 0.2, `AutoUpdate`), which holds the GUI's actors, keeps it and never autopauses; the model picker's robot clears +0x1c0 but calls `DisableAutopause` (+0x1bc), so its scene never autopauses either. GUI scripts' `Scene()` is therefore a clock that runs on through choosers, the in-game menu and replay speed changes (BCommonButton's rotators read `PreciseTime`, BDoorMover tells door sounds apart by `Time`, the backdrop's spawners and button executors are temporals), and a GUI script's `Scene().Add` (BActorSpawner's lightning flashes) adds to the GUI scene. `IsPaused` is +0xf0, the scene's own pause, not autopause.
163. **Glow super-sampling** [Direct3D `DE_CRendererDirect3D::SuperSampleForGlow` 0x200283b0, `UpdateGlowMaps` 0x20026ce0]: five passes (the loop runs while i < 5.0, 0x200fcd58) added with ONE/ONE into the cleared spare target. Pass 0 has radius 0; passes 1-4 (jump table 0x20028850) all take the call's radius, 0.0078125 (2 texels). Each tap's texture matrix offset is (sin a, cos a) x radius with a accumulated across every tap and pass in steps of 45 degrees (90 with 4 taps, when the device has fewer than 8 texture stages), converted by 0.0174533; so every ring samples 45..360 degrees, the same taps. The result is sat(avg(centre)) + 4 x sat(avg(ring)), saturated; we had three passes with the second ring turned by half a step.
164. **Bump reflections: fixed function unless GeForce FX** [Direct3D `DE_CGenericShader::AddStage`, `DE_CPixelShader::Create` 0x20022880, `DE_CD3DDevice` creation (adapter-name test at 0x2001657e), `FindBumpFormat`, `DE_CShader::CStage::SetStates` 0x20031b60, `DE_CD3DTexture::CreateTextureFromImage` 0x2001bce0; Core `DE_CStandardImage::GenerateBumpUVs` 0x70195b70, `DE_CImage::CheckImageType`]:
   - **Which path.** The bump pixel shader is created only when device +0x204 is set, and that is set only when the adapter description starts "NVIDIA GeForce FX" (0x2011ee58; it also turns the glow off there), the pixel shader version is at least 1.4 and the stage signature is `FVF274-220-2220-44-1-` or `FVF274-440-2220-44-1-`. Every other card with `D3DTEXOPCAPS_BUMPENVMAP` and more than two blend stages takes the fixed-function path, so a modern card would too.
   - **Stages.** When a reflection stage (element flag 0x100) follows a stage whose image has a `Bump` child, Bump Mapping Enabled is on (registry default 1) and the device has a bump format (Q8W8V8U8, else V8U8), AddStage inserts a `D3DTOP_BUMPENVMAP` stage between them with the previous stage's image and coordinates; if that image is all white (CheckImageType +0x48 bit 8) the previous stage itself becomes the bump stage.
   - **du/dv.** The bump texture is the stage image's own channels 6 and 7 (each byte + 0x81, signed): GenerateBumpUVs stores them from the `Bump` child's height (channel 0, or 4 for a grey image) as (h[x-1] - h[x+1]) x 127 >> 8, + 127, and across rows likewise. The `Bump` child's pixels are not used at draw time.
   - **Matrix.** Without a `RotateValue` on the `Bump` child, BUMPENVMAT = BumpFactor x identity (texture +0xbc). With one, [cos a, -sin a; sin a, cos a] with a = RotateValue x 0.000723, and the factor is ignored (City and Iceberg water, Volcano lava; their RotateValue is a MathFunction over the arena's scene time).
   - **Result.** The reflection stage's coordinates are the camera-space reflection vector turned to world (renderer +0x4f4), and D3D adds du M00 + dv M10, du M01 + dv M11 to their first two: the reflection's world X and Y. We had the GeForce FX shader, with the `Bump` child's RG as the vector.
165. **Fighter paint, linked** [OMF `DE_CCreatureModel::Setup` 0x35037470 (from `DE_CFighterDescription::NewResource`), `DE_COMF2FighterImageFilter::Filter` 0x350350e0, `UpdateStaticColorRemapperTable` 0x35036150, `DE_CPilot::UserDefinedColor` 0x3504c8e0; Core `DE_CStandardImage::AssureUpdatedImage` 0x701950f0]:
   - Setup walks every descendant of the fighter's FighterModel once, by name prefix: `RGBMain*` and `ReflectionMask*` get the fighter's filter in their filter list, `RGBStatic*` join the filter's static list, `RGBScaled*` its scaled list. Nothing in the data names an image RGBStatic, and Chronos's `RGBScale` misses the prefix. The filter saves no images or colours; the images' filter references are empty in the data.
   - The filter runs when an image is decoded (AssureUpdatedImage), on channels 0-2. The lightened colours are used only for an image named exactly `ReflectionMask` (so Mantis's `ReflectionMaskBugs` takes the plain ones). Then every scaled image of the same size is added byte by byte, times clamp(0.7 L + 0.3, 0, 1) with L = 0.35 (0.7 r + 0.8 g + 0.5 b) of colour 0 + 0.10 of colour 1 + 0.05 of colour 2; then every static image unscaled.
   - Colour 0 is the pilot's faction's colour (pilot +0x32c, `DE_CFaction` +0x314: its RGBA advanced reference, chunk v1) when the pilot has a faction (`CPilot::SetFaction`); colours 1 and 2 are always the pilot's.
   - There are no run-time static or scaled lists (damage overlays): Setup is their only writer.
166. **No facing test for light projectors, verified** [Direct3D `DE_CD3DLight::UpdateTouchedPolygons` 0x2002cd00, `DE_CD3DRenderMeshElement::FillProjectedVertexBuffer` 0x2002e4f0; Core `DE_COctree::GetPotentialCollisions` 0x702065c0, `CIndexNode::GetPotentialCollisions` 0x70205be0]: the touched polygons are those in octree cells whose boxes overlap the light's affector (or sphere), and FillProjectedVertexBuffer copies every one; nothing compares a normal with the light. Only the pass's camera culling (`D3DCULL_CW`) removes faces.
167. **Shadows, corrected** [Direct3D `CShadow::DoneAddingLights` 0x20036aa0, `CShadow::Setup` 0x20036000, `DE_CShadowCaster::Update` 0x20036540, `DE_CD3DTexture::CreateTextureFromImage` (render targets), `DE_CShader::CStage::SetStates`, `DE_CD3DDevice::SetCurrentDevice` / `GetFilterCaps` 0x20016dd0; Core `DE_CTCoord3D::RotateX` 0x7022b600, `DE_CActor::FocalPoint` 0x70091070]:
   - **The 0.6 cap applies to Low too.** After the 0.3 floor, DoneAddingLights computes L = |C - P| (disabling the cone when 0) and caps I at 0.6 (0x20036c5d) before it tests the overhead flag (0x20036c74). FINDINGS 141 and the spec had the cap on the directional path only, which made Low shadows much darker than the others.
   - **Overhead cone.** Setup resets the cone's axes to identity and calls RotateX(pi/2), which maps each row (x, y, z) to (x, y c - z s, z c + y s): Z = (0, -1, 0), Y = (0, 0, 1). It looks straight down from C + (0, 4r, 0).
   - **Caster centre.** The affector actor's vfunc +0x154 is `DE_CActor::FocalPoint`, its world position (+0x70); only creatures override it, so it is the sphere affector's position.
   - **Map.** A render-target texture: one level, `D3DUSAGE_RENDERTARGET`, the display mode's render-target format (X8R8G8B8 first). The projector stage sets the device's default filters, which GetFilterCaps makes linear (anisotropic where offered; default max anisotropy 1).
   - **Texture Detail.** Low (2) adds one level to every caster's base map level.
168. **Session scenes; deleting a session** [Session `DE_CStandardSession::Begin` 0x4500ecf0, `Scene` 0x4500efa0, `End` 0x4500ef40, `ObjDestroyContents` 0x4500e6f0, `DE_CStandardSessionModifier::OwnEnvironment` 0x450161e0 / `Scene` 0x45016040, `DE_CStandardClient::Scene` 0x45002c10, `DE_CScene::OwnTree` 0x450183c0; Core `DE_CSession::End` 0x701f9400, `ObjInitContents` 0x701f7e90, `ObjDestroyContents` 0x701f7f20, `SetMaster` 0x701f9060, `Begin` 0x701f9090, `EndAndDieCaller` 0x701f7d90, `DE_CScene::ObjDestroyContents` 0x7006ea60, `DE_GLOBAL::HouskeepingObjects` 0x70011b00; scripts `BSubSessionLauncher`, `BMaster` (`StartNewMatch`)]: every standard session makes its own scene in `Begin` (`g_SceneFactory`, +0x16c) and `Scene()` returns it (before `Begin`, the scene viewer's (+0x160) scene); a session modifier's and a client's `Scene()` is their session's, an actor's the scene it was added to. `OwnEnvironment` has the scene own the environment's tree. `DE_CSession` +0x6c is the master modifier (`SetMaster`), +0x60 the master description, +0x7c the attached modifier descriptions: `End` destroys the clients and deletes those three; `DE_CStandardSession::End` then deletes its own scene and its slots. A scene's destruction destroys its actors and owned trees. Both `ObjDestroyContents` call `End` (vtable +0x174, as `EndAndDie` does after `DeleteThisLater`), so a session deleted with `DeleteThisLater` ends when housekeeping destroys it, and what `End` deletes goes at the next housekeeping (`HouskeepingObjects` destroys only the objects queued on entry). `StartNewMatch` deletes the old sub-session and begins the next in the same call, so for a frame both scenes exist.
169. **`IsMemberOf` and `DE_CSessionMasterDescription::Update`** [Core `IsMemberOfCaller` 0x701f1130, `DE_CSessionMasterDescription::Update` 0x701ff4c0, `DE_CSessionModifierDescription::Update` 0x70201fc0, `DE_CSessionModifier::ObjProcessReferences` 0x70200b10; `re/proto/vtable.py`]: `IsMemberOf` searches only the description's cast list (+0x34). `Update` (vtable +0x12c) makes the description's resource and merges its required casts (modifier +0x34, references v2/v3 list 1) into the description's required casts (+0xf0) and its member casts (+0x48, list 2) into the description's cast list, then deletes the resource. No retail module calls slot +0x12c on a description (the calls through +0x12c are on other classes), so this is the editor's; the shipped data holds its result: each of the 61 `Description\SessionMaster` / `SessionModifier` objects lists every member cast of its resource modifier.
170. **`TestLineSegment` against a mesh** [Core `DE_CMeshAffector::DE_CMeshReference::IsTouchingConeAsLineSegment` 0x700854e0, `UpdateMeshData` 0x70082fc0, `DE_CFaceInfo::SetCS` 0x70083a10, `AssureOctree` 0x700828e0, `DE_COctree::GetPotentialLineSegmentCollisions` 0x702066d0 / `AssureMaximumSize` 0x70207350 / `BuildingComplete` 0x70206240 / `SetupLeafArray` 0x70206360, `DE_CStandardMesh::CalculateNormals` 0x7012fb10 / `Process`, `DE_TCoord3D::CrossProduct` 0x7022b800, `DE_CCoordinateSystem::Set` 0x70025fe0, `DE_CConeAffector::IsTouchingMesh` 0x70080530, `DE_CCollisionResults::ReverseResults`, `DE_CAffector::InternalIsTouching` 0x7007baa0, `NoRepeatOwnersCaller` 0x7007aae0]: a mesh's face normal (`FaceNormal` element) is `CrossProduct(V0, V1, V2)` = (V0 - V1) x (V2 - V1), normalised; on the arenas' collision meshes it points into the solid (BladePit's floors down, its walls away from the centre). Each face's frame (`SetCS`) has that normal as z and +0x7c the plane's offset along it; the segment's ends are taken into the frame and the face counts only when z0 <= 0 and (z0 >= 0 or z1 >= 0), z1 != z0: the start behind or on the plane, the end in front or on it, i.e. entering the solid. The crossing (z0 / (z0 - z1)) must be inside the face (`IsInsideFace`, tolerance 0). Candidates come from the mesh's octree (a regular grid of leaves, built when the actor's +0x1b0 cell size is set) walked by a 3D DDA, but each cell's sorted face ids are merged into one sorted, de-duplicated list, so faces are taken in face order, whichever cells the segment crosses; without an octree, every face in order. Collisions are appended in that order (no distance sort; scripts call `SortByTouchDistance`). With `NoRepeatOwners` (+0x38 and +0x3c) the mesh's new collisions shrink to the one with the least +0x38 field, which this path never writes (fresh array slots are zeroed), so the first. `DE_CConeAffector::IsTouchingMesh` then reverses them: touched and source swap and both normals are negated, so the normal reported faces the start. Depth (with push-back values): -z1 when z0 >= 0, else -z0. `NoRepeatOwners`' skip of affectors whose owner already touched compares against the results' first actor, the scene's line cone, so it never skips here.
171. **Needs: team, area and enemy** [OMF `DE_COMFNeed::DesireLevel` 0x350679a0, `ObjPrepare` 0x35067930, `DE_COMF2Fighter::TeamIndex` 0x3502a6e0, `IsEnemy` 0x3502a6f0, `ObjPrepare` (+0x49c); Core `DE_CUniqueActorProperty::Owner` 0x700937f0, `DE_CSphereAffector::IsTouchingSphere` 0x70089c30; Creature `DE_CCreature::InternalUpdateNeedInfo` 0x15018400]: a fighter's TeamIndex (+0x49c) is -1 unless its pilot has a faction (+0x32c), then the faction's INT value "TeamIndex", read when the fighter is prepared. A need's team (+0x64) is its "TeamIndex" value, else its `Owner()`'s (the affector's owner actor +0x118, else the affector), else -1; with "Area" set its affector's intensity at the creature (vtable +0x164) weighs the desire, and a fighter of another team gets none of flag 8's. The holder checks use `Owner()` too, and flag 4's bonus needs `Owner()` to be a fighter to which the creature is an enemy (`IsEnemy`: `IsEnemyFighter` for a fighter). `InternalUpdateNeedInfo` reads each `TestSphere` collision's first actor, the touched affector (`IsTouchingSphere` stores the target there), from the last collision back, in the scene's affector order.
172. **Single-player opponents** [scripts `BMaster` (`StartNewMatch`), `BSubSession` (`MatchStacyAndGracy`), `BSinglePlayerSubSession` (`Entry`, `SubSessionActivateSlot`), `BCreatureList`; Core `SingleDimensionReferenceShuffleCaller` 0x7014d220]: the computer players' creatures come from the sub-session's creature list: every `CCreatureDescription` in the data that is a member of the required casts (`g_CreatureSPLadderCast`) and user-selectable, shuffled, then popped one per `CreatureListGetRandom`. Nothing excludes the user's creature (`CreatureListExclude` has no caller), so an opponent may fight with the player's creature, never two opponents with the same one until the list runs out. North American Open's first match (Welcome) gives Stacy and Gracy random creatures this way. `Shuffle` swaps from the last element down with one picked by the CRT `rand()` stream (the shared MSVC stream).
173. **A recording's file and its manager IDs** [Core `DE_CTransferAgentWinFile::SaveToFile` 0x700514b0 / `NewUserFile` 0x70051e80, `DE_CTransferAgent::GenerateHeader` 0x70055990 / `ObjGenerateContents` 0x700544e0, `DE_CTransferObjectInfo::GenerateHeader` 0x70055690 (+ WinFile 0x7004fd20), `DE_CSessionRecord::ObjGenerateContents` 0x70202890 / `ObjProcessContents` 0x702029b0 / `ObjGenerateReferences` 0x70202eb0, `DE_CStateManagerRecord::GenerateContents` 0x702042c0, `DE_CDataBlock::Set(char*)` 0x70029140, `DE_CGUID::Get` 0x7001eee0, `DE_CSession::ProcessEvent` 0x701f88a0, session modifier BuildID 0x70200c80; Session slot modifier 0x4501ef50; Creature `DE_CCreature::BuildID` 0x15014c20, `SetInstanceNumberCaller` 0x15014100]: SaveToFile writes the signature block, format byte 2, the agent's own EX count (u32 0), the header block (v9: the agent's v4 info with its WinFile offset, the agent's v1 contents of an empty block, zero GUID, empty GUID pool and BOOL 1, the object count and each object's v4 info: name, class path, GUID, BOOL flag, i32 +0x84, u32 flags, then a sized u32 file offset), then per object its contents, references and hierarchy blocks and a u32 EX count. Contents are compressed only when the object's factory has +0xec set, which the SessionRecord factory leaves 0. Rebuilding Creature.DEObjects this way gives the same bytes except the never-initialised fields (0xcd fill) of the agent's own info and +0x84. A recording is such a file with one `SessionRecord` object named after the server name, references `02 00 01 00` and hierarchy `01 01 00`; the agent is named "Session Recording, <date>", as the file. `DataBlock::Set(char*)` keeps the NUL, except that "" is empty. Manager IDs: a session's is its class path; a session modifier's is its name then the GUID text of its data object (copies share it; the 16 bytes in memory order as lowercase hex, grouped 4-2-2-8); a slot modifier's is its class path (`StandardSessionSlotModifier`), that GUID text, " Slot" and the slot's index in its session's list; a creature's is as FINDINGS 137, with the `SetInstanceNumber` digits (one NUL kept at the end) between its path or name and its GUID, its path running up to the scene's name; any other owner's is empty, so all such managers would share one record.
174. **What a state holds** [Core `DE_CObject::PackState` / `UnpackState` 0x7003de40 / 0x7003de80, `DE_CCoordinateSystem::Decompose` 0x70027230, `DE_CRecordPacker::AddElementID` 0x702049b0, `DE_CSession::AddObjectByGlobalID` 0x701f9a90 / `ExtractObjectByGlobalID` 0x701f9940, `AddSqueezed` 0x70019100, `DE_CSimplePhysics::PackState` 0x701dc5b0; Creature `DE_CCreature::PackState` 0x150150b0; OMF `DE_CFighterPhysics::PackState` 0x3503a5d0 / `UnpackState` 0x3503a9d0 / `RepackState` 0x3503aca0 (read from Ghidra's instruction listing), `DE_COMF2Fighter::PackState` 0x3502a4e0 / `UnpackState` 0x3502a500, `RegisterGetHitState` 0x350296b0]: the executor instances' statuses follow each other with no count. A creature then adds 12 bytes of position (+0x300), three angles from `Decompose` (+0x304: x = atan2(m5, m8), y = asin(-m2), z = atan2(m1, m0)), and its physics' own state with no flag: a simple physics' momentum when its contents' squeezed +0x128 is 1, or a fighter's. The fighter physics writes a u16 mask: 0x10 momentum (12 bytes; at or below 0.01 it is zeroed in the live object instead), 0x08 acceleration (likewise at 0.001), always the position and a u16 heading ((atan2(row0.x, row0.z) + 3π/2) / 2π × 65536), 0x04 gravity and its recovery, 0x100 turn rate, 0x200 forward speed, 0x01 the last actor hit (guide mode 1; with none, the guide bits are dropped), 0x02 the last actor hit by (guide mode 2 with none, as the binary tests it, or after `RegisterGetHitState`), 0x0c00 the guide mode with its power and squeezed frames, 0x1000 squeezed invulnerable frames, and bits 0x20 / 0x40 / 0x80 for skip friction, skip fighter collision and on floor. Unpacking sets every part outright (absent: 0, 1.0 or -1) and the orientation to a pure yaw of h / 65536 × 2π + π / 65536; the fighter then moves its actor to the physics. No controls or input are packed. References are a v1 chunk of a kind byte (0 an object no manager owns, 1 known, 2 null) and for 1 the u32-length ID of the newest manager it owns, matched newest first on playback. Type-2 values are a u32 length around: CText and CData a squeezed length with the NUL and the bytes, CGUID 16 bytes, CGUIDPool `01`, u16 count, the GUIDs, CVector 12 bytes, CCoordinateSystem 36.
175. **Creatures in a replay are not locally controlled** [Session `DE_CStandardSessionSlot::SetupCreatureControl` 0x4501a9b0; Creature `IsLocallyControlled` 0x150130b0, `SetAsUserControlled` 0x15016ca0, `DE_CCreature::DeltaTick` 0x15016700, `DE_CCommandManager::DeltaTick` 0x1501e1b0 / `Tick` 0x1501e000, `DE_CStandardHookupExecutor::Tick` 0x1502b650; Core `DE_CClassExecutorInstance::IsMaster` 0x70145830]: SetupCreatureControl sets creature +0x1f8 and its control only when the session is not replaying, so in a replay no creature is locally controlled. +0x1f8 is copied to the main executor's +0xf8, and `IsMaster` (op 113, `master`) is +0x114 clear and +0xf8 set and the scene not paused: a replayed fighter's `Control.*` and navigation `master` code never runs, and `BFighter`'s `Air.FallFromSpawn` waits for a state. Its pilot still runs: `AttackBestEnemy` and the `Command*` natives, and the command manager's delta tick (gated only by not user-controlled and the scene not paused) drives the hookup executor's `Executor*` methods, which only set `m_AI*` members; the fixed-tick command manager needs +0x1f8 and does nothing. So AI commands run but nothing acts on them; the fighter moves only through its recorded states.
176. **When a restored state runs, and going back in time** [Core `DE_CStandardStateManager::SetupOwnerForState` 0x701af020, `DE_CClassExecutorInstance::ProcessStatus` 0x70145b50, `IsExecuteStatesFromAllInstant` 0x70146440, `DE_CClassExecutor::ObjProcessContents` 0x701434b0, `DE_CScene::PassTime` 0x70070040, `DE_CActor::ObjPrepare` 0x70090de0 / `ProcessEvent` 0x70090ed0; Session `DE_CStandardSessionModifier::Update` 0x45016050, slot modifier `Update` 0x4501f660; Creature `DE_CCreature::UnpackState` 0x15015160, `SetTime` 0x15015d20, `RewindTime` 0x15015eb0]: `ProcessStatus` rebuilds the frames, sets the state method and clears the sleep but runs nothing; on the no-frames byte it leaves the instance alone. `UnpackState` then runs each restored instance whose ClassExecutor has +0x4c (contents v4/v5, after the name, entry, interval and the +0x48 BOOL; default 1) up to its first wait at once; the next modifier `Update` fires the state-changed hooks. Every Session.DEObjects executor has it except ServerMonitorSystemExecutor and EStateReplicationMonitor; the fighters' executors do not, so a fighter's restored state waits for its next tick (the creature version runs flagged instances at the state's time and later re-ticks from it to now, at most 100 ticks). Going back, `PassTime` clamps at 0, resets the tick sets, rewinds each tick-based creature (`RewindTime`: its states rolled back and +0x2fc = 2, physics and brain held until a state comes), passes the negative delta to the delta-tick list, and sends `TimeRewound` to the scene: in a replaying session each actor carries its "ScenePrepareTime", and one prepared after the new time with flags +4 & 6 (run-time copies, inferred) deletes itself. Session-node brains with no record and script sleeps (which count ticks) carry on, so "anything not driven by a state carries on" holds only for those.
177. **`Value\FLOAT\MathFunction`** [Core `DE_CValueFLOATMathFunction::ObjInitContents` 0x70110300, `ObjProcessContents` 0x70110420, `ObjProcessReferences` 0x701105c0, `GetValue` 0x701100f0 / 0x70110770 / 0x701106d0, `DE_CValueFLOAT::GetValue` 0x7000eff0 / 0x7000f000, `DE_CObject::GetFLOATValue` 0x7003d0e0]: contents after the FLOAT chunk a v1 chunk with the operation (+0x54, default 3); references a v1 chunk of three { BOOL, reference } inputs (+0x34, +0x40, +0x4c). `GetValue(t)`: a = input 1 at t, b = input 2 at t, each the function's own value (+0x2c) when unset; operation 0 b + a, 1 a - b, 2 a / b (1 when b is 0), 3 a x b, 4 input 1 at b (0 without it), 5 the product of the inputs at t. `GetValue()` is `GetValue(0)`, and `GetFLOATValue` calls `GetValue()`; a plain `DE_CValueFLOAT` returns +0x2c whatever t, so time reaches these functions only through a value a script sets.
178. **SceneTime** [scripts `BSetSceneTime`, `BRenderUpdater` (SharedObjects `PSetSceneTime` / `ESetSceneTime`); Direct3D `DE_CShader::CStage::SetStates` 0x20031b60]: the arenas with moving water or lava (City, Iceberg, Volcano; OMFBGGUI has them too) keep a plain FLOAT named `SceneTime` under the environment, and `BSetSceneTime` (a `BRenderUpdater`: `RegisterRenderTickHook` on Entry) sets it to `Scene().Time()` every render tick. Their `MathFunction`s multiply it (op 3, input 2 = SceneTime): City's water `RotateValue` x 1.0, so the bump matrix turns 0.000723 rad per scene ms, and the texture scrolls (City's water 1e-5 per ms, City's highway 1e-4, Volcano's lava 2e-5) or compose an `Inter` over it (op 4, `UShift`: frac(t x 0.0005)). `SetStates` reads `RotateValue` and `BumpFactor` with `GetFLOATValue` every time the stage is set, so the matrix follows the clock (and stands at BumpFactor x identity while RotateValue is 0).
179. **`ScaleModifier` on a texture stage** [Core `DE_CScaleModifier::ObjInitContents` 0x7011dc40, `ObjProcessContents` 0x7011df30, `ObjProcessReferences` 0x7011e170, `Process` 0x7011e2b0 (from its instruction listing), `DE_CTextureManager::Process` 0x701327c0 / `ObjProcessReferences` 0x70131c40; Direct3D `DE_CShader::CStage::SetStates` 0x20031b60 case 2]: contents v2: the name, BOOL dynamic (+0xe0), then nine FLOAT advanced references (v1: three, then the BOOL): scale x y z (+0x2c, +0x40, +0x54, default 1), three more (+0x68, +0x7c, +0x90, default 0), translation x y z (+0xa4, +0xb8, +0xcc, default 0); the references chunk has the nine advanced references in that order. A texture manager's references list (+0x34) holds its image and its modifiers; for a render material it makes a UV element (0x37) with an identity matrix and has each referenced object process it in list order. On a UV element (type 8, 0x37 / 0x38) the modifier replaces the matrix: the frame's rows scaled by +0x2c, +0x40, +0x54, the translation (+0xa4, +0xb8, +0xcc), then m[2][0..1] = m[3][0..1]; the three middle values are never read. SetStates sets it as `D3DTS_TEXTUREn` with `D3DTTFF_COUNT2`: u' = u sx + tx, v' = v sy + ty. A dynamic modifier registers itself on the element (vfunc +0x40, 300000) to process it again; a static one processes once. Volcano's `WaterScale` drives its middle value (+0x7c) with SceneTime, so that water does not scroll in the original.
180. **Bump Mapping Enabled and Texture Detail are the applied options** [Core `DE_CRenderer::LoadOptions` 0x701837c0, `SaveOptions` 0x70183ae0, `ApplyUserOptions` 0x70183db0, `ApplyOptions` 0x701851a0; Direct3D `DE_CRendererDirect3D::ApplyOptions` 0x200269f0, `DE_CGenericShader::AddStage` 0x20033500, `DE_CStandardRenderTexture::CreateTextureData` 0x20012580]: `CRenderOptions` (`g_GlobalOptions`, 12 dwords) holds Lighting +0, Reflections +4, Bump Mapping +8, Glow +0xc, High Quality Geometry +0x10, Full Screen +0x14, Shadow Type +0x18, Texture Mip Map +0x1c, resolution, refresh, bit depth. The Video panel saves the registry; Apply copies the user options into `g_GlobalOptions` and each renderer's +0x184. AddStage inserts the `D3DTOP_BUMPENVMAP` stage only while options +8 is set, and when +8 or +0x1c changed since the last apply the Direct3D renderer deletes every render texture, so the shaders and textures are rebuilt with the new values. The shadow caster's Texture Detail is renderer +0x1a0 (options +0x1c), the applied value, not the registry. +0x1c also sets the mip level textures are made from (entry 196).
181. **The renderer draws one scene** [Session `DE_CStandardSession::Begin` 0x4500ecf0; Core `DE_CSceneViewerControl3d::AttachScene` 0x700c19f0 / `DetachScene` 0x700c1be0, `DE_CRenderer::SetScene` 0x70185160]: Begin, when the session needs a display and has no scene viewer yet, asks for the scene viewer (`GH_GET_SCENE_VIEWER`) and attaches its own new scene to it; `AttachScene` detaches the scene it showed and hands the new one to the top control's user scene, and the renderer's `SetScene` replaces its scene (+8). So once `StartNewMatch` has begun the next sub-session, its scene is the one drawn, and the old one, still alive until housekeeping (FINDINGS 168), is not.
