So the original too shows the main menu for the delay (three 20 ms frames in a screenshot run), then the iris nearly closed, then the opening; only the five empty frames were missing from ours, which now waits them out (`AppManager::frames_to_wait`).
240. **The drifting squares behind 2.123's menus** [OMFBGGUI (2.112 / 2.123) `MainMenuTunnelEffects\Spots\SpotsRenderer` (BillboardedQuad), `SpotGenerator`, `SpotmeshSpawner`, `SpotAcceleration`, on `V.Backdrop`; Direct3D `DE_CGenericShader::AddStage` 0x20033500 (disc)]: the squares are the backdrop's `SpotsRenderer` particles: additive (blend 0), colour (0.62, 0.86, 1) at alpha 0.09 (colour mode 2), billboards 5 × 5 times the particle size (mode 0), spawned from the spots mesh and drifting by `SpotAcceleration`. Through 2.112 the renderer textured them with `Blinklightimage` (a soft round light, in the `BevelSurfaceBlinkLights` folder); 2.123 deleted that folder and the renderer's reference with it (its contents are byte-identical), so the renderer has no image. `AddStage` makes the stage whatever the material holds, and a stage without a texture modulates by white in Direct3D's fixed pipeline (inference), so the original draws them as the plain translucent squares ours draws. Nothing to change. *Corrected by 327:* a renderer with no image makes no material and no generator instance, so 2.123's spots are never spawned or drawn.
241. **Buttons are push buttons; the grey inside the hovered OK** [Core (2.123) `DE_CButtonControl3d::ObjInitContents` 0x200c2270 (disc 0x700bd810), `New` 0x200c3770 (disc 0x700bea60), `CreateVisualControl` 0x200c28a0, `DE_CButtonRobot::ProcessControlEvent` 0x200ca280 / `SetUp` 0x200ca770, `DE_CControl3d::CreateVisualControl` 0x200e8d00, `SetFillColor` 0x200e5dd0, `SetFrameWidthOnly` 0x200e89a0, `DE_CGUIStandardInterface::CreateControl` 0x200f9be0; `DE_CDialog3d::UserInformation` 0x200f8310 (p123b/dec/userinfo.c); checked against notif.png]: `ObjInitContents` sets the type (+0x1f8) to 1 and `New` leaves it, so every `New` button, the notification's OK and the confirm dialog's Yes / No included, is a push button: `CreateButtonControl`'s 2-pixel up bevel with the launcher's ColorFace face (grey 0.5 at alpha 0.5, the 0.2 gradient). `re/specs/controls.md`'s "New makes type 0, a plain button" is wrong. That face is the grey inside the hovered OK; the overlay's frame child is made after the button, so its blue glow lies over it, and the box's own fill (black 0.2, clear when hovered) under it. Ours drew OK plain, so only the glow showed; OK and Yes / No are now push buttons and the glow is painted after them. The screens' other `New` buttons (Options' Apply, Controls' Assign / Remove / Set Defaults, the Replays' Delete / Rename, the VCR's Snapshot, Choose Creature's View) are push buttons too and are still drawn plain.
242. **`SatisfyMasterRequirements` and the Choose Cast page** [Core (2.123) `DE_CSession::SatisfyMasterRequirements` 0x20201790, `AreMasterRequirementsSatisfied` 0x20201ba0 / caller 0x201ff790, `AssureSessionModifiersCreated` 0x20200d80, `DE_CSessionModifier::ObjProcessReferences` 0x20209450, `DE_CSessionSettings::ObjInitContents` 0x201ed030 / `Begin` 0x201ed660 / `Setup` 0x201ed6d0 / `SetupControls` 0x201edd40 / `AddChosenSettingsToSession` 0x201ed7e0 / `UIManagerComplete` 0x201ed930 (vtable +0x158) / `SaveModifiers` 0x201ed970 / `LoadModifiers` 0x201edb40, `DE_CSessionModifierList::FillList` 0x201ef510, `DE_CSortCast::Sort` 0x201ef7b0, `CreateUniqueName` 0x201ed4a0; OMF (2.123) `DE_COMF2Manager::ProcessEvent` 0x107157a0]: a session modifier's references (v2/v3) hold its required casts (+0x34) and member casts (+0x48); `AssureSessionModifiersCreated` makes each attached modifier description in the info's GUID pool (+0x8c) a modifier first. A required cast (master description +0xf0) is met when some modifier's member casts hold it. When one is not, `SatisfyMasterRequirements` (target: the app manager) makes the page with *all* the required casts (not only the unmet ones) and the casts whose own cast list holds `COREMeshes\OptionalSessionModifier` (Block Penetration, Crowd Response), `Setup`s it (which already loads the saved modifiers) and stacks it; `ProcessEvent` then returns, and the page's Complete brings it back to the same tail: a user client "User", `SinglePlayerPlayed`, `AddIfAbsentLocalSession`, `Begin`. In 2.123 every single-player master requires Session Speed; Host's LMS masters add LMS Score Limit. Folder `ChooserListNextBack` (+0xcc); markers `Window`, `ListWindow`, `Next` (Enter), `Back` (Esc, `SetLastUIManager`). `AreMasterRequirementsSatisfied(0)` (the script native; `BCreatureAndPilotPicker`, `BSubSessionLauncher.SubSessionBegin` and BMatchServer use it) is false while the session's page (+0xbc) is active and not auto-selecting, true for a session with +0xa4, else the test above. `Begin` with auto-select loads the saved modifiers and completes at once when they satisfy the master. A cast's modifiers are the listed `Description\SessionModifier`s that are members of it, ordered by the description's f32 +0xd4 (Normal 1.4, Turbo 1.55, Nitro 1.73; None, Light, Medium, Heavy; Sedate, Normal, Excited); the default is the one the registry's `Session Options\Last Modifiers\<cast name>_<cast guid>` names (unique names are `<name>_<DE_CGUID::Get>`), else the first with +0xd8 (Normal Speed, Block Penetration Light, Crowd Response Normal). `UIManagerComplete`: the chosen descriptions (required casts first, then optional, each once, nulls skipped) replace the info's GUID pool, the pool is saved under `Session\Modifiers\<master description guid>`, INT `StartingMatch` = `g_StartingMatch` goes to the info, then the base Complete. Info +0xe8 is `IsContentControllable` (1 by default, cleared for "SinglePlayer", which also sets +0xec); `g_OptionalModDisabledMaster` is never written. Ours: `AppManager::satisfy_master_requirements`, `SessionSettings` in `ui.cpp`, `master_requirements_met` in `session.cpp`.
243. **What the cast choices do: the modifiers and `SetTimeScale`** [Core (2.123) `SetTimeScaleCaller` 0x20071af0, `TimeScaleCaller` 0x20071b20, `DE_CScene::ObjInitContents` 0x20072760, `PassTime` 0x20073f30; scripts `BSessionSpeedModifier`, BMaster's `StartNewMatch`; OMF (2.123) `DE_COMF2Fighter::NumEnemies` 0x1072a990 / `Enemy` 0x1072a9d0; Core `RemoveEventRelaysCaller` 0x200914a0]: the chosen descriptions become the master session's modifiers (their resources: SessionSpeedModifier, BlockPenetrationModifier, CrowdResponseModifier, each with the description's values), and BMaster copies `AttachedSessionModifierDescriptionGUIDs()` into every match's sub-session, whose `SubSessionBegin` stops with "SubSession has not satisfied it's MasterRequirements." unless Session Speed and the score limit are among them. `BSessionSpeedModifier` runs `Scene().SetTimeScale(GetFLOATValue("SessionSpeed"))`: the scene's double +0x188 (1 from `ObjInitContents`), by which `PassTime` multiplies the time it passes (keeping the fraction in +0x190). So 2.123's single-player fights run at 1.4 times real time at Normal speed; before Choose Cast the port ran them at 1. The crowd response slot modifier, which now runs, uses `CCreature::NumEnemy` / `Enemy(int)` (vtable +0x174 / +0x178: a plain creature has none; a fighter counts the fighters of its scene list +0x338 whose `IsEnemyFighter(this)` holds) and `CActor::RemoveEventRelays` (the caller's `DE_CPCodeHookAutodestruct` relays on the actor), all three now in the port.
244. **The Choose Cast layout** [Core (2.123) `DE_CChooseCastRobot` ctor 0x201ee5a0, `ProcessButtonClickEvent` 0x201f0510, `ProcessSelectedObjects` 0x201f05b0, `SetCurrentModifier` 0x201f0670; `DE_CCurrentSettingsRobot::RefreshList` 0x201ef8c0 / `ProcessPostDraw` 0x201efba0 / `ProcessButtonClickEvent` 0x201ed1e0 / `SetModifierDescription` 0x201ee420; `DE_CModifierControlRobot::FillButtonControl` 0x201f0a50 / `SelectModifier` 0x201f09f0; `DE_CSingleButtonGroupRobot::ProcessButtonClickEvent` 0x201ed250; `DE_CControl3d::NewStandardHeader` 0x200ec4d0; `gNewDescriptionTop` 0x2009b350; `BUTTON_SELECTED_COLOR` / `UNSELECTED` initialisers 0x201ed0d0 / 0x201ed0f0]: ListWindow holds the casts' list (sorted by name, no icons) to 0.5 down (0.4 with content control, then "Available Arenas:" VPU 14 at 0.4-0.45 and the arenas' list); in single player `gNewDescriptionTop("AI Difficulty", INT combo on the app manager's difficulty)` from 0.55 and, from 2.112, the Starting Match combo from 0.75 (labels 20 pixels high with ":" added, the control under them). Window: Current Settings 4 pixels in, VPU 80 high, framed down VPU 2, a standard header (an up frame 3 pixels wide in the face colour, the title in SizeListControlEntryText centred, VPU 4 + 4 taller than the text) over a row of VPU 32 boxes every VPU 40 from VPU 10 (centred when it fits), the current cast's box filled white, its inner square (VPU 2 in) the chosen modifier's icon or red when none; a box click selects its cast. Under it (from VPU 80 + 8) the selected cast's modifier control: b = (width - 19) / 6, buttons b - 4 square at 12 + row × b down and 12 + col × b across, six to a row, two rows at most, each face cyan (0, 1, 1) when chosen, transparent otherwise; from 2b + 20 a VPU 2 down panel with the modifier's `Description` value (VPU 13, centred; "Description Unavailable" without one). Showing a cast clicks its default, and every button click stores the cast's choice, its Current Settings icon and the registry's Last Modifiers. Next completes, Back returns to Choose Match. The robot's per-cast registry loads in the constructor are dead code.
245. **A fighter's model hookup** [OMF (disc) `DE_CFighterDescription::NewResource(DE_CGUID&)` 0x35033080 (2.123 0x10733930), `DE_CCreatureModel::Setup` 0x35037470 (vtable +0x118, 2.123 +0x11c), `DE_CCreatureModel::ObjProcessReferences` 0x35037240, `DE_CCreatureModelFactory::InternalNew` 0x35036d70, `DE_CFighterModelDescription::NewStrippedFighterModel` 0x35038940; Core `DE_CActor::FindPropertyByName` 0x70090fa0, `DE_CActor::ObjChild` 0x7007cb80; a probe over every `FighterModel`'s references in both data sets]: a data `FighterModel` is a `DE_CCreatureModel` (factory path `FighterModel`). Its references after `DE_CObject`'s chunk are v2 [instanced objects (+0x48)][properties (+0x34)], and v3 adds [BOOL + creature description (+0x54)]. `NewResource` builds the robot (`DE_CDescription::NewResource`), parents its HUD (+0x100) and its model (the chosen GUID's, else +0xdc) under it, and when the model is a creature model calls `Setup(robot)`: (1) each property becomes one of the robot's properties (`DE_CActor` +0x20, the list `FindPropertyByName` walks, first match); (2) each instanced object is copied (`ObjNewCopy(1)`), parented under the robot and made a system tree; (3) when one of the robot's children (`ObjChild`, vtable +0xe4 / +0xe8; not its properties) is an `ImageFilter\OMF2Fighter`, every `Image` among the model's descendants (`ObjDescendant` order) becomes a property of the robot, after the filter hookup of FINDINGS 165. Shipped: every paint lists [`DestructionDebrisParent` of its own file, Effects' `GetHitBitsParent`] as instanced (Force's paints `EnergyEye` first) and its `LODSwitcher` as property; Chronos's and Force's `Electro` models list body parts and no description. That is where `BFighter.ResolveFighterMembers` finds `RGBMain` / `ReflectionMask` (`FindPropertyByName`) and `GetHitBitsParent` / `DestructionDebrisParent` (`FindChildByName`), in 2.090 data as in 2.123's. The port runs the same hookup (`World::setup_creature_model`) on the model instance `new_resource` parents under the fighter. The pieces under both parents are inactive in the data (actor flag 8 clear), so they stay out of the scene until the scripts copy them.
246. **Get-hit debris (2.123)** [scripts (2.123 Jaguar.DEObjects `PJaguar`, compiled `BFighter`): `ResolveFighterMembers`, `EffectsGetHit`; Effects `BStandardDebris`; Core (disc) `DE_CScene::PassTime` 0x70070040, `DE_CScene::Add` 0x7006ed50 / `Remove` 0x7006ef00, `DE_CAffector::ObjProcessContents` 0x7007ae40, `DE_CAffector::ObjPrepare` 0x7007bf60, `DE_CMeshAffector::IsTouchingSphere` 0x70082260, `DE_CSimplePhysics::PerformCollisionTests` 0x701dc1f0, `DE_CActor::Momentum` 0x700904e0, `DE_CPhysics::TimeScale` 0x701d8de0, `DE_CSimplePhysics::GetMomentum` 0x701d9a00]: `ResolveFighterMembers` replaces each image child of the robot's `GetHitBitsParent` named `RGBMain` / `ReflectionMask` with the robot's own image of that name (`ReplaceWithThenDie`), pre-caches its actor children and keeps them in a reference array; with a `DestructionExplosion` it renames `DestructionDebrisParent` to `DebrisParent`, puts it under the explosion, replaces its images the same way and moves its meshes to the robot. `EffectsGetHit`, with high-detail geometry, adds the attack's damage × `RenderQuality()` to a member; while that is over 11 it takes 11 off and spawns a random piece: `NewCopy`, placed at the attack's connect point, `Activate`, `Scene().Add`, its physics' momentum the attack direction plus (CenterRandom 3, Random 1, CenterRandom 3), × 0.008, kept within 0.05 .. 1.2 long. Each piece's `BStandardDebris` spins it every render tick, deletes it after 20 s, and on each bounce (its `Physics\Simple`: gravity 0.65-0.8, bounce, groups 0x200000: the arenas' fighting surfaces and mine fields) adds the power + 0.3 and explodes (its `ExplosionActor` reference, smoke off, body unrendered) past 2 or when `Momentum()` is under 0.04. The pieces are zero-radius spheres whose affector chunk v3 sets its first BOOL (+0x138): `DE_CScene::Add` lists such affectors (+0x190), `PassTime` stores each one's world position (+0x13c) and frame (+0x148..) at the start of every frame, and against a mesh such a sphere is tested as the line it moved along since (a scene cone at +0x13c, `IsTouchingConeAsLineSegment`), nothing when it did not move. Also flagged: Effects' smoking and fiery bits, Canyon's shells, Pickups' boulder, Force's surface and bomb testers, Warlord's bounce affectors and every model's destruction pieces. `CActor::Momentum` sums each physics' `GetMomentum` (zero while disabled) × `TimeScale` (1 / its rate, else +0x44, 1); the port had it only for fighters, so a piece read 0 and exploded at its first bounce.
247. **Images are per instance** [Core (2.123) `DE_CObject::ReplaceWithThenDie` 0x20043800 (FINDINGS 220); disc `DE_CTransferAgent::CPassiveReference::New` 0x7005c070]: in the original every copy has its own objects, and a copy's references to objects inside the copied branch point at the copy's, so replacing a `GetHitBitsParent` copy's `RGBMain` re-points only that copy's texture managers, at that robot's painted image. A texture manager's images are its references chunk v1-v2 (a count, then references), not BOOL-terminated lists. The port keeps the data identity on every instance, so it now resolves a reference from an instance to the nearest instance of the data object (`World::instance_near`: the instance's branch, each ancestor's, then from a copy's root the branch it was copied from), keeps a script-set image on the texture manager or mesh instance (`AddProperty`, `RemoveAllProperty`, `ReplaceWithThenDie`) and a paint filter on the image instance (`Setup`, `AddFilter`); the actor renderer asks per drawn instance and keys its batches by the images they draw and the painting fighter's colours. Only data objects some instance changed are looked up.
248. **The VCR's image buttons are push buttons whose label is the image** [OMF (2.123) `DE_CVCRRobot` ctor 0x107665e0, `DE_CVCRControls::ObjUpdate` 0x10765e70; Core (2.123) `DE_CButtonControl3d::New` 0x200c3770, `NewToggle` 0x200c3890, `SetVisualFace(image, rgba, int)` 0x200c35b0, `SetVisualFace(state, image, rgba)` 0x200c2db0 and its face robot (a `DE_CButtonFoundationRobot`, vtable 0x2026881c; entry setter 0x200c45c0, state update 0x200c4260), `DE_CButtonRobot` ctor 0x200c9e70]: Pause/Play is `NewToggle("")` (type 1 from `ObjInitContents`, +0x208 toggle) with faces `SetVisualFace(0, image, g_White)` and `(1, image, g_White)`, bound to the paused int (+0x130); Advance Frame, Fast Backward and Fast Forward are `New("")` with `SetVisualFace(image, g_White, 1)`, bound to their ints. So all four are push buttons: the 2-pixel up bevel with the launcher's face, sunk while pressed or (Play/Pause) while down. `SetVisualFace(image, rgba)` deletes the label (+0x214) and puts in its place a plain control (`DeFaceImage`) textured with the image, filled with the colour, its frame bit cleared, alignment 6 for a push button, bumped up 5: it has no edges, so it fills the parent's client area, which for a push button is the `DE_CButtonRobot`'s holder actor (+0x58, made the control actor's client actor +0x140): (b, b)-(1 - 1.5b, 1 - 1.5b) up, (1.5b, 1.5b)-(1 - b, 1 - b) down. The per-state version keeps one such control per state (a text control when the state's face is a string) and shows the one for the button's +0x1fc. No hover look; `ObjUpdate` never touches the buttons' look. Ours drew only the images, over the whole button; they are now push buttons with the image as the label (`Button::paint_self`).
249. **A modifier button's face: `NewIcon` through `DE_CNewControlEvent` type 4** [Core (2.123) `DE_CModifierControlRobot::FillButtonControl` 0x201f0a50, `SelectModifier` 0x201f09f0 (vtable +0xcc), `DE_CChooseCastRobot::SetCurrentModifier` 0x201f0670, `DE_CDescription::ProcessEvent` 0x201f9440, `DE_CDescription::NewIcon` 0x201f97a0 (vtable +0x12c), `DE_CEvent::SendTo` 0x20063ca0, `DE_CSingleButtonGroupRobot::ProcessButtonClickEvent` 0x201ed250, `DE_CButtonControl3d::ProcessEvent` 0x200c2460 / `SetDown` 0x200c2500 / `SetUp` 0x200c24d0 / `Click` 0x200c27c0, `NewMini` 0x200c3da0, `DE_CButtonRobot::ProcessMouseEvent` 0x200ca040, `DE_CControl3d::SetFillColor` 0x200e5dd0 / 0x200e5d20, `FindChildByGUID` 0x200eadc0; `BUTTON_SELECTED_COLOR` / `UNSELECTED` 0x2036aaf0 / 0x2036aae0]: for each listed modifier `FillButtonControl` makes a factory `DE_CButtonControl3d` (type 1) with +0x20c = 1 (mini: `SizeMiniButtonBorderControl`, 1 pixel) and +0x208 = 1 (toggle), `SetLenY` / `SetLenX` 30 (the layout of FINDINGS 244 then replaces them), and `SetDown` on the list's chosen one (the robot's list +0x80, its +0x18 at robot +0x98). Its face is the reply to a `DE_CNewControlEvent` of type 4 sent to the description: `DE_CDescription::ProcessEvent` answers type 4, when the description has an icon (+0x44) and the event no control yet, with `NewIcon()` in event +0x44 (and passes the event on to event +0x10); type 5 builds a text control. `NewIcon` is a plain control (+0x1e8 |= 0x2000000) holding a visual child filled `g_White` (RGB), frame bit cleared, textured with the icon, its tooltip the description's `Description` value; both have no edges, so they fill the button's holder (FINDINGS 248's rectangle with b = 1). Without an icon `DE_CControl3d::New(button)` stands in. The face control gets the GUID 0x2036aad0 and the fill `UNSELECTED` (0, 0, 0, 0). The button group (`DE_CSingleButtonGroupRobot`) on a button's click: `FindChildByGUID` → fill `BUTTON_SELECTED_COLOR` (0, 1, 1, 1), button +0x210 = 1 (which `DE_CButtonRobot::ProcessMouseEvent` checks first: the mouse is ignored), the Current Settings icon, the settings' choice, the modifier robot's `SelectModifier(i)`, the registry's Last Modifiers; then the previous button +0x210 = 0, `SetUp`, fill clear. `Click` sends event 0x33, on which the toggle flips +0x1fc (up / multi → down, down → up) before the group hears it. Opening a cast: `FillButtonControl` clicks the button the registry's Last Modifiers names (else button 0), then `SetCurrentModifier` runs `SelectModifier(chosen)` and clicks the chosen one. When the two agree the chosen button ends down (down, up, down), cyan and deaf; on a fresh registry where the chosen default is not the first (Block Penetration Light), button 0 is clicked down and then released, and the chosen one is clicked from down to up: cyan, deaf, raised. Ours drew a 1-pixel bevel with the cyan square under the icon at the bevel's edge; it now follows the buttons' states, clicks and holder.
250. **Host's arena toggles: `DE_CLockedContentTokenList` and lock 0xffff** [Core (2.123) `DE_CChooseCastRobot` ctor 0x201ee5a0 (the Available Arenas list), `DE_CLockedContentTokenList::InternalValidate` 0x201f0000 / destructor 0x201f0340, the token control generator 0x201ede00, `DE_CChooseCastRobot::ProcessPreDraw` 0x201f1040, `DE_CDescription::GatherVisibleMasters` 0x201fa380, `IsLocked` 0x201fa030 (vtable +0x144), `Lock` (+0x138) / `Unlock` (+0x13c), `IsUserLockedCaller` 0x201f8b80; script `BEnvironmentList` (Session)]: with content control the arenas' list (+0xd4, `NoIcons`, sorted by name) owns a `DE_CLockedContentTokenList` (+0x80). Its validation finds `SharedObjects\EnvironmentMultiplayer`, gathers every description factory's visible masters (all masters less those `IsLocked(0)` and not `IsVisibleWhenLocked`), keeps the cast's members, and for each appends `IsLocked(0xffff)` to a global int array (0x2036aac0) and makes a token named after it with INT `LockedContentListIndex` = its entry and the control generator 0x201ede00; then it copies the array (0x2036aab0). The generator answers a type 5 `DE_CNewControlEvent` with a plain control holding `NewCheck(name, &array[LockedContentListIndex])`, or, for an `IsLocked(0)` arena, a text control VPU 16 high `OffsetFromTop(VPU 2)`, `OffsetFromLeft(VPU 23)`. Every pre-draw (and the list's destructor) compares the array with the copy: a changed entry runs `Lock(0xffff, 0)` when checked and `Unlock(0xffff)` when not (the description's registry `DLock` list of unlocked ids, FINDINGS 144, 158), then the copy is retaken. Since an id other than 0 is locked until unlocked, every arena starts checked; unchecking one unlocks 0xffff. `IsUserLocked()` is `!IsLocked(0xffff)`: `BEnvironmentList.EnvironmentListBuild`, in a joinable session, drops the user-locked environments while more than one remains, and only `BMatchServer.StartNewMatch` (`EnvironmentListPopRandom`) uses it in multiplayer. Ours: the list's rows are check rows bound to the array, applied in the page's update and before its buttons act; `IsUserLocked` is registered. The rows are the list's own (FINDINGS 271).
253. **Which scenes pass time; the temporal master** [Core (disc) `DE_CScene::TemporalUpdate` 0x70070710 (vtable +0x11c), `TemporalGlobalTime` 0x700729b0 (+0x118), `PreRender` 0x7006f710, `Time` / `PreciseTime` 0x7006f970 / 0x7006f990, `Genesis` 0x70070900, `SetInitialSceneTime` 0x7006fa10, `AssureTemporalMaster` 0x70072a00, `TemporalPassTime` / `TemporalMoveToScale` / `TemporalPause` / `TemporalScale` 0x700728e0 / 0x70072960 / 0x700728c0 / 0x70072990, `AutoUpdate` / `UpdateAllAutoUpdate` 0x70073500 / 0x70073540, `DE_CTemporalMasterScene::ObjInitContents` 0x700787a0 / `TemporalGlobalTime` 0x70078910 / `BeginSceneUpdate` 0x70078b30 (+0x16c) / `TemporalPause` 0x70078850, `DE_CSession::Update` 0x701f8ab0, `DE_CAppManager::InternalUpdateSessions` 0x7019a540 / `ObjUpdate` 0x7019a450, `DE_GLOBAL::UpdateSystem` 0x700117f0; Core (2.123) `DE_CScene::PassTime` 0x20073f30, `TemporalUpdate` 0x20074600 (its fields +0x14 on); Session `DE_CStandardSession::Begin` 0x4500ecf0; scripts `BSinglePlayerSubSession` (Entry), `BTimeAccelerate`, `BSessionTimer`]: a scene passes time only in its `TemporalUpdate`, and nothing before `Genesis` (+0x164). A scene that is not itself a master first asks its temporal master (+0x140) `BeginSceneUpdate(arg)` (with arg 1, under 10 ms since the master's last pass skips the update) and runs the master's `TemporalUpdate(0)`. Its global time is `TemporalGlobalTime`: with no master (+0x140 the scene itself, from `ObjInitContents`) gTime; else the master's own time since it last asked (real time: a master's `TemporalGlobalTime` of itself is gTime at its `BeginSceneUpdate`) times the master's scale +0x1c8 (first moved towards +0x1e8 by that time × +0x1f0, clamped), less a pause (+0x1d8; −1 holds it), plus +0x1e0 (`TemporalPassTime`'s accumulator, which also keeps the fraction), added into +0x1f8. The scene then passes global − last (+0x15c), or 0 while `IsUIManagerAutopaused` (or the app is inactive) when +0x1c0 and +0x1bc are clear and its session has no remote clients, or while paused (+0xf0) and not a master; 2.123's `PassTime` multiplies that by +0x188 (`SetTimeScale`; `TimeScale` reads it, `BSessionTimer` divides by it) keeping the fraction in +0x190 as a float, rewinds on a negative result (to 0 at most), jumps past 2000 ms in a replaying session (+0xbc → +0xa0), and calls the delta-tick hooks with the given time × +0x188. The post-master (+0x144) passes the master's last pass (+0xf4); nothing reads it. Callers each frame: `UpdateSystem` → the app manager's `InternalUpdateSessions` (each local session in the order added: `DE_CSession::Update` first checks its record and fetches replayed states, then on the server runs its scene's `TemporalUpdate(1)`, the modifiers' and clients' updates and `TemporalUpdate(0)`), its `ObjUpdate` → `UpdateAllAutoUpdate` (`TemporalUpdate(1)` of the auto-update scenes: the top control's GUI scene), and each renderer's `PreRender` → `TemporalUpdate(0)` of the scene its viewer shows. As a pass passes only the time since the last, a scene passes the frame's real time once whoever comes first, the master session's before its sub-session's. `AssureTemporalMaster` makes the master and post-master (`DE_CTemporalMasterScene`s, +0xec set, scale and target 1, rate 0); its retail callers are `DE_CStandardSession::Begin` for a replaying session (+0xa0) and `BSinglePlayerSubSession`'s Entry ("So we can pass time quickly": `BTimeAccelerate` passes up to 200 ms a frame while the slot's creature has buttons down). Practice's sub-session has none, so `TemporalPassTime` and `TemporalMoveToScale` on its scene do nothing. `Genesis` starts a master made before it (vtable +0x138); one made after its scene's `Genesis` never starts, and only passed time reaches the scene. `Genesis` ends in `SetInitialSceneTime(0)`: the time is 0, every set restarts at 0 and the `SetTime` list gets 0, so a session's scene counts from the moment its environment came in. Ours: one `SceneClock` per session (`World::clocks_`, keyed by the session object; null for what is in no session, which never autopauses), temporal sets tagged with their scene, `scene_ms()` the clock of the scene being passed or updated; `tick` passes the scene of no session, then `update_session` passes each session's scene before its update (a replayed session's state fetch before its pass and its fixups after, FINDINGS 270); `Time`, `PreciseTime`, collision testers, simple physics, particles, incident recorders and state stamps use their object's scene. The GUI scenes keep their own clocks (FINDINGS 162).
254. **Replays and the session speed** [Core (disc) `DE_CSessionRecord::Close` 0x70203810, `Replay` 0x70203e30, `SetTimeScaleCaller` / `TimeScaleCaller` 0x7006dc90 / 0x7006dcc0 (+0x178; 2.123 0x20071af0, +0x188); Session `DE_CStandardSession::Begin` 0x4500ecf0; OMF `DE_CVCRControls::ObjUpdate` 0x35064ff0; scripts `BSessionSpeedModifier` (2.123 Session.DEObjects; its Entry is `Scene().SetTimeScale(GetFLOATValue("SessionSpeed"))`), BMaster's `StartNewMatch`]: the scale is not recorded. The record keeps the master description's GUID and the session's modifier GUID pool, which `Replay` hands back; a match sub-session's pool holds the Session Speed choice (BMaster copies the master's), so the replayed session makes a `SessionSpeedModifier` whose Entry sets the replayed scene's +0x188 again (a restored executor state does not re-run Entry), and a 1.4 recording plays at 1.4. `Close` stores the length +0x78 as the scene's time / (+0x178 × 1000): real seconds. The VCR's choices go to the replayed scene's temporal master, which `DE_CStandardSession::Begin` makes for every replayed session: 1/2..1/8 and 2X..16X are `TemporalMoveToScale(speed, 9999)` (instant), pause scale 0, the frame step `TemporalPassTime(50)`, rewind scale 1 with `TemporalPassTime((last dt + dt) × −speed − 2000)` and `+2000`, the slider's seek `TemporalPassTime(end × slider − Time())`. All are master (real) milliseconds, which the scene's `PassTime` multiplies by its scale: 2X at Normal speed runs 2.8 times real time, a step is 70 scene ms, a rewind 1.4 times the amount asked, and the seek, worked out in scene time, overshoots by the scale. Ours: `World::time_scale` and `passed_ms` (the VCR's) go to the replayed scene's master each frame; the record's length divides by the scale.
255. **The picker's stripped model; get-hit debris needs a camera** [OMF (disc) `DE_CFighterModelDescription::NewStrippedFighterModel` 0x35038940, `DE_CCreatureModel::Setup` 0x35037470, `DE_CChooseFighterModelPickerUIManagerRobot::UpdateModel` 0x35055a10; Core (2.123) `DE_CActor::RenderQuality` 0x20095260; scripts `BStandardOMFSession` (`Session().SetCamera(Slot(t).OwnedCreature().Camera(0))`)]: `NewStrippedFighterModel` makes a new `DE_CCreatureModel` and a new instance of the description's model (+0x5c) parented to it (vtable +0xe0), replaces the instance's texture managers' images by name from +0xe4, and appends the instance to the new model's properties (+0x34); its instanced objects (+0x48) stay empty. Its `Setup(robot)` therefore makes the `FighterModel` instance one of the robot's properties and, the template having a `FighterImageFilter` child, hooks every image under the creature model to the filter and makes them robot properties; the `FighterModel`'s own lists (`DestructionDebrisParent`, `GetHitBitsParent`, `LODSwitcher`) are never set up, and the template has no executors to resolve fighter members. Get-hit debris (FINDINGS 246) spawns only while `RenderQuality()` is above 0, which needs a renderer camera: a session sets it to the user slot's creature's `Camera(0)`. `openbg-fight --direct` (no session) now builds its robots through `new_resource` (the description's HUD, default model and `Setup`) and sets the renderer camera as the session would.
262. **A copy's references, exactly** [Core (disc) `DE_CTransferAgent::CPassiveReference::New` 0x7005c070, `DE_CObject::ObjNewCopy`; Core (2.123) `DE_CObject::ReplaceWithThenDie` 0x20043800]: a reference in a copy points where the copied object's reference pointed when it was copied, or, when the same `NewCopy` copied that object too, at its copy; a later `ReplaceWithThenDie` re-points every reference to the dying object at its replacement, those of copies made earlier included, while copies made later take the replacement from their source. A loaded object's references point at the loaded objects. Ours: `copy_tree` gives the copies one `NewCopy` makes a shared map (source to copy) and a sequence number, `ReplaceWithThenDie` marks the object replaced (with its number), and `World::referent(from, r)` walks `from`'s copy sources back to a loaded object, starting from r's loaded instance and applying, per copy, the replacements made before it and its map, then every later replacement; null once the target is destroyed. It replaces FINDINGS 247's nearest-instance search (`instance_near`) and the subtree-then-ancestors `nearest_copy` (affector owners and encompassers, physics affectors and forces, particle bolt targets, auto-spawners and kill victims, trails, navigation links, observer cameras, a creature model's properties). The actor renderer resolves a stage's image through the drawn instance's mesh, its texture manager (a script-set image first) and the image reference; get-hit debris still draws each robot's painted image (the `GetHitBitsParent` copy's `RGBMain` was replaced by the robot's), and seeded fights and the eight robots' screenshots are unchanged.
263. **Which meshes light projectors reach** [Direct3D (disc) `DE_CD3DRenderMeshElement::InitialSetup` 0x2002e550, `AssureProjectedTargetOctree` 0x2002e1f0, `FillProjectedVertexBuffer` 0x2002e4f0, `DE_CD3DLight::UpdateTouchedPolygons` 0x2002cd00, the light's update (calls `UpdateLitActors` 0x2000f720 every frame, `UpdateTouchedPolygons` only once the light has moved or changed reach by about a tenth of its falloff, or after lighting nothing), `DE_CStandardProjectorShader::DrawPatches` 0x20033b20, `DE_CRendererDirect3D::SetWorldSpace` 0x200260e0; Core `DE_CRenderMeshElement::IsStaticPosition` 0x7002ce60 (vtable +0x40)]: a mesh element becomes a projector target (renderer +0x5c8) when it is projector-lit (+0x160 bit 2) and its vertices are static (no position process: skinned and morphed meshes never are), on any render actor, with the lightable affector rule of FINDINGS 211. Its octree is in mesh space; its vertex copy (+0x168) is taken into the world by the actor's frame once, when the element is set up, only when the actor is position-static, and otherwise stays in mesh space. A light finds the faces with its affector in the actor's current frame but copies those vertices as they are, and `DrawPatches` draws them with the world transform: a position-static actor's polygons stay where it stood at setup, a moving one's are drawn at its mesh coordinates. In the shipped data only scene geometry is projector-lit (53 meshes: arenas, cinematics, Trophy Room, the picker's `LoadScene` walls and pedestal frame, the GUI doors); no fighter, pickup, projectile, debris or effect mesh is, so no target marker, scorch or spark light ever lands on them. Ours: arena targets of actors that are not position-static keep their vertices in mesh space; a path-moved arena actor keeps its arena target when position-static (else the actor renderer gives it one); the actor renderer makes projector targets of its projector-lit meshes (mesh space, or placed once per position-static instance) and draws the projector passes on them; the LightMap and ShadowAttenuation load without an arena (the picker). The picker's rotating spots now reach its walls (they fall outside the cone's penumbra, so nothing shows); no fight or menu screenshot changed. Not ported: the light's lazy re-query.
264. **PreciseTime** [Core (disc) `DE_CScene::PreciseTime` 0x7006f990 (listing), `Time` 0x7006f970, `SetInitialSceneTime` 0x7006fa10, `Genesis` 0x70070900, `TemporalUpdate` 0x70070710, `DE_CTemporalMasterScene::TemporalGlobalTime` 0x70078910; Core (2.123) `PreciseTime` 0x20073880 (time +0x174, last global +0x170, scale +0x188)]: while a re-tick holds the time (+0x1ac) it is that time (+0x1b0); otherwise `Time() + ftol(double(uint32(TemporalGlobalTime(this) - last)) x scale)`, `last` (+0x15c) being the global time of the scene's last `TemporalUpdate` (set after `PassTime`, also when autopaused; 0 from the constructor, gTime from `Genesis`). Without a temporal master that is the wall-clock time since the scene last passed: the sub-frame time a render tick sees. With one it is what the master was given since (`TemporalPassTime`), which the call hands over as already given (+0x1f8), so the next pass does not pass it again. The scripts read it in two places, both render ticks: `BActorRotator` (the picker's pedestal, Desert's beacon) and `BCommonButton`'s rotators. Ours: `World::precise_ms_of`, each scene clock keeping the global time it last passed at (GUI scenes too); in screenshot and headless runs gTime is the frame's 20 ms steps, the GUI passes counting as the frame they start and the render ticks a frame begins with as the last frame's render, so there it equals `Time`.
265. **Sparks and trails on their scene's clock** [OMF (disc) `DE_CSparkProcess::Tick` 0x3502f890; Core `DE_CPointStreamer::DE_CInstanceInfo::Process` 0x701d17f0, `DE_CManualParticleSpawner::Spawn`]: the spark process is a temporal of the scene the spark copy was added to, a point streamer steps with its actor's scene and a manual spawner stamps its particles with its own scene's time. Ours stepped them, and stamped manual particles, with the clock of the scene being updated (the current session's); they now read `scene_ms_of` of the spark actor, the trail's actor and the spawner.
268. **Choose Cast's saved modifiers are the GUID pool's contents** [Core (2.123) `DE_CSessionSettings::UIManagerComplete` 0x201ed930, `SaveModifiers` 0x201ed970, `LoadModifiers` 0x201edb40, `DE_CGUIDPool::GenerateContents` 0x20022c20 / `ProcessContents` 0x20022c60, `DE_CRegistry::Save(const char*, const DE_CContents&)` 0x20219240 / `Load` 0x20219270, the pool's add-if-absent 0x20060f90; strings 0x2035cfd0..0x2035cfec]: `SaveModifiers` (only with a session, info +0x24) generates the session info's GUID pool (+0x38 → +0x8c) into a contents and saves it in `DE_CRegistry("Session")` under `"Modifiers\\" + DE_CGUID::Get(master description GUID)` (session +0x60, vtable +0x78), so `Session\Modifiers\<guid>` is a `g_ValueDataStreamFactory` value holding `01`, the u16 count and the GUIDs' 16 bytes each in memory order. `LoadModifiers`, when that value exists, processes it into a scratch pool (nothing unless the format byte is 1), empties the info's pool and adds each GUID if absent; GUIDs are not checked against descriptions (unknown ones make no modifier). The port's text registry keeps the value as the bytes in lower-case hex and still reads its earlier GUID-text values.
269. **What the Choose Match page made dies when it begins again** [OMF (2.123) `DE_COMF2Manager::ProcessEvent` 0x107157a0 and its list add 0x1071c150 / `AddIfAbsentLocalSession` 0x1071c190; Core (2.123) `DE_CUIManager::Begin` 0x201f2e30, `Deactivate` 0x201f30d0, `DE_CUIManager::DE_CMultipleReferenceCompleteCreatedObjects` (+0x38: array +0x40, count +0x44), `DE_CSession::SatisfyMasterRequirements` 0x20201790]: on the `ChooseSesssionType` chooser's Complete the manager makes the standard session info and its session and adds both to the chooser's +0x38 list (a multiple reference: RefNotifyAdd, so a destroyed one leaves it), then `SatisfyMasterRequirements`; only the completion tail (after Choose Cast's Complete when a page was needed) adds the session to the app manager's local sessions (`AddIfAbsentLocalSession`) and begins it. Choose Cast's Back (`SetLastUIManager`) therefore leaves an unbegun session that no session update reaches; popping back to the chooser runs its `Begin`, which `DeleteThisLater`s everything in +0x40 (as `Deactivate` does when the page was backed out, +0x60), so housekeeping destroys the session (`ObjDestroyContents` → `End`) and its info. A completed session goes the same way only if that chooser instance begins again, which `MenuSelect` never makes happen (it makes a new chooser each time). The port keeps the created objects on the page (`UIManager::created`); `has_local_session` counts only a begun session.
270. **A replayed creature's tick fetches first; fixups come after the scene's pass** [Creature (2.123) `DE_CCreature::Tick` 0x10216990, `UnpackState` 0x10215250, `AssureSequenced` 0x10215d00; Core (2.123) `DE_CStateManager::GetState` 0x201b8a50, `DE_CStandardStateManager::UpdateForReplay` 0x201b5eb0, `AddLaggedState` 0x201b5770; `re/specs/replay-playback.md` (`DE_CSession::Update`)]: `GetState(t)` first calls `UpdateForReplay(t)` (vtable +0x12c) when the manager is replay-driven (+0x78), then returns the state of exactly time t in the list (+0x3c), if any. `UpdateForReplay` finds the record's newest state at or before t; unless that time is the last one taken (+0x80), it unpacks it into the manager's pending state (+0xd8) and calls `AddLaggedState(state, -1, 0)`, which puts it in the state and new-state lists and sets +0x48 (NeedFixup) but restores nothing; an identical state at the same time is dropped without the flag. So in a creature's tick (2.123 keeps its mode at +0x300, its unpack marker +0x214) the fetch of its own tick queues the record's state, and a state of exactly that tick is restored at once (`SetupOwnerForState`, physics and brain skipped). The session update fetches every manager's record (`UpdateForReplay(-1)`: the owner's local time) before its scene's `TemporalUpdate(1)` and runs the fixups after it, so a state that arrived in either fetch is restored only once the scene has passed (again, when the tick already took it). `UnpackState` clears +0x214 when it returns. The port did both fetches with an immediate restore after the pass; it now queues and flags (`StateManager::need_fixup`) and restores in `replay_after_pass`. A fresh practice recording replays with its fighters within 0.65 units of the recording and on the same ticks. The record has no field or version that tells which build made it.
271. **The Host check rows and the notification overlay's frame** [Core (2.123) the locked-content token's control generator 0x201ede00, `DE_CButtonControl3d::NewCheck` 0x200c39b0 / `SetText` 0x200c2580, `DE_CGUIStandardInterface::CreateCheckButtonControl` 0x200fac50 (control) / 0x200fb160 (actor), `DE_CControl3d::GetMinPixelSize` 0x200e71b0 (its sum 0x200e71e0) / `CalcMinPixelSize` 0x200e72b0, `DE_CTextControl3d::CalcMinPixelSize` 0x200bbc90, `DE_CListEntryManager::CEntry::PositionEntry` 0x200aad00, `DE_CControl3d::ObjInitContents` 0x200e4f90, `SetFrameWidthOnly` 0x200e89a0; `DE_CDialog3d::UserInformation` 0x200f8310 (the overlay's construction inlined at 0x200f8c23..0x200f8f25), `DE_CButtonOverlay::DE_CButtonOverlay` 0x20182ab0, `ConvertToVPU` 0x200da070 and its pair helper 0x200ed2e0]: the arena row's cell is a plain control holding `NewCheck(name, &array[index], 0, 0, 0)`; a size of 0 means 16, so the label is a text control VPU (0, 16) high (+0x1f8), with no autosize (+0x218 unset). A control's minimum size sums its child controls' minimums, then applies its own (+0x19c / +0x1a0, 0 by default) and adds its frame width (+0x104 / +0x108, −1 until `SetFrameWidth*`); the check's box and mark are control actors (`CheckMarkHolder`, `CheckMarkImage`: `SizeButtonCheckMark`, alignment 2, frame 1, `ColorCheckBoxButtonBack`), which do not count. So the row's minimum height is the label's VPU 16, and `PositionEntry` makes a row the larger of its cells' minimum heights and the list's minimum entry height (20; 30 with neither): the arena rows are ordinary list rows, not the box's VPU 18 + 2. The notification overlay's frame child gets `SetFrameWidthOnly(VPU x 8, VPU y 8)`: the constructor converts (0, 8) and (8, 0) and passes the x of the second and the y of the first, so its left and right edges are VPU 8 across and its top and bottom VPU 8 down, as ported.

256. **The end of a tournament: the cinematic session** [scripts (2.123 SinglePlayer) `BSinglePlayerSession` (`SetupCinematicSession`), `BCinematicMaster`, `BSubSession` (`Cinematic`), `BUnlockDescriptionEvent`, `BCinematicQuote`; Core (2.123) `IsLoadingCompleteCaller` 0x20071b80 (scene +0x1c8; set at 0x20073038 by `ActivateSceneWatchers`, disc 0x7006f020, +0x1b8), `LastKeyPressedSystemTimeCaller` 0x2004a240 (`DE_GLOBAL::LastKeyPressTime`, written only by Input2's `DE_CDirectInputControllerComponent::Update` with `gTime()` when a button component goes down), `UnlockCaller` 0x201f8aa0 (vtable +0x13c with id 0; 0x201f8ac0 passes the id, 0x201f8a80 is `Lock()`), `UnlockAndNotifyCaller` 0x201f8b00 → `DE_CDescription::UnlockAndNotify` 0x201f9c20]: after the last match `SetupCinematicSession` unlocks the tournament (`lockIDCompleted`, 0x10000) and the Trophy Room (id 0), starts the Cinematic master in the tournament's ending environment and sets `EnableQuickExit`. `BCinematicMaster` waits in AttemptingBeginAction until `IsLoadingComplete`, so without that native the cinematic never began. Then the unlock events show one "Feature Unlocked" notification each (`UnlockAndNotify`: while id 0 is locked, unlock it, `NewUserInformation("Feature Unlocked", text or "<name> will now be available.")`, save the registry), the cinematic quote plays for 45 s, "Press any key to continue" appears, and the first key pressed after that (`LastKeyPressedSystemTime` newer than the time it noted) deletes the master session: the main menu returns. North American Open plays Welcome, Madness ("Wild Science"), Intensity, Sabotage and Raven; Vendetta is only reached after a lost Sabotage (`SetNextMatch(matchRaven)`). Its cinematic is in CinematicCitySimple and unlocks the North American Open trophy, Redemption, HAR Master (Jaguar) and Ultimate Warrior; the opponents' pilots are unlocked as it starts. IFL Cup ends in PhoenixBuilding and unlocks World Open among others. Ours: `IsLoadingComplete`, `LastKeyPressedSystemTime` (any key the app sees), `Unlock()`, `UnlockAndNotify` (the app's notification).

257. **Quotes and match events are session modifiers added at run time** [Core (disc) `DE_CSession::AddModifier` 0x701f8f90 (vtable +0x148; 2.123 caller 0x201ff7b0), `DE_CSessionModifier::SymbiotSessionModifierInvalid` 0x70200640, `Initialize` 0x70200f10 (vtable +0x12c, +0x58), `Begin` 0x70200ee0; scripts `BSinglePlayerSubSession` (`AddPilotQuote`, `AddCinematicQuote`, `AddUserNotification`, `AddDropActorOnSlotCreature`, `AddUnlock`), `BPilotQuote`]: `AddModifier` appends the modifier once (the session's +0x88 list) and, when the session has begun (+0xa4) and the modifier is not initialised, initialises it (its executor properties start); it is not begun. The scripts copy a template, set its values and `AddProperty` (the quote executor) before adding it, so the copy's property list exists before it belongs to a session. The port lacked `CSession::AddModifier`, so no pilot quote, cinematic quote, notification, dropped actor (Sabotage's explosions, Madness's acid) or unlock event ever ran. Quote texts and the pilot frame use `CPilot::Sex()` (OMF `SexCaller` 0x35048260: +0x3d4, the last u32 of the pilot's contents after the AI stats), `IsUIManagerActive` (Core 2.123 0x2004a4f0: `DE_CUIManager::ActiveManager` is not null, any UI manager with +0xa0 set; ours: the app's current page is up) and BText's underlay (`CTextPrimitive::GetSize`, `SetBlendTranslucentAlpha`, FINDINGS 259). A quote without a pilot is a user notification: `NotifyUser(title, text)` (Core 2.123 `NotifyUserCaller` 0x200495e0) is `DE_CNotification::NewUserInformation(title, text)`, Madness's "Notification" about the acid.

258. **RECON DATA does unlock in play (corrects 144)** [scripts `BCreatureAndPilotPicker` (`AttemptToCreatePilot`), `BSinglePlayerSession` (`SinglePlayerMatchComplete`)]: the picker constructs the pilot's description, takes its pilot from `NewResource`, then parents the description to the pilot and renames it "PilotDescription" ("because some other components may search for it by name"). So `Pilot().FindChildByName("PilotDescription")` finds it and every finished match unlocks three RECON DATA stats for each opponent (`ObjectInfo\<pilot description>\DLock` gets three ids 100 + k). FINDINGS 144's "never unlocks in play" was wrong.

259. **Text size, line breaks and the underlay** [Core (disc) `TextGetSizeCaller` 0x7012bac0 (2.123 0x20130ca0) → `DE_CFlatText3::GetActualSize` 0x701ce1d0 / `GetSize` 0x701ce060, `SetText` 0x701ccc50 (`SplitNewLine`), `CFontInfo::Update` 0x701caf70, `ObjInitContents` 0x701cc650; `PrimitiveSetBlendTranslucentAlphaCaller` 0x7012b680 (2.123 0x20130860)]: a flat text keeps its lines (split at line breaks). `GetSize` is the widest line's advance by (lines − 1) × the line spacing (+0xa0, 1.2 font units) + the font's height (+0x24); `GetActualSize` scales both by the text size (+0xd4). `CFontInfo::Update` justifies each line on its own (0.5 of its width for centred text, 1 for right) and moves down one line spacing per line. `SetBlendTranslucentAlpha` sets the primitive's blend +0x1c to 3. BText's underlay band (cinematic and pilot quotes) is sized from `GetSize`. Ours drew every text on one line; now each line is laid out as above.

260. **A creature's environment; the Trophy Room** [Creature (disc) `EnvironmentCaller` 0x15014250 → `DE_CCreature::Environment` 0x15018f80 (vtable +0x164); scripts (2.123 Trophy Room) `BMaster`, `BSubSession`, `BTrophyController`]: `Environment()` is the last actor of the creature's root scene that is an environment (null outside a scene). Ours answered a placeholder object, so the Trophy Room's controller never found `TrophyPosition` and showed nothing. The Trophy Room (its own SessionMaster, unlocked with id 0 by the first finished tournament, then listed in Choose Match) is a cinematic session with `EnableQuickExit` off: the controller gathers the trophy-cast creature descriptions that are unlocked, puts the current one's `NewResource` on `TrophyPosition` and shows its name, "ObtainedFrom" and "Description"; left/right switch trophies, up/down zoom, the "Switch Trophy Camera" mouse key flies its own camera. The trophies are actors of the Trophy Room file outside its environment: the arena renderer now bakes only the environment's branch (the file's other actors, in every arena, appear only as copies, drawn live where they are placed; PracticeArena's FireBrandNew and TrainingRing were the only others baked before, at their template places).

261. **Training's pads switch themselves** [scripts (PracticeArena) `BTrainingPad`, SharedObjects `BSwitch`]: Training's pads are copies of one `TrainingRing` template made by passive references; each pad's Activator holds a `Switch` object reference to the template's root, which in a copy names the copy (FINDINGS 262), so the Activator pulses its own pad and the pad's pages show. Object-reference values resolved the template ("No BSwitch interface found on TrainingRing") and no lesson ever showed; they now resolve through `World::referent` like the other references.

272. **Executor stacks** [ours]: each brain's executor has a 256 KB stack from the bump-allocated script arena, and nothing was ever freed: IFL Cup ran out of the 1 GB arena in its fifth match ("script address space exhausted"). A destroyed object's brain no longer runs (its hooks included; destroyed debris and fiery bits had kept running their render ticks), and its stack is reused at the next frame; the arena reserve is 3.75 GB (32-bit addresses). With this, North American Open and IFL Cup played through end at about 300 and 350 MB (IFL Cup had passed 1.1 GB). Seeded fights are unchanged by this.

273. **The camera at the end of a match won by the user, through a whole tournament** [scripts as FINDINGS 160, 193, 207]: with `OPENBG_KO=auto` (the kill hits reach the player) every round and match of North American Open and IFL Cup ends on the winner ("Elizabeth Wins", "You Win!"), between rounds the observer watches the fighters with interest, and the quotes point it at their speaker (`StandardSessionObserve`). An AI-vs-AI run of North American Open's first match (`OPENBG_AUTOPLAY=1`, real hits, first round won by the player after about 28800 frames) did not show the defeated fighters either. The parked report stays unreproduced.

274. **KillCallers in a native call; the instant executer** [Core (disc) `DE_CPCExecuter::KillCallers` 0x70152cd0, `ContinueExecutionInternal` 0x7014eaa0 (op 0x22, its frame-end tail), `InternalExecute` 0x7014e580, `IsInstant` 0x70152e60, `DE_CClassExecutorInstance::ExecuteInstant` 0x70145860 (2.123 0x2014ae50); scripts (2.123 SinglePlayer) `BSinglePlayerSubSession::SubSessionUpdate`, `BSubSession::SetupMatch` / `Cinematic`]: an instance has two executers, the state one and the instant one (+0x8c); a call from outside (a hook, an interface call) runs on the instant one, with frames and a stack of its own. `KillCallers` (op 315) keeps only the running frame of that executer, moved to the bottom of its stack. When the last frame runs off its end, `ContinueExecutionInternal` empties the frame list and returns 1 but leaves the pc (+0x2c) at that frame's end, so each enclosing `ContinueExecutionInternal` of the killed callers finds its loop over (pc ≥ end) and returns 1 in turn. `SubSessionUpdate` (an update hook) calls `SetupMatch`, which calls `Cinematic`; `Cinematic` kills its callers and sleeps, the sleep returns to `SetupMatch`'s loop, which runs `Cinematic`'s end, and the hook is over: the state executer's frames are untouched. Ours runs calls from outside on the state's executer above its frames, so `KillCallers` dropped the state's frames too, and the finished call set the pc to 0, which the enclosing loop then read ("vm: bad address 0" once per end cinematic). The frames below a native call now belong to the state (`Executor::call_floor_`): `KillCallers` keeps them, a call's last frame ends the call with the pc left at its end, and `call` resumes the state's frame. Seeded fights are unchanged.

275. **A constructed object's reference names its target's newest instance (amends 262)** [Core (disc) `DE_CTransferAgent::ExtractReference` 0x70057690 (2.123 0x2005b4c0), `DE_CTransferAgent::New` 0x70057220, `DE_CTransferObjectInfo::New` 0x70055140 (2.123 0x20058f40), `DE_CTransferAgent::CPassiveReference::New` 0x7005c070 (2.123 0x2005fec0), `Construct` 0x70059b70, `DE_CDescription::NewResource` 0x701f2170, `DE_CPassiveActorReference::Create` 0x700929a0; scripts (FirePit) `BLauncherActivator`, `BFireballLauncher`, SharedObjects `BDelayedTrigger`]: descriptions' resources (an arena) and passive references' objects (FirePit's target drones) are not `NewCopy`s but objects constructed from their file entries (`CPassiveReference::New` → the agent's `Construct`). Each object a file entry constructs joins that entry's instance list (+0x10, count +0x14), and when a constructed (non-master) object's references are processed, a reference within the file names the newest non-system object in its target's list, else the entry's master (+0x30); FINDINGS 279 says which objects are system and that copies join the list too. The drone template's `FighterDetector\Switch` names the arena's `FireballLauncher` (the activator, `PLauncherActivator`: `BSwitch` + `BLauncherActivator`); the drones are constructed when the arena prepares their references, after the arena, so their `Switch` names the arena's activator. Ours resolved it to the loaded `FireballLauncher`, which has no brain ("FighterDetector: No BSwitch interface found on FireballLauncher"), so no fireball was ever launched. `copy_tree(o, true)` now marks a construct (passive references, `NewResource`'s object), and `World::referent` takes, for a reference outside the construct's own objects, the newest live instance constructed before it. The activator now charges a launcher every 25 of its ticks and fires at a fighter the drones see. Seeded fights in the other arenas are unchanged.

276. **`CSpeaker::SetVolume` and `PlayProgress`** [Core (2.123) `SetVolumeCaller` 0x20221770, `PlayProgressCaller` 0x20221830 (name strings 0x2035f8e4, 0x2035f938), `DE_CSpeaker::Process` 0x20222500 (disc 0x70219a60), `DE_CValueFLOAT::CAdvancedReference::Value` 0x20113fb0; AudioPlayer (disc) `DE_CDirectXSpeakerElement::PlayProgress` 0x10004f70, Core `DE_CSpeakerElement::PlayProgress` 0x70213440; script (Session) `BCrowdResponse`]: `SetVolume(v)` sets the speaker's Volume value (the FLOAT behind its advanced reference +0x9c, vtable +0x13c), which `Process` multiplies by the active noise's volume (+0x54), at most 1, into its element's volume. `PlayProgress()` returns +0xd4, which `Process` copies each frame from the element while the speaker plays, with its playing state (+0xd0): a wave element's write cursor over the wave's data bytes (file size − 44), 0 for a segment or an element without a buffer. A one-shot buffer stops at its end. `BCrowdResponse` alternates two speakers per crowd noise, starting the second when the first is past half way, and fades them with `SetVolume`. Ours: the speakers play through speaker elements in the mixer, `PlayProgress` and `IsPlaying` coming from the playing voice (FINDINGS 311-316).

277. **`CSession::IsJoinable`** [Core (2.123) `IsJoinableCaller` 0x201ff360 (session +0x38, vtable +0x160), `DE_CSessionInfo::IsJoinable` 0x20205370, `SetJoinableCaller` 0x201ff310 (+0xcc; disc `IsJoinable` 0x701fd640 reads +0xbc); scripts `BSinglePlayerSession::Begin`, `BEnvironmentList` (Session)]: the session info answers its `SetJoinable` flag (+0xcc) when it has a network presence (+0x38 or its session's +0x60, and +0x4c and +0x50) and its session is not a client (+0xcc), else 0. Single player's master session calls `SetJoinable(false)` as it begins, so a local session is never joinable, and `BEnvironmentList` keeps locked arenas in single player's random choice. Ours answers false.

278. **A creature's clock: the creation time and the time a tick sees** [Creature (2.123) `DE_CCreature::SetTime` 0x10215e40 (disc 0x15015d20), `ObjPrepare` 0x10216170 (disc 0x15016050), `SceneTime` (disc 0x15019650 / 0x1500da40: ticks x rate + +0x1d8), `SetCreationTime` (disc 0x150195d0); Core (2.123) `DE_CScene::AddTemporal` 0x20075d70, `PassTime` 0x20073f30 (disc 0x70070040, `AdvanceTime` 0x70072090, `TemporalElementSetForRate` 0x70072230)]: `ObjPrepare` sets the creation time (+0x1d8) once, when it is 0: to what `AddTemporal` returns, the creature's set's last tick (+0x38), and 1 when that is 0 (or with +0x110 bit 0x10); a later prepare keeps it. `PassTime` advances a set when the clock reaches its last tick + rate, but holds the scene time at that last tick (+0x38) while the set's elements tick, so tick k of a creature sees `Time()` = creation + k x rate (`SceneTime(k)`), one rate behind the clock that advanced it. `SetTime(t)`: before the creation time the ticks become 0; else tick = (t − creation) / rate; when t is at or before `SceneTime(ticks)` it rewinds to tick if that is earlier, else re-ticks up to tick (at most 100, each with the scene time held at `SceneTime(ticks)`). `UnpackState` calls it with `Time()`, so a creature that takes the state it registered for this very tick (its own state coming back) finds t = `SceneTime(ticks)` and does nothing. Ours keeps the scene clock at the advance time inside ticks (one rate ahead), re-derived the clock base every tick from that clock, and gave `SetTime` our clock: each such take re-ran the tick once more and left the creature a tick ahead, the base following it down until it wrapped below 0 and `SetTime` reset the ticks to 0. The fighters' `Stats` gained a tick at every state they took (14 in the first 1800 frames of a Desert fight); fighters re-ran a tick (physics and brain) at each of theirs (Katana's first at frame 1634); a FirePit launcher that had fired took, 26 s later, the `Charge` state it had registered at that tick count before. Now the base is set once (the creature's tick-0 time, at least 1) and `SetTime`, its re-ticks and `UnpackState` use the original's times (with FINDINGS 291 our in-tick clock is the original's). The Stats tick at base + (k + 1) x 100 ms throughout a fight; a fired launcher waits until the activator charges it again. Seeded fights change from the first state a fighter takes in combat (Desert, City, BladePit, Iceberg; PracticeArena's is unchanged).

279. **Which instances a reference can name** [Core (disc) `DE_CObject::ObjInitContents` 0x7003aa80 (flags +4 |= 0x16), `ObjIsSystem` 0x70170560 (+4 & 6 clear), `ObjSetAsSystemObject` 0x70170570, `ObjSetAsSystemObjectTree` 0x7003b590, `ObjNewCopy` 0x7003b360, `DE_CPassiveObjectReference::Create` 0x7004dbd0 / `ObjPrepare` 0x7004db70; Core (2.123) `DE_CTransferAgent::ExtractReference` 0x2005b4c0]: FINDINGS 275's "flags bit 1 or 2" is right, and those bits are the inverse of `ObjIsSystem`: every object gets them in `ObjInitContents`, and only system objects have them cleared. 2.123's `ExtractReference` walks the target's instance list from the newest, skipping system objects, and takes the master when none is left. `ObjNewCopy` also appends each copy to its source's file-entry instance list and copies the source's bits 0-2 (a system child's copy is system), so `NewCopy`s are instances too. `DE_CPassiveObjectReference::Create` (and its `ObjPrepare`) makes the object it creates a system object; passive actor references and `NewResource` leave theirs as they are; session modifiers' property copies, slot components and unique-property copies are system trees. Ours: every copy of a loaded object joins its instance list, an object reference's created object is system, and `World::referent` skips system instances and those destroyed before the referring construct was made (a destroyed one it would have named is null). Seeded fights are unchanged by this.

280. **Who a fighter's death is credited to: `m_LastDamageInflictor`** [scripts (2.123 Fighter.DEObjects, compiled `BFighter`): `ReceiveDamage`, `UninterruptibleState.AttackableReceiveAttack`, `FighterRecoverFromBeingHit`, `SendDeathEvent`, `Ground.Destruction`; Session `BScoringSystem` (`CreatureProcessEvent`), `BCrowdResponseSlot` (`SlotProcessEvent`), `BLastManStanding`; OMF (disc) `OwnerCaller` 0x35012590 (`DE_CAttackInfo` +0x14)]: the member is script-only. Its name is in no 2.123 module, nor in the 2004 launcher build (only Pilots' `BStandardPilot` declares a member of that name, its own); in the bytecode it is `BFighter`'s reference member 57 (op 62 stores it, op 64 reads it; VMC2 names no reference member). `ReceiveDamage` sets it after `AdjustHealthAndStun` when the attack's owner is an actor: always for a fighter, otherwise only while it is unset, so a hazard or an unowned attack leaves the last hitting fighter credited; `UninterruptibleState.AttackableReceiveAttack` (the `Ground.Destruction` state's) does the same before passing its copy on. `FighterRecoverFromBeingHit` clears it unless the fighter is dead (`Air.Fall`, the stands, `Ground.Stunned`, `FighterChangedState` when not being hit), so a fighter knocked off who recovers in the air falls into a death volume uncredited. `SendDeathEvent(destroyed)` (once) sends FighterDead with Object1 the fighter, Object2 the member and INT3 the destruction to the session and itself, and OMFAttackerDestructionHit (Object1 the fighter) to the member. Its readers: `BScoringSystem` counts a destruction (INT3) for the slot owning Object2, which feeds `BSinglePlayerSubSession`'s destruction remarks; `BCrowdResponseSlot` raises its crowd's reaction on the destruction hit; the end of a round uses Object1 only, so the score and the HUD do not depend on it. Ours ran this from the bytecode already (a seeded knockout's FighterDead names the hitter; a recovered fall names nobody); only `OPENBG_KO`, which drains health through `AdjustHealthAndStun`, left it unset. It now also stores the player in that member, found as the reference member `SendDeathEvent` reads: the death event names the player and the destruction hit reaches it. Seeded fights, Practice and the KO run's state digests are unchanged, and so are the KO run's screenshots.

281. **The Trophy Room's "Obtained From:" text** [Core (2.123) `GetStringValueCaller` 0x20119500 (disc 0x70114370: vtable +0x11c) → `DE_CValueString::GetValue(DE_CString &)` 0x2003dea0 (disc 0x70039cf0: the string at +0x2c, length +0x30), `SplitNewLine` 0x2002d160 (disc 0x700298a0), `DE_CFlatText3::SetText` 0x201d3be0 (disc 0x701ccc50), `CFontInfo::Update` 0x201d1f00 (disc 0x701caf70); script (2.123 Trophy Room) `BTrophyController`; Trophy Room.DEObjects values]: the controller sets its text to `g_ObtainedFrom.GetValue(m_Text)`, the `ObtainedFrom` value beside the controller ("Obtained From:" and two line breaks), then appends the trophy description's `ObtainedFrom` ("Tournament Victory-", a line break, "North American Open"). `SetText` splits the text at each line feed, strips trailing CR and LF from each line and keeps an empty line as a blank one; `CFontInfo::Update` lays each line out on its own, one line spacing lower, with no wrapping and no separator. So the original shows four lines: "Obtained From:", a blank line, "Tournament Victory-", "North American Open". The data strings are as intended: the port lacked the native `CValueString::GetValue(class CText)`, so `m_Text` kept the trophy's name and the text read "North American OpenTournament Victory-". Ours now has it (the value's own text, as set at run time when it has been); the layout code was already the original's.

282. **A spark copy's scene is its fighter's** [OMF (2.123) `DE_COMF2Fighter::NewGetHitEffect` 0x10726b20 (disc 0x35026710), `NewGetHitSpark` 0x10726e70 (disc 0x35026a60)]: both end with `DE_CScene::AddTemporal(fighter +0x10, spark process, fighter +0x1dc)` and the same scene's `Add` (vtable +0x134; disc +0x130) with the copy: the copy joins the scene the fighter is in, whichever session is being updated (FINDINGS 265's clock follows from it). Ours entered it into the current session's scene; it now takes the fighter's (`World::scene_session_of`).

283. **When a light searches its touched polygons again** [Direct3D (2.123) `DE_CD3DLight::Update` 0x1032d080 (disc 0x2002c8c0), `UpdateTouchedPolygons` 0x1032d4e0, `UpdateConeUVWs` 0x1032d790, `DE_CStandardRenderAffector::Update` 0x10306590 (disc 0x20006570), `CalculateLocation` 0x10306810; constants 5.0, 4.0, -50.0, 1.0, 0.1 at 0x103fdd70, 0x103fde80, 0x103fde84, 0x103fbd34, 0x103fde7c, and 250.0 (double) at 0x103fbd28]: every frame, after `UpdateLitActors`, with A the light's render affector +0x138 (min(1, 250 / max(its camera-space depth, 1)) while it is in view and less than its reach R (+0x11c: a sphere's falloff, a cone's far end) behind the camera, else 0): a light that lights nothing sets its budget B (+0x110) to 0 and stops. Otherwise B -= A x the distance it moved since the last frame, and -= 5 x |R - last R|; when B <= 0 it searches (`UpdateTouchedPolygons`) and sets its projection budget U (+0x114) to 0. A cone also takes U -= A^3, then with its axis (the affector's camera-space Z turned by the light's frame) c = -50 A (axis . last axis - 1): U -= c, B -= 4c, U -= 5 A x the distance, U -= |R - last R|; when U <= 0 it searches too if it has not and B < 0, sets U = 1 and recomputes its projection (`UpdateConeUVWs`). After a search B = 0.1 R. A light at zero intensity (or switched off) leaves its lit actors and empties its touched polygons (+0xec) without touching B, so they stay empty until its next search. The disc build set U = 4 after each projection, weighted the distance by 0.5 in U, had no A^3 term, and its turning term was 5 A (axis . last axis), which counted a still cone as turning. Ours searched every frame; it now keeps each light's budgets by its object, finds a target's polygons at its first draw after a search with the light as it was then, and draws a cone's projection as it was when U last ran out. Seeded fights and Practice through the menus draw as before (no pixel off by more than 8 at the frames compared); in Practice's fight the target marker searched 64 times in about 1500 frames.

284. **The player's robot destroyed before North American Open's first match is the original's too** [scripts (2.123) `BCreatureAndPilotPicker` (`WaitingToLoad`, `Loading`, `AttemptToCreateCreature`), `BRulesDisplay` (`LoadingCoreElements`: one `g_OMFSessionLoadElements` at a time), `BFighter` (`FighterJustCreated`, `ObjectSpawn`, `AttackableDestroy`, `Ground.Destruction`, `SendDeathEvent`, `FighterProcessEvent`), Session `BStandardOMFSession` (`StandardSessionSpawnSlot`); Session (disc) `DE_CStandardSessionSlot::OwnCreature` 0x4501aa60; Creature (disc) `DE_CCreature::Tick` 0x15016870, `ObjPrepare` 0x15016050, `SetCreationTime` 0x150195d0; OMF (disc) `DE_CFighterPhysics::ObjPrepare` 0x35039730, `Tick` 0x35039970; Core (disc) `DE_CPhysics::ObjProcessContents` 0x701d9640]: the destruction death event at frame 248 of a North American Open launch (Welcome, in BladePit) is the player's Jaguar, made while the rules display loads the match. The rules display has the elements load one at a time, the environment first; each picker then makes its robot (`NewResource`) and `OwnCreature` adds it to the session's scene where its data puts it. Jaguar's root actor (Jaguar.DEObjects #0) is at (0, 0, 0), Force's and Mantis's at (0, 50, 0), and BladePit's centre floor is at y 18, so the Jaguar starts 18 units under the floor and the others above it. The fighter's script then waits invisible in `FighterJustCreated` until `ObjectSpawn` places it, but nothing holds its physics: `DE_CCreature::Tick` waits only while the scene's time is under its creation time (the picker's `Scene().Time()` when it began waiting to load, 0 here, which `SetCreationTime` makes 1), `DE_CFighterPhysics::ObjPrepare` sets the fighter (+0x1a8) and its `Tick` runs whenever that and the enabled flag (+0x38, the data's v2 BOOL: 1 for every robot) are set. Its floor sphere touches no face from below, so the Jaguar falls into the pit; an attack volume in the pit destroys it (`AttackableDestroy` with a delay: the pre-destruction effect and the explosion play), and after `Ground.Destruction`'s waits `SendDeathEvent(true)` sends FighterDead with no killer to the session and the Jaguar. Nothing counts it: the round's end ignores deaths before the action starts, `BScoringSystem` needs a killer, and the session's `StandardSessionSpawnSlot` places it (`ObjectSpawn`) and sends `g_OMFFighterRevive`, which makes it visible again and clears `m_DeathEventSent`. The event reaches the right fighter, and no stand-in is involved; the port follows the original here, so nothing was changed.

285. **The rest of the touched-polygon rule** [Direct3D (2.123) `DE_CStandardRenderAffector::CalculateLocation` 0x10306810, `Update` 0x10306590, `DE_CD3DLight::Update` 0x1032d080, `UpdateTouchedPolygons` 0x1032d4e0; Direct3D (disc) `DE_CRendererDirect3D::Render` 0x20026e10, `DE_CStandardRenderer::UpdateActorPositions` 0x2000c270, `MarkVisibleAffectors` 0x2000c3e0, `DE_CStandardRenderAffector::DoVisibilityTree` 0x20006460, `DE_CStandardRenderActor::ProcessActor` 0x20004290, `DE_CStandardRenderLight::NotifyRenderActorDisconnect` 0x2000f020, `DE_CStandardRenderer::CreateRenderLight` 0x2000c440 / `DestroyRenderLight` 0x2000c4e0; Core (2.123) `SetUnrenderedCaller` 0x20092a30, `IsRenderedCaller` 0x20092c00]: (1) the "in view" (+0x90) the camera weight (FINDINGS 283) reads is set by `CalculateLocation` from the affector's own rendered flag (`DE_CActor` +0x108, which `SetRendered` / `SetUnrendered` write), every frame in `UpdateActorPositions`, before `MarkVisibleAffectors` tests the view (`DoVisibilityTree`); the weight is computed before that test, so it never sees the frustum. (2) `UpdateTouchedPolygons` walks every projector target of the renderer (+0x5c8) whose render affector the light lights, so a search covers the targets that exist then, in their actors' frames of then. (3) A renderer's light is made when its render actor processes the Light (`ProcessActor` → `CreateRenderLight`) and destroyed with it (`NotifyRenderActorDisconnect`, the Light's destruction), so a light that leaves the scene loses its budgets and comes back as a new one. Ours: each light's affector's rendered flag zeroes its weight; every light of a gathering is updated once, before any pass; a target drawn for the first time after a light's search gets no polygons until its next search; each view keeps its own lights, and a light missing from a gathering drops its state. Not determined: `DE_CD3DLight::Update`'s first test (its virtual +0x80 and the light's +0xa8), which the port treats as passed by every light it gathers. Practice through the menus, a Desert fight and the Trophy Room draw as before.

290. **KillCallers closes the frames it drops** [Core (2.123) `DE_CPCExecuter::KillCallers` 0x20158540 (disc 0x70152cd0), `DE_CExecuteInfo::Destroy` 0x201596c0 (disc 0x70153e30), `DE_CClassCompiledMethod::CloseStack` 0x201423c0]: for each frame below the running one, oldest first, `KillCallers` does what a frame's end does (`DE_CExecuteInfo::Destroy`): `CloseStack` queues its class instances for the class factory's update (FINDINGS 218), its local references are deleted and its local instances destroyed (vtable +0xd4). Ours dropped those frames without closing them; it now closes each one. Seeded fights are unchanged by this.

286. **`DE_CD3DLight::Update`'s first test: is its actor rendered** [Direct3D (2.123) `DE_CD3DLight::Update` 0x1032d080 (`CALL [EAX + 0x80]`, then `[ESI + 0x30]` +0xa8), the vtables `DE_CD3DLight` 0x103fddd8 / `DE_CStandardRenderLight` 0x103fc2d0 (+0x80 `DE_CStandardRenderLight::IsVisible` 0x1030f6d0) and `DE_CD3DRenderActor` 0x103fd9e4 / `DE_CStandardRenderActor` 0x103fba84 (+0x80 `DE_CStandardRenderActor::IsVisible` 0x10304440), `DE_CStandardRenderActor::MirrorInvalidated` 0x10303fd0, `SetActor` 0x10304220; disc the same at 0x200fcdc0 / 0x2000f710, 0x200fc9d0 / 0x20004420, `MirrorInvalidated` 0x20003fc0, `SetActor` 0x20004200, `DE_CStandardRenderLight::SetLight` 0x2000f510 (+0x30 the render actor, from its reference +0x2c), `DE_CStandardRenderActor::ProcessActor` 0x20004290 (a Light among the actor's properties gets a render light with that render actor)]: the light's +0x30 is its render actor, the render actor of the actor that holds the Light; the virtual +0x80 is `IsVisible`, which forwards to that render actor's own +0x80 `IsVisible`, true when its +0xa8 is set; and +0xa8 is a copy of the actor's rendered flag (`DE_CActor` +0x108), taken by `SetActor` and again by `MirrorInvalidated` whenever the actor changes. So both halves of the test ask the same thing: is the light's actor rendered. When it is not, the light leaves its lit actors and empties its touched polygons (+0xec = 0) without touching its budgets, as at zero intensity (FINDINGS 283). The 2004 dev build names none of these classes (it is the launcher; no `DE_CD3DLight`, `DE_CStandardRenderLight` or `DE_CStandardRenderActor` string). Ours: a light whose affector is unrendered is cleared that frame and draws nothing until its next search.

287. **The rendered flag comes from the actor's data** [Core (disc) `DE_CActor::ObjInitContents` 0x7008fa20 (+0x108 = 1), `ObjProcessContents` 0x7008fca0 (versions 2 to 7: name, position, coordinate system, then BOOLs into +0xd8, +0xdc, +0xe4 and +0x108); Core (2.123) `SetUnrenderedCaller` 0x20092a30 / `IsRenderedCaller` 0x20092c00 (+0x108)]: an actor's fourth contents BOOL is its rendered flag. Ours started every actor rendered; it now reads that BOOL (`de::ActorInfo::rendered`) into `GameObject::visible`. In the shipped robots and arenas the cleared ones are affectors (hit detectors, push and floor spheres, needs, speakers, testers) and placement actors (bones, spawn and follow points, jump-pad markers): Practice through the menus, a Desert fight, the Trophy Room, seeded fights and Practice's state digests are unchanged.

288. **Every GUI copy of a projector-lit mesh is a target of its own** [Direct3D (disc) `DE_CStandardRenderActor::ProcessActor` 0x20004290 (each render actor gets its own render mesh elements), `DE_CD3DRenderMeshElement::InitialSetup` 0x2002e550, `DE_CD3DLight::UpdateTouchedPolygons` 0x2002cd00 (per element)]: the projector targets the touched-polygon search walks are render mesh elements, one per actor that draws the mesh, so copies of one mesh are searched and kept apart. The front end drew its GUI and model-picker actors without an instance, so their copies shared one target in the cache; they now pass their object as the instance and follow FINDINGS 285 like the game's actors.

289. **Lighting off stops the lights' updates** [Direct3D (disc) `DE_CRendererDirect3D::Render` 0x20026e10: the shadow casters' and lights' `Update` only while `g_LightingEnabled` and the renderer's +0x184 are set; `DE_CStandardRenderActor::ProcessActor` 0x20004290 makes render lights only with +0x184]: with lighting off no `DE_CD3DLight::Update` runs; but lighting only changes through an options apply, which makes every light anew (FINDINGS 296). +0x184 is the renderer's applied Lighting option (`CRenderOptions` +0, FINDINGS 180), the same switch. Not ported: render lights are made only while it is set (`ProcessActor`), so an actor processed with lighting off has no render light until it is processed again; when `ProcessActor` runs again for an existing actor was not traced, and ours keeps every light.

296. **When a renderer makes its lights again** [Direct3D (disc) `DE_CStandardRenderActor::SetActor` 0x20004200 (the only caller of `ProcessActor`, vtable +0xa4), `DE_CStandardRenderer::AddActor` 0x2000aed0 (the only caller of `SetActor`, vtable +0xa0; from `Render` 0x20009d40 for the actors added since), `ProcessActor` 0x20004290 (render lights only while +0x184 is set), `DE_CRendererDirect3D` vtable 0x200fcb24 (+0x134 `SceneActorTreeAdded`, +0x138 `SceneActorTreeRemoved`; 2.123 0x103fdb38: +0x138 / +0x13c); Core (disc) `DE_CRenderer::ApplyOptions` 0x701851a0 / (2.123) 0x2018ae90, `DE_CRenderer::SetScene` 0x70185160, `DE_CSceneWatcher::RefreshAllActors` 0x700779d0 / (2.123) 0x2007bea0; data: SinglePlayer's `Cinematic` session master's `ForceLighting` and `ForceReflections` BOOLs]: `ProcessActor` runs only when a render actor is made for an actor, which happens when the actor comes into the renderer's scene. An actor's mirror invalidation does not run it (`MirrorInvalidated` only copies the rendered flag). `ApplyOptions` copies the options into the renderer (2.123 +0x188; disc +0x184), sets Lighting and Reflections when the renderer's scene keeps its never-autopause flag (2.123 scene +0x1d0, disc +0x1c0: the GUI's scene) and Lighting / Reflections when the scene's session's master description has `ForceLighting` / `ForceReflections` set, and then `RefreshAllActors` takes every root actor out of the renderer and puts it back. That destroys every render actor with its render lights and makes them anew, the lights only if lighting is now on. `SetScene` applies the renderer's options again for its new scene. So the lights of actors processed while lighting was off come back at the next options apply that turns it on, all of them made anew, and any apply, or a new scene for the renderer, restarts every light's budgets; nothing keeps a light's state while lighting is off (this corrects FINDINGS 289). The model picker's scene clears its never-autopause flag (FINDINGS 188), so it follows the option. Ours: each options apply (`World::render_options_applied`) and each change of the scene a renderer shows drops that renderer's light states; the game view is lit when the option is on or the shown session's master description has `ForceLighting`; the picker's view follows the option, the GUI's is always lit. Reflections: FINDINGS 297. Seeded fights, Practice, a Desert fight and the Trophy Room are unchanged with lighting on; Practice with Lighting Enabled 0 draws as main does.

297. **Reflections Enabled and ForceReflections** [Core (2.123) `DE_CRenderer::ApplyOptions` 0x2018ae90 (renderer +0x18c, options +4: set when the scene keeps its never-autopause flag +0x1d0 or the session's master description has `ForceReflections`), `DE_CMultiStageTextureProcess::CStage::ShouldProcess` 0x2011f9f0 (a stage whose texture manager is a `TextureManager\Reflection` is turned down while the element's renderer, element +0xcc, has +0x18c clear, unless the manager has a `NeverDisable` BOOL set; also when the stage is disabled, +0x34), `DE_CMultiStageTextureProcess::Process` 0x2011f790 (disc 0x7011a5f0); data: SinglePlayer's `Cinematic` master description (`ForceReflections`, `ForceLighting`), which the Trophy Room's session also has; `NeverDisable` on two managers in City and two in Iceberg]: `Process` walks the stages and, when the stage after the current one stacks onto it (+0x2c) and is turned down, processes neither of them nor the stacked stages that follow; so with Reflections off a robot's or a mesh's "mask + environment" pass is not drawn, and a reflection that stacks onto a base stage takes that base with it. A renderer whose scene never autopauses (the GUI's) and a session whose master description has `ForceReflections` reflect whatever the option.
