Ours read the option but used it nowhere: the meshes it builds now leave out such a stacked reflection stage, the stage before it and the stacked stages after it, when the renderer's effective Reflections are off (`render::g_reflections_enabled`: the option or the shown session's `ForceReflections` for the game view, always for the GUI, the option for the model picker), and the game view rebuilds its arena and robots when that changes. A disabled stacked stage (+0x34): FINDINGS 298. With Reflections Enabled 0, Practice's robots lose their reflection pass, while the Trophy Room (Cinematic master, `ForceReflections`) draws exactly as with the option on; with the option on, Practice, a Desert fight, the Trophy Room and seeded fights are unchanged.

298. **A disabled stacked stage drops the stage it stacks on** [Core (2.123) `DE_CMultiStageTextureProcess::Process` 0x2011f790, `CStage::ShouldProcess` 0x2011f9f0 (disc `Process` 0x7011a5f0)]: `Process` treats a stacked stage (+0x2c) whose +0x34 is set like a turned-down reflection: neither it nor the stage it stacks on is processed, nor the stacked stages after it; a disabled stage that starts a pass drops only itself, and a stage without a texture manager is never turned down. Ours had dropped only the disabled stage; it now drops the group. No mesh in the fourteen arenas, Jaguar, Katana, Practice's menus or the Trophy Room has a disabled stacked stage, so none of them draws differently. The actor-mesh renderer the GUI and the model picker share now keeps a build per Reflections state (its batch key starts with it) instead of rebuilding whenever the state flips between the two views.

291. **The scene clock inside a tick** [Core (2.123) `DE_CScene::PassTime` 0x20073f30 (disc 0x70070040), `DE_CTemporalElementSet::AdvanceTime` (disc 0x70072090), `TemporalElementSetForRate` (disc 0x70072230)]: `PassTime` picks the set whose last tick (+0x38) + rate comes first; if that lies within what passes, it sets the scene time (2.123 +0x174, disc +0x160) to the set's last tick, runs `AdvanceTime` (which ticks the elements, then sets +0x38 to rate × (count + 1)) and only then sets the time to the old time + the step, the advance's own time. So everything a set's elements read during their tick (`Time()`, `PreciseTime`, the times scripts and natives store) is one rate before the time that advanced the set, the GUI scenes' sets included. Ours stood at the advance time while the set ticked. Now both passes (`pass_scene`, `tick_gui`) hold the clock at the set's last tick during `advance` and move it on after, and `set_time` no longer converts our clock to the original's (FINDINGS 278). Effects: a seeded AI fight plays the same until the AI's first time-dependent choice (Desert: Katana's at frame 900), then differently; the countdown, READY/FIGHT and the round and match events come on the same frames; screenshots of a fight (frames 700 and 705) and Practice reached through the menus are identical to the pixel; replays (practice, 4000 frames) recorded before and after the change play back within 0.58 units of their recording on either build. North American Open and IFL Cup with `OPENBG_KO=auto` play through to their cinematics and the main menu. One `OPENBG_KO=auto` screenshot run of North American Open stalled in Sabotage: Pyros was in `Ground.Evade.Duck1` when the drain came, a state an AI-controlled Pyros leaves only through a hit (BPyros's `Control.Ground.Duck` has no `AIStandardNavigation`), and the test switch takes health without a hit reaction, so he stayed alive in the duck; `OPENBG_KO=auto:170` plays it through.

292. **SetTime on a creature that has never ticked** [Creature (2.123) `DE_CCreature::SetTime` 0x10215e40, `DE_CStateManager::GetStateOrPrevious` (2.123 0x1021a630, its call at +0x130 restoring)]: when the creature has not ticked yet (+0x20c clear) and the time is not before its creation time, `SetTime` first ticks it once (vtable +0x40; a creature without a rate gets `DeltaTick(0)`, +0x44) and then restores its state manager's newest state at or before the tick it computed (`GetStateOrPrevious`, vtable +0x130), before the rewind or re-tick. Ours skipped this; now a ticked creature does it (a replay's fighter `Stats` takes it as the replay begins; live play never meets it). Creatures without a rate do it too since FINDINGS 300.

293. **A frame's local instances** [Core (disc) `DE_CPCExecuter::ContinueExecutionInternal` op 0x2a (0x7014eaa0), `DE_CPCNodeClass::NewCopyInstance`, `DE_CExecuteInfo::AddLocalInstance` 0x70154370, `DE_CExecuteInfo::Destroy` 0x70153e30 (2.123 0x201596c0), `KillCallers` (2.123 0x20158540)]: op 42 copies an instance through its class (`NewCopyInstance`, the class's +0xd8) and adds (class, instance) to the frame's local-instance list (+0x10, count +0x14). When the frame ends, and for each frame `KillCallers` drops, the list is emptied from the last, each instance destroyed at once by its class (vtable +0xd4), unlike the `CloseStack` instances, which are queued (FINDINGS 218). Ours keeps the list per frame, gives op 42's copy the class it names, and destroys (forgets) it as the frame closes. Seeded fights are unchanged.

294. **Volcano's file and its master object's name** [shell: `deobj-dump header` on Volcano.DEObjects]: Volcano's master object is named "Volcanoe" while the file is Volcano.DEObjects. The windowed and screenshot views found the session's arena by the environment file's master object name and threw "no arena file Volcanoe" (exit 1) whenever a tournament drew Volcano; headless runs never render, so they passed. The view now takes the file's stem.

299. **A replayed session's managers join before their entries run** [Core `DE_CStateManager::ObjPrepare` (FINDINGS 137, `re/specs/replay-playback.md`), `IsStateAlterable` (replay-driven +0x78); scripts (2.123 SinglePlayer) `BSinglePlayerSubSession` (`Entry`, `SubSessionUpdate`, `SetupMatch`, `SubSessionPickCreatureByGUID`, `SubSessionPickEnvironment`), Session `BCreatureAndPilotPicker`, `BEnvironmentPicker`]: a state manager joins its session's record when it is prepared, before its owner's executors run their entries, so in a replay a recorded owner is replay-driven from its first instruction. `BSinglePlayerSubSession::Entry` registers the `SubSessionUpdate` hook only `if (IsStateAlterable())`, so a replayed tournament match never runs `SetupMatch`, whose opponents and arena are random picks; the pickers' recorded states (all registered at scene time 0, before the scene's genesis, so the last one, with the choices, is kept) supply them. Ours joined a manager in the replay's first update, after the entries: the replayed sub-session registered its hook, `SetupMatch` re-picked the computer slots' robots and the arena after the pickers had taken their recorded states, and a North American Open replay showed other robots in another arena (Stacy and Gracy's Force and Mantis in The Blade Pit came back as Jaguar and Chronos in Neo Vegas); Practice, which has no such setup, was unaffected. `IsStateAlterable` (`replay_driven`) now joins an owner that has a state manager and is in the replayed session when it is first asked. Replays of North American Open's Stacy and Gracy match (3 fighters, 3500 frames), Raven (2) and Sabotage (4) now build the recorded robots and arena and keep the fighters within 1.94, 1.71 and 0.00 units of the recording; Practice stays within 0.58. Live play is unchanged.

300. **SetTime's first tick for a creature without a rate** [Creature (2.123) `DE_CCreature::SetTime` 0x10215e40]: a creature without a tick rate that has never ticked gets `DeltaTick(0)` (vtable +0x44) and then its state at or before the given time (`GetStateOrPrevious` of the time itself, the tick of a rateless creature being its time); its tick count becomes the time. Ours does the same through its delta tick (`creature_delta_tick`); no replay tested meets it (the creatures given their first tick there all have rates, such as the fighting surfaces and the fighters' `Stats`).

301. **The help pages, all of them** [Core (disc) `DE_CControlCreator::CreateNewControl` 0x7021c520, (2.123) 0x20224ee0; `DE_CPageViewerControl3d::AdjustViewClient` 0x700f9550; FINDINGS 238; OMFHelp.DEObjects' 167 help descriptions and their ControlCreators]: every page, opened from the main menu's Help (`OPENBG_HELP_CYCLE` shows each entry as a click on its row does), is laid out as FINDINGS 238 describes: its text blocks wrap to the page and are left-aligned or centred by their creator's alignment, a page taller than the view gets the vertical scroll bar (21 of the 167: Control Notation, Credits, the longer arena pages, Quick Reference and the Options, Single-Player, HUD, stats, combos and control pages), and no line runs past the page. `CreateNewControl(0)` gives the page viewer's own page only a vertical stacking robot, so the page is as wide as the view and the viewer never shows a horizontal bar there; an image row wider than the page (the arena pages' four pictures, centred) is cut at the view's edges, as ours draws it. PLACEHOLDERS' note that 2.112+ help text is not wrapped and runs past the page predates FINDINGS 238 and is dropped.

306. **Why a replay drifts from its recording: the original's own** [Creature (disc) `DE_CCreature::Tick` 0x15016870 (2.123 0x10216990), `UnpackState` 0x15015160, `SetTime` 0x15015d20; Core `DE_CClassExecutorInstance::IsExecuteStatesFromAllInstant` 0x70146440 (+0x14 → executor +0x4c); FINDINGS 176, 224, 270]: a recording holds only the states scripts register, and a replay re-simulates every tick between them. When a replayed fighter takes a recorded state in its tick (`GetState` of exactly that tick), `Tick` skips its physics and brain, and `UnpackState` runs at once only the executor instances whose ClassExecutor has +0x4c set; the fighters' executors (`PyrosExecutor` and the others: contents v5, the BOOL after +0x48 is 0) do not, so the brain resumes from the `Register*State` call on the next tick. In the recorded tick the code after that call (the state's animation, momentum, turn) ran in the same tick, so after each taken state the replayed fighter does it one tick late and trails its recording by about one tick of motion until a later state with a position puts it back; the deferred `SetTime` and the after-pass fixup re-take the same state and re-tick, which does not change this. Measured on per-frame samples (3500-frame round trips): the samples off their recording lie on the recorded track 0-2 ticks behind (Practice: 345 of 377), the fighter's tick count is the recording's, and positions at the taken states are exact; making the restored brains run at once after the physics (a test switch, not the original) removes almost all of it (Practice: 10 samples off, worst 0.61). So the drift is the original's, not a stamping, re-tick, precision or `rand()` difference: between states the AI draws nothing in a replay (replayed fighters run no AI commands, FINDINGS 137), the state times are the owner's tick counts on both sides, and the positions are packed as 12 raw bytes.

307. **A fighter's restored state waits for its next tick in ours too** [Core `DE_CClassExecutor::ObjProcessContents` 0x701434b0 (+0x4c), `IsExecuteStatesFromAllInstant` 0x70146440; Creature `UnpackState` 0x15015160]: ours read +0x4c from a ClassExecutor found among the owner's children and components and took 1 when there was none; a fighter's executors are its properties, so every restored fighter state ran its brain at once, before the physics was restored over what it had just set. Each brain now keeps the +0x4c of the ClassExecutor that made it. This changes live play wherever a fighter takes back its own state registered since its last tick (FINDINGS 270): seeded fights differ from the first such take. Round trips (every 30 frames, worst distance): North American Open's Stacy and Gracy 1.94 → 1.94, Raven 1.71 → 0.58, Sabotage 0.00 → 0.00, Practice 0.58 → 0.64; a recording made before plays with its fighters as before (worst 1.94; a pre-match robot template falling out of the scene differs by 9.2).

311. **A script's own speaker: `DE_CSpeaker`'s contents, `SetActiveNoise` and `Process`** [Core (2.123) `DE_CSpeaker::ObjProcessContents` 0x20221d70, `SetActiveNoise` 0x202226a0, `Frequency` 0x20222870, `Process` 0x20222500, `DE_CNoise::GetNoiseToPlay` 0x2021d4e0, `MATH::CenterRand` 0x200296f0 (constants 0x2025e84c = 32768, 0x2025e850 = 0.5; cap 0x202644a0 = 1.5); disc Core `DE_CSpeaker::Process` 0x70219a60, `Play` 0x70219d60, `Stop` 0x70219d70, `MATH::Rand` 0x70025e70 / 0x70025ea0; scripts (2.123 Session) `BCrowdResponse`; data: Session.DEObjects `SpeakerActor` / `Speaker`]: a speaker's contents follow its unique actor property chunk: v1 [name][Volume, a FLOAT advanced reference (+0x9c)][loop +0xb8], v2 + autoplay (+0xbc), v3 + a frequency advanced reference read into a local and dropped, v4 + finish (+0xc0), v5 + track (+0xc4); v6 is [name][Volume][loop][autoplay][finish][track][squeezed 3D mode +0x98][f32 frequency +0xb0] (0 becomes 1). Loading copies autoplay into the pending play (+0xcc). `Play` and `Stop` only set +0xcc and +0xd8. `SetActiveNoise(n)` (a noise) sets the pending play to autoplay when n differs from the active noise (+0x8c), makes n active, and takes `GetNoiseToPlay` into +0x94 (with alternates, `MATH::Rand(100.0f)` = rand() × 100 / 32768 below the alternate chance picks alternate `MATH::Rand(count)` = rand() × count / 32768), then +0xb4 = min(1.5, (1 + CenterRand(variance)) × +0xb0 × frequency) of that noise, CenterRand(v) being rand() × v / 32768 − v / 2: rand() is drawn on every call, variance or not. `Frequency()` is +0xb4 (1 with no active noise). `Process(element)`, run by the speaker element's update every audio frame: the element takes +0x94 as its noise and min(1, Volume × the active noise's volume) as its volume (+0x50); the loop flag sets element flag 0x800 (finish 0x400, used by segments only); with no active noise the element begins its stop transition and Process returns; with track set it copies the element's `IsPlaying` into +0xd0 and `PlayProgress` into +0xd4, and passes a pending `Stop` (element vtable +0x54, `AssureStopTransition`); then a pending play clears +0xcc, calls the element's `Play` and sets +0xd0 to 1. Without track neither copy nor `Stop` happens, so such a speaker reads as playing for ever after its first `Play`. The crowd response's speakers are copies of `SpeakerActor`, a sphere affector at the origin with hotspot 100000 and falloff 1000000 holding a v6 `Speaker`: volume 1, track on, no loop, no autoplay, 3D mode 0, frequency 1. `BCrowdResponse::Play` calls `SetActiveNoise` then `Play` on one of a pair whenever it has stopped and the other is past half way (or stopped), so each new cheer draws rand() at a time the playing waves decide.

312. **The speaker element's play, stop, progress and volume** [AudioPlayer (disc) `DE_CDirectXSpeakerElement` vtable 0x1000fa5c (+0x3c `Play` 0x10005010, +0x40 `Stop` 0x100052f0 (empty), +0x44 `IsPlaying` 0x10004430 → +0x60 `IsAudible` 0x10005300, +0x48 `PlayProgress` 0x10004f70, +0x4c `SetActiveNoise` 0x10004370, +0x54 `AssureStopTransition` 0x10001050 → +0x64 `BeginStopTransition` 0x100053f0, +0x5c `Update` 0x100047b0), `Activate` 0x10004460, `DE_CStandardSpeakerElement::Update` 0x10006a50, `DE_CDirectXNoiseElement::AssureLoopable` 0x10005a50; AudioPlayer (2.123) `Play` 0x10004da0, `PlayProgress` 0x10004d00, `SetActiveNoise` 0x100041a0, `IsAudible` 0x10005030, `BeginStopTransition` 0x10005120 (the same logic; 2.123's `SetActiveNoise` also clears flag 0x8000000); Core (disc) `DE_CSpeakerElement::DE_CSpeakerElement` 0x70215530, `GetNoiseVolume` 0x70215770 (2.123 0x2021e0d0), `DE_CSphereAffector::IsTouching` 0x7008a1b0]: an element made for an affector and its speaker copies a sphere affector's hotspot (+0x184) and falloff (+0x17c) into +0x5c / +0x60 (0 for any other affector) and starts with volume −1. Each update first takes the affector's position (`DE_CStandardSpeakerElement::Update`) and runs the speaker's `Process`, then `GetNoiseVolume`: the affector's `IsTouching` at the listener (a sphere: 1 within the hotspot, falling linearly to 0 at the falloff; no Intensity value) × GlobalSFXVolume × +0x50, at most 1; the update-rate accumulator and the 10000 log10(1 − (1 − v s)²) buffer volume of FINDINGS 121 follow, a value below −10000 failing so that the buffer keeps its volume. `SetActiveNoise` sets the rate +0xb0 = min(1, length / 7000 + 0.1) unless looping. `Play` for a wave releases the old buffer and duplicates a fresh one (`CreateNewAudiopath`), so a wave always restarts; with the loop flag the noise element is made loopable (`AssureLoopable`, +0x30: played with DSBPLAY_LOOPING, started once the volume passes 0.09 and stopped below 0.03, flag 0x4000000); a one-shot is not started at or beyond +0x60 from the listener (flag 0x1000, which the player's update drops) and otherwise, unless its SpeakerVolume is 0, `Activate`d: SetVolume(−10000), Play, SetFrequency(trunc(the wave's rate × `DE_CSpeaker::Frequency`)). `Play` clears the stop transition and runs `Update` at once. `IsPlaying` is `IsAudible`: a loopable noise element counts as playing while the element has its affector, a wave as its buffer's DSBSTATUS_PLAYING. `PlayProgress` is the buffer's write cursor over the current noise element's EX payload bytes − 44 (the canonical WAVE header), 0 without a buffer. `Stop` (`AssureStopTransition` → `BeginStopTransition`) sets flag 0x4000 and a 1500 ms countdown (+0x98, +0x9c) for a wave; each update (without the accumulator) then sets the volume 10000 log10(remaining / 1500 × the shaped volume) and takes the frame's milliseconds off; past 0 it sets −10000 and `Activate(0)` stops and releases the buffer.

313. **Ours: the scripts' speakers in the mixer, and what they change** [`app/audio.cpp` `Mixer::update_speakers`, `game/music.cpp` `MusicSystem::set_active_noise`; FINDINGS 311-312]: the CSpeaker natives the scripts use are `MusicPlayerSpeaker`, `ActiveNoise`, `SetActiveNoise`, `Play`, `Stop`, `SetVolume`, `IsPlaying`, `PlayProgress` and `SetMood` (`SetFrequency` appears only commented out, in PowerPlant's `BCenterMechanics`). A script's own speaker keeps the speaker's state, and once a frame after the scene's tick the mixer runs `Process` and the element for each one under an affector in an audible scene, its voice going through the same mixer as the other sounds (32.32 fixed-point positions, the same Hermite interpolation and dsound software 3D pan); `PlayProgress` is the voice's mixed position, standing for the write cursor. The music player's speaker keeps its own path (its `SetActiveNoise` was later given the original's rand() draw, FINDINGS 315). Without an audio device or a pump (headless, screenshot), the voices advance by the frame's time at 44100 Hz exactly as a recording run's pump does, so `OPENBG_RECORD_AUDIO` changes nothing about them. Because `SetActiveNoise` now draws rand() as the original's does, and the crowd calls it when its voices end, single-player matches leave their old course at the crowd's first noise: North American Open (seed 7, `OPENBG_KO=auto`) is identical to before up to frame 1313, where the first cheer starts, and at the knockout of frame 2442 Chronos stands at (−169, 5, 65) instead of (−170, 5, 64); the tournament still plays through to its end. `openbg-fight` sessions have no crowd response: seeded fights are byte-identical and their music bit-identical. A recording of that tournament with the music off has the cheers and clapping from 26 s at about −24 dB RMS where the old build was silent, panned by the arena origin's side of the player and fading with the crowd's excitement. The positional sounds' frequency variance had used ±variance; it is CenterRand's ±variance / 2 now (the same single rand() draw).

314. **Speakers no script touches: where their elements come from** [AudioPlayer (disc) `DE_CStandardAudioPlayer::IsValidActor` 0x10006130, `AddActor` 0x100061d0, `RemoveActor` 0x100062c0, `PlayAudio` 0x10006350, `DE_CDirectXAudioPlayer::AddActor` 0x10003280, `NewSpeakerElement` 0x10001a30, `~DE_CDirectXSpeakerElement` 0x10004220; Core `DE_CSpeaker::ObjProcessReferences` (2.123 0x20222130, disc 0x70219490); `deobj-dump tree` and the speakers' contents in every 2.123 data file]: the audio player watches its scene. An actor that is an affector, entering it, gets one speaker element per speaker among its properties (+0x20 / +0x24; a speaker with +0x4c set is skipped), and `NewSpeakerElement` runs the speaker's `Process` at once; an actor leaving it takes itself from its elements (their +0xc goes null). Every `PlayAudio` first deletes the elements flagged 0x1000 that are not fading (0x4000) or no longer heard (`IsAudible`), the destructor stopping and releasing the buffer; updates every element (`Update` with the listener); then, for each element without its speaker or affector: one no longer heard is flagged 0x1000, one heard begins its 1500 ms stop transition and is flagged, unless it is already fading or has the finish flag (0x400: the speaker's finish without loop), which lets a one-shot play on to its end. An element without its affector takes its volume from its own copy of the sphere round its last position (`GetNoiseVolume`'s second path) and a looping noise element no longer counts as heard, so loops stop at once. `ObjProcessReferences` calls `SetActiveNoise` on the noise the data names, so a loaded speaker has a pending play when it autoplays (its active noise was null) and draws rand() for `CenterRand` (and the alternates) as it loads. Of the 119 shipped speakers all autoplay but the crowd response's, and most loop; nearly all sit on sphere affectors, one on a cone (Warlord's `ConeNoise` holding `GrenadeConeSpeaker`), none on a box or mesh; the rest sit under plain actors, creatures or folders and get no element (`IsValidActor` wants an affector). City's `CityAmbientSpeaker` and `TorchSpeaker` lie in its `Noises` folder, which nothing places in the scene, so City has no ambience in the original either. Ours: each audio update scans the scene for speakers on affectors, makes and removes elements as `AddActor` / `RemoveActor` do (an element dropped by the player is not made again while its affector stays), and runs the end-of-update rules. The load-time draws use a stream of their own rather than the shared rand(): the original makes them as each data file loads and as each copy is made, which our loader does not reproduce in order. Heard in 1500-frame AI fights (music off): Canyon's wind loop alone at about −45 dB RMS where the old build was silent; Iceberg's sea waves, spinning pillar and missile-trigger loops at about −23 dB; Space Station's ring and structure loops at about −11 dB (the ring speaker has volume 1 and its sphere holds the arena); Desert's wind and the fighter-jet pads' loops, starting and stopping by volume as the fighters move; Power Plant's rain at about −28 dB; Volcano's lava loop near the spawn, stopping below 0.03 as the camera leaves it; Blade Pit's fire and blade loops; Fire Pit's ambience and seekers. No clipping and no discontinuities at the loop seams; WAV renders in the scratchpad (`cs/wav`).

315. **The music player's `SetActiveNoise` draws rand() too** [Core (2.123) `DE_CSpeaker::SetActiveNoise` 0x202226a0 (FINDINGS 311); scripts (2.123 Music) `BMusicPlayer`]: the music player's speaker is a `DE_CSpeaker` like any other, so `BMusicPlayer`'s `MusicPlayerSpeaker().SetActiveNoise(m_CurrentNoise)`, run whenever the speaker is not playing and the mood is above 1, draws `CenterRand` (one rand(); a segment has no alternates) before `Play`. Ours now does. In a seeded `openbg-fight` (Jaguar against Katana in Desert, seed 7) that happens once, at frame 60 when the mood first rises to 2, and the fight leaves its old course at frame 1130, where Jaguar's laser animation reads rand() (t 0.07 instead of 0.03); with that one draw taken from another stream the log is byte-identical to before. Tournaments diverge the same way from their first music start.

316. **DirectSound's cursors for the game's buffers** [DirectX 9.0b `dsound.dll` (nt, from `DirectX/dxnt.cab` on the disc) with its public symbols (`dsound.pdb`, NB10 3dedc967 age 1, from Microsoft's symbol server; kept out of the repository): `CDirectSoundSecondaryBuffer::GetCurrentPosition` 0x510a334f, `CEmSecondaryRenderWaveBuffer::GetCursorPosition` 0x510acdda, `CMixSource::GetBytePosition1` 0x510af245 / `GetBytePosition` 0x510af2b6 / `Stop` 0x510af127 / `SetFrequency` 0x510af60a, `CGrace::GetBytePosition` 0x510ae6b4 / `MixEndingBuffer` 0x510ae97c, `_PadCursor@16` 0x5109bc6e]: a software secondary buffer's cursors come from its mix source. While it plays, `CGrace::GetBytePosition` puts the write cursor at the source sample that sits at the device's write cursor (the mix position less what is mixed ahead of the device) plus (frequency × 10) >> 10 samples (+0x38, the SetFrequency hertz: about 10 ms), wrapping round the buffer; without the 0x13200 flags (DSBCAPS_GETCURRENTPOSITION2 among them; AudioPlayer's buffers are STATIC | CTRLVOLUME | CTRLFREQUENCY, with CTRL3D or not) `GetBytePosition1` then reports that write cursor as the play cursor and moves the write cursor one sample further. A stopped source reports both cursors at its saved sample (+0x60); `CMixSource::Stop` saves the play position, and `MixEndingBuffer` stops a non-looping buffer that has been mixed to its end with +0x60 = 0, so `PlayProgress` reads 0 after a one-shot ends. Ours: the write cursor is the voice's mixed position plus (hz × 10) >> 10 + 1 samples, modulo the wave's length, so the last ~10 ms of a wave report a progress near 0, as the original's do. The KS (hardware-mixed) buffers' path (`CKsSecondaryRenderWaveBuffer::GetCursorPosition` 0x510b50be) is not modelled.

317. **Head-relative speakers and cone affectors** [AudioPlayer (2.123) `DE_CDirectXSpeakerElement::Play` 0x10004da0, (disc) `Update` 0x100047b0; Core (disc) `DE_TCoord3D<float>::Rotate` 0x7022b420 / `InverseRotate` 0x7022b4e0, `DE_CConeAffector::IsTouching` 0x70080940 / `SetFarAttenuationBegin` 0x700801a0 / `SetFarAttenuationEnd` 0x70080250 / `SetUmbra` 0x70080460 / `SetPenumbra` 0x70080470; speaker contents of every 2.123 data file]: 3D mode 1 (contents +0x98) is shipped on Pickups' `EnergyBoostSpeaker` (and SinglePlayer's copies), Session's `AdrinalineNoise` speaker, Volcano's `MissileSphereSpeaker` and Warlord's two `GrenadeSphereSpeaker`s and `MissileSphereSpeaker`. `Play` then sets DS3DMODE_HEADRELATIVE and +0xa8, and each `Update` with a 3D buffer gives the buffer the offset from the listener turned by the listener's axes with `Rotate` (the axes as rows: local to world, where `InverseRotate` would be world to local), at full volume (v exactly 1) with x × r² and z made positive plus (1 − r) × 200 (r, the distance over the element's hotspot, is 1 for an affector's element), x negated as for every position. Ours does the same when the software 3D pan is on. Warlord's `ConeNoise` holds `GrenadeConeSpeaker`; the cone's `IsTouching` (far attenuation begin +0x17c, end +0x180, umbra +0x198, penumbra +0x19c, degrees) is 0 beyond the far end; else 1 within the begin, falling linearly to the end; then 0 unless the point is ahead (0 < local z < far end) and inside the penumbra cone, scaled between the umbra and the penumbra by the cosines, and past the begin along the axis scaled again by (z − end) / (begin − end). Ours uses it for cone speakers; a cone element keeps +0x60 = 0, so its one-shots never start and its loops follow the volume thresholds.

318. **A light's colour is its value's rgb times its alpha** [Direct3D (disc) `DE_CLight::Color` 0x2002d6e0, (2.123) 0x1032dec0, read from the instructions; (disc) `DE_CD3DLight::Update` 0x2002c8c0; Core (disc) `DE_CLight::ObjProcessContents` 0x701baf70, `DE_CValueModulatedRGBA::GetValue` 0x70111ae0 / 0x70111b70]: `Update` takes the light's colour from `DE_CLight::Color`, which calls the colour Value's GetValue (vtable +0x154 in 2.123) into (r, g, b, a) and returns (a·r, a·g, a·b); the intensity (+0x4c) stays a separate factor. A `ModulatedRGBA` colour's alpha is its colour's alpha times its FLOAT value. Lights whose alpha is not 1 in 2.123's data: Desert's `SUN Light` (0.79), Volcano's `Spot02 Light` (0.8), OMFBGGUI's `New Light` (0.82), Pyros's `Light` (0.28), LoadScene's and Unranked Challenger Cinematic's rotating spots (0.72, 0.74), and the modulated ones: Blast Furnace's flamethrower `Light` (FINDINGS 319), FirePit's and Pyros's `LightProperty` (alpha 0 × `LightColorModulate`), Effects' `LightProperty` (× `Fade`), CinematicMasters' and Unranked Challenger Cinematic's (× an inter value). Ours dropped the alpha, so every one of them shone at full colour.

319. **Blast Furnace's flamethrowers light only when they fire** [`Grudge Match Arena.DEObjects` `BFlamethrower` (Entry, AttemptAttack, FlamethrowerOn); SharedObjects `BEffectFader` (DeltaTick, SlideTo, SetFadeRate); FINDINGS 318]: each of the six `ChainsawAffector` copies holds a `FlamethrowerNoise` actor whose `LightEmmiter` sphere (inner 150, outer 600) carries a `Light` (property, mode 0) coloured by `LightColor`, a `ModulatedRGBA` of (1, 0.8, 0.2) whose FLOAT is the noise's `Intensity` value (0 in the data). `BEffectFader` lowers `Intensity` by the fade rate per ms on every delta tick, clamped to 0..1 (`BFlamethrower` sets the rate to 0.00058); a robot touching `FlamethrowerPOS`'s attack material runs `FlamethrowerOn`, which for 110 frames slides it towards 0.65 + Random(0.5) at 0.2. So at rest the six lights are black. Ours lit all six at full strength, and their projector passes (mask 6) added (1, 0.8, 0.2) over the whole arena and the robots: the yellow wash. The light's colour now takes the value the scripts set on that copy (the `Intensity` child of the light's own branch) and its alpha, so one flamethrower's light flares while it fires and fades after.

320. **Animation channels compose through the actor tree, not the bank's parent field** [Core (disc) `DE_CAnimationSequenceGroup::ObjProcessContents` 0x701bbf70, `DE_CAnimationSequenceManager::DE_CChannel::SetTime` 0x701c0230; the eight fighter files' banks and actors]: a bank v4 channel stores an i16 parent (+0x10) after its two BOOLs, but `SetTime` writes each stream's position and orientation into its channel's actor (+0x14, or each actor of the list at +8) local frame (+0x4c) and invalidates it, so the composition is the actor hierarchy's; no run-time reader of the parent field was found. In every fighter file the bone actors' parents match the bank's, except that `Pelvis`, `HeadAnchor` and `ThrowFollowTarget` hang from the fighter itself, which plays the root channel `Body`. Seven banks give `Body` parent -1; Warlord's `WarlordAnimationBank` gives it 1 (`Pelvis`), whose parent is 0: a cycle. Ours composed by the parent field, left every channel of that cycle unposed, and drew Warlord's skin through identity bones: the body collapsed below the floor and only the head and an arm showed, in every move (the user's "lying" Warlord). `Body` is now always the root.

321. **A light's colour value is read without a time** [Direct3D (disc) `DE_CLight::Color` 0x2002d6e0 (vtable +0x150, one argument), (2.123) 0x1032dec0 (+0x154); Core (disc) `DE_CValueModulatedRGBA::GetValue(DE_CRGBA&)` 0x70111ae0, `ObjInitContents` 0x701116c0, `DE_CReferenceTarget::SetTime` 0x70006560; 2.123 Core's symbol table]: `Color` calls the colour Value's timeless GetValue, not GetValue(RGBA&, float). A `ModulatedRGBA`'s timeless GetValue takes its colour's timeless value and evaluates its FLOAT at the time member +0x64, which `ObjInitContents` sets to 0 and nothing else writes (no ModulatedRGBA `SetTime` in either Core; the base `SetTime` is an empty return). So a light's colour is its value at time 0, as stage colours are (FINDINGS 94): CinematicMasters' `New Light` holds alpha 0.77 and Unranked Challenger Cinematic's 0.63, instead of following the scene clock through their inter values. A plain FLOAT the scripts set (Blast Furnace's `Intensity`, FINDINGS 319) is still read live, as its value is not a function of time.

322. **The HUD is unlit; its panels are translucent by data** [Core (disc) `DE_CPrimitiveGeneratorMesh::ObjInitContents` 0x7012bbf0, `DE_CMesh::ObjInitContents` 0x701273c0; Pilots `BDefaultHUD` (the background quads' setup); Pilots' `PilotInfoBackground` and `MainStatsBackground` images]: a primitive mesh keeps DE_CMesh's default flags (6) plus 0x4000, never the lit bit 0x08 (FINDINGS 88), so no scene light reaches the HUD, as in ours. `BDefaultHUD` draws its pilot-info and main-stats backgrounds as white `SetBlendUnsortedTranslucentAlpha` overlay quads whose images' alpha peaks at 120 and 181, so the arena shows through them: a bright scene (a firing flamethrower, and before FINDINGS 319 all of Blast Furnace) brightens and tints the panels in the original as well. Nothing changed.

326. **The model picker's panel: push buttons over VE.LoadFrame's console** [OMF `DE_CChooseFighterModelPickerUIManagerRobot` 0x350551b0, `DE_CChooseFighterModelPickerUIManager::SetupControls` 0x35054070; OMFBGGUI `F.LoadFrame` (`VE.LoadFrame`, `LoadFrame Mesh`: materials `MainmetalSurface`, `CutMetal`, `PlugMod`); checked against the original's picker screenshot]: the name, the two arrows, Back and Next are each a `g_ButtonControl3dFactory` object (`NewObject`, so a push button: the standard 2-pixel bevel over the half-alpha grey face), the name's label set by vfunc 0x1b4, the arrows' faces by `SetVisualFace(image, rgba, 1)`; the scene viewer control is a child of the robot's control area, so the page's own 3D actors (the grey console of `VE.LoadFrame`, its yellow-striped glass and plug) and then the controls draw over the picker's scene. Ours drew the picker's scene last over the console and the five as bare text and images.

327. **A particle renderer without an image draws nothing** [Core (disc) `DE_CParticleRenderer::Process` 0x701c30a0]: on its first processing of a render mesh element (+200 == 0) the renderer collects the `Image`-derived objects of its properties (+0x34) into the element (+0xcc); only when there is at least one (+0xd0) does it set the element mapping, `gCreateSingleMaterial` with the first image (blend from +0x54: 0 → 8, 1 → 4, 2 → 0x40), and make the generator's instance (`DE_CParticleGenerator::NewInstance`). With none, no material, no instance, no particles: nothing spawns and nothing draws (the `BlankWhite` default image of `EnsureRenderTexture(NULL)`, Direct3D 0x20008d90, is only reached by a stage that exists). Image-less renderers in 2.123: OMFBGGUI `SpotsRenderer` (the backdrop's squares, FINDINGS 240) and `TestEffect`, Pickups `FighterJetTargetRingRenderer`, Pilots `MoodIndicatorMesh`, PowerPlant `Clouds`. The original's main menu (screenshot) shows no squares; seeded fights are unchanged.

328. **A page's old copy kept answering for its values: the logo's fire froze on a return to the main menu** [Core `DE_CScaleModifier::Process` (FINDINGS 179), script `BSetSceneTime`; ours `World::values`, `GameView` live values]: `VE.Menu Logo`'s `SceneTime` (which `UShift`, `UShift2` and the sheen `MathFunction`s multiply into `Fire1`, `Fire2`, `FireGlow`, `Sheen` and `Sheen2`) is set every render tick by the logo's own `BSetSceneTime`. Ours looked a modifier's value up by data object across every object that ever had values set, in pointer order, and never dropped a destroyed one; the main menu is rebuilt on each return (`F.MainMenu` copied again), so the first destroyed logo's frozen `SceneTime` was found first whenever its address sorted lower, which depends on where the copies were allocated: the fire froze in most return runs measured before (every one of 8 by the whole logo's frame-to-frame change, 2 of 4 by its fire band alone) and in none of 10 after; first visits animated. A modifier now reads the value as set on the drawn actor or one above it (as the original's modifier reads its own copy), then the scene's live objects; destroyed objects are skipped.

329. **Only an element's first shader is lit, and a lit first stage drops its flat colour** [Direct3D (disc) `DE_CD3DRenderMeshElement::InitialSetup` 0x2002e550, `DE_CShader::CStage::Setup` 0x20031760, `DE_CGeometryPatch::Draw`, `DE_CGenericShader::ActivateAndDrawPatches` 0x20032ed0 (instruction listing for the material)]: InitialSetup passes the lighting flag (8, with 4 and 0x20) to the first `GetShader` only and clears it for every later shader of the element, so in a vertex-lit mesh only the first pass is lit (`D3DRS_LIGHTING`, `COLORVERTEX`, ambient material from COLOR1); the later passes (the logo's fire, Gradient and reflection) are unlit. In a lit shader stage 0 is `MODULATE TEXTURE, DIFFUSE`: the flat colour of a stage without vertex colours goes only to TFACTOR (`GeometryPatch::Draw` multiplies the patch's non-vertex-coloured flat colours into it), which that stage does not read; a colour Value (+0x50) becomes the `D3DMATERIAL9` diffuse and ambient (`FlatColor`, else white, specular white, power 5), which vertex colours override; a vertex-coloured element's flat colour is multiplied into its vertex colours. So the menu logo's `Base` (0.22, 0.27, 0.29) has no effect, its metal is the lit `Base` × `MainMetal` × `Gradient`, and the fire is `Testlava` × `Testlava` × the corner colours unlit: the light brushed metal and bright fire of the original's screenshot. Ours lit every pass and applied the flat colour. Fighter models have no tinted first stages and few fight meshes are vertex-lit, so fights change little (1% of a PowerPlant frame's pixels).

323. **Mesh flag 0x80: layer 10, on top of everything** [Core (disc) `DE_CMesh::PostProcessRenderMeshElement` (the material loop after flags 0x100 / 0x200), `DE_CPrimitiveGeneratorMesh::ObjInitContents` 0x7012bbf0; Direct3D (disc) `DE_CGenericShader::AddStage` (the `IsPlacement(7)` test), `DE_CClipVolume::DrawShaders` (case 0), `DrawLayer` 0x2002a2a0; (2.123) `DE_CShader::PrepareForShaders` 0x10332fd0, jump table 0x10333678 case 10 at 0x103335da, read from the instructions]: a mesh whose +0x40 has 0x80 gives each of its render materials placement 7 (0x400 gives 5), and `AddStage` puts a placement-7 material's shader in layer 10. `DrawShaders` draws layers 0-8, then the glow, then layer 10, sorted as 6 and 8 are (FINDINGS 147, 148: highest static key, 1000 per placement time, minus view z first). `PrepareForShaders(10)` sets ALPHABLENDENABLE 1, ZFUNC ALWAYS, ZWRITEENABLE 0, FILLMODE solid, CULLMODE NONE, ZENABLE 0, LIGHTING 0 and COLORVERTEX 0: no depth test at all. In 2.123's data only SinglePlayer's quote frame (`FrameBevel`'s `ColorBox`, `PilotImageActor` and `TeamImageActor` meshes, flags 0x4086) and one OMFBGGUI mesh (0x8086) have it. `BPilotQuote::SetupText` adds a copy of that frame for each pilot quote: the dark band (placement times -1) and the speaker's photo and team image (-10), so the photo draws over the band. Ours drew them as ordinary depth-tested scene meshes, so arena geometry (City's billboard, Blade Pit's walls) covered the band and the photo; they now go to a layer drawn after the glow with those states and that order. The quote's text is a text primitive, always in screen space (FINDINGS 162), and is unchanged; world-space quads that are not overlays keep their depth test.

324. **Axial billboards take the renderer's offsets** [Particles (disc) `DE_CBillboardedQuadParticleRenderer::DE_CInstanceInfo::SetParticleVerticies` 0x400119b0 (the common tail of modes 6, 7, 9, 11, 16, 17), `DE_CInstanceInfo::Process` 0x40010620 (+0x14)]: `Process` sets +0x14 when the renderer's x or y offset (+0x78, +0x7c) is not 0. For the camera-facing axial modes, after the axis a (times height × size) and the side s = normalize(cross(P / P.z, axis)) (times width × size) are scaled, +0x14 moves the centre to P + a × offset y + s × offset x before the corners P ± a ± s are written. So a quad with offset y 1 starts at its particle and reaches one length along the axis. Ours centred every axial quad on its particle: City's car headlight cones (`LightConeRenderer`, mode 7, offset y 1) and searchlight beams (`FakeLightConeRenderer`, mode 11, length 400, offset y -0.75), Katana's blade stream (offset y -0.6) and Jaguar's energy rays stuck out behind their source by half their length, towards and past fighters standing in front of the light. (The same rule, with the corner order, is FINDINGS 341.)

325. **Layer 10's sort uses the placement times** [Direct3D (disc) `DE_CClipVolume::DrawLayer` 0x2002a2a0; FINDINGS 148, 323]: in the quote frame the band and the photo sit at the same view depth (within a unit), so without the static key the band, drawn last, covered the photo; with 1000 × placement times (band -1, photo and team image -10) the band draws first, as in the original.

330. **City's sign block turns with its screens: every render actor is drawn where it is** [Direct3D (disc) `DE_CRendererDirect3D::Render` 0x20026e10 (`DE_CStandardRenderer::UpdateActorPositions` every frame); City `Box01` (`RotateYSpeed`, `Box01 Mesh` / `Edge`), its children `LongSignMSG-Style-01..04` (each with the `LongAdd1` billboard renderer, mode 15, actor space, the first ad after 12 s) and `BottomLight-01..04`; SharedObjects `BActorRotator` (Tick: `LocalOrientation().Reset()`, `RotateX/Y/ZDeg` from `Scene().PreciseTime()`)]: the original has no baked scene; each render actor's meshes are drawn from its current frame. `BActorRotator` turns `Box01` every render tick and the screens, its children's billboards, turn with it. Ours baked the arena's environment branch once and drew live only the actors an `AnimationStreamPlayer` path had moved, so the block stood still while the screens turned through it. Now an arena actor whose world transform is not its data's (the bake's), whether a rotator turns it or a script placed it before the first draw, leaves the bake at its first draw that finds it elsewhere and is drawn live with its branch from then on (`OPENBG_DEBUG_PATHS=1` names each). Compared by world, not local, as scripts reparent some actors (Desert's warning light placements, BladePit's fighting surfaces) without moving them. In the 16 arenas, those found elsewhere at the first draw (frame 9-11) are all turned by rotators or scripts from their first tick: BladePit's blades, City's sign block and light rotators, Iceberg's centre and jump pad, SpaceStation's ring and batteries, Sport's fan blades, PowerPlant's centre and pickup spawner (later, frames 29-119: its lightning bolt ends and pickup effect). The same rotators had left other moving parts standing: SpaceStation's `SpaceStationRing`, Iceberg's `CenterSpinningLoop`, BladePit's `SpinningBlade1/2`, Canyon's gun pivots, PowerPlant's `Centerrotator`, Desert's warning lights, City's `Sparta` and fake light cones. Rendering only: seeded fights are unchanged.

341. **PowerPlant's electric fence: the axial billboards' offset and corner order** [Particles `DE_CBillboardedQuadParticleRenderer::DE_CInstanceInfo::SetParticleVerticies` 0x400119b0 (the tail shared by modes 6, 7, 9, 11, 16, 17); PowerPlant `ElectricWalleffectlong` / `Medium` / `Short` / `ShortInside` (BillboardedQuad mode 7, `BarEnergy`, width 3, heights 171.615 / 100.975 / 49.237 / 39.437, offset (0, 1)) and `RandomWallEffect*`, placed by `WalleffectPositionParent`; the hurting surface is `FightingSurfaceCollisionAffector`'s `ElectricWall` element, `WallTriggerMaterial`, hooked by `BLightningWall`]: in the camera-facing axial modes the half-extents are a = axis × height × size and s = normalize(cross(view direction, axis)) × width × size, and when the offset is set the centre moves to P + a × offset y + s × offset x (the same rule as modes 0 and 14-23). The corners are v0 = P + a − s, v1 = P + a + s, v2 = P − a + s, v3 = P − a − s, so with the default UVs the image's v = 1 edge lies at the +axis end. Ours ignored the offset and drew v0 at the −axis end. Every fence bar has offset y 1, so it runs from its particle (a vertex of `ElectricWallbarrierParticleSpawner Mesh`) along the actor's Z for twice its height; ours centred each bar on its particle, half a bar back, across the walkway, while the `ElectricWall` faces (y 140..228, the perimeter from (−101, −553) round to (−39, 228)) hurt at the edge. With the offset the 22 bars' ends meet post to post on those faces to within about 3 units. 213 shipped axial renderers set an offset, among them City's light cones, the robots' electric stun effects and the Pyros, Warlord and Chronos thruster and beam flames: their flames now start at the nozzle with the purple base of `ThrusterImage` there (they use negative heights with offset −1, which keeps the quad on the +axis side and turns the image round).

342. **The axial billboards' axis is not normalised: a scaled actor's scale counts twice along it** [Particles `DE_CBillboardedQuadParticleRenderer::DE_CInstanceInfo::Process` 0x40010620 (+0x38 / +0x3c from vfunc 0x104's first row length), `SetParticleVerticies` 0x400119b0 (modes 6, 7, 9, 11, 16, 17)]: Process sets the extents +0x38 = height × |row 0| and +0x3c = width × |row 0| of the render element's actor-to-camera system (vfunc 0x104), which carries the actor's scale. Modes 7 / 9 / 11 take the axis from that system's row 2 / 0 / 1, and 6 / 16 / 17 a local generator's orientation row rotated by a copy of it (+0x60; a world generator's goes through the unscaled world-to-camera +0xb0). The axis is never normalised. With width or height not 1 (+0x98) the half-axis is axis × +0x38 × size, so the actor's scale counts twice along it (row length × row-0 length), and the side, normalize(cross(view, axis)) × +0x3c × size, counts it once. With both equal to 1 it is axis × size, once. Cone mode 3 replaces +0x38 / +0x3c with glow × height / width but leaves the axis scaled. Ours normalised the axis when width or height was not 1. In fights on all nine arenas with all eight robots, the front end, Training and a North American Open run, no such renderer sits on an actor whose axis is not unit length, so no shipped effect changes; only float rounding moves (a few pixels by one level, PSNR 98 dB).

346. **Every fighter carries its own ground force: Desert's mines reach Katana, Chronos, Force, Mantis and Warlord too** [OMF (disc) `DE_COMF2Fighter::ObjInitContents` 0x35023af0, `DE_CFighterPhysics::Tick` 0x35039970, `DE_COMF2Fighter::ApplySurfaceCollisionForce` 0x3502ab60; OMF (2.123) the same sequence at 0x107240b9 (+0x5a4, the string `FighterGroundCollisionForce` at 0x107a9b84); Core (disc) `DE_CCommonForce::ObjInitContents` 0x7020cc40, `DE_CCommonForceParticleSpawningMaterial::ProcessForce` 0x7020a160; scripts (2.123 Desert) `BMines` (`MineFieldProcessForce`, `AttackerBuildAttack`); data: each robot's `OMF2Fighter` property list]: the force the fighter physics hands every touched fighting surface (+0x5a0, 2.123 +0x5a4) is not the data's `PlayerForce`. `ObjInitContents` makes a new `CommonForce` for every fighter, names it `FighterGroundCollisionForce`, sets the actor to the fighter, ORs type 0x20 into its default type 0 and appends it to the fighter's properties; the rest are `CommonForce` defaults (strength 1, mode 0, size 1, rate 0), and both the tick and `ApplySurfaceCollisionForce` set its strength before each delivery. Only Jaguar, Gargoyle and Pyros list a `PlayerForce` among their fighter's properties (type 0x20, mode 2); Katana, Chronos, Force, Mantis and Warlord ship one in their files that their fighter does not list. Ours used the listed `PlayerForce`, so those five touched Desert's `MineFieldAttacker` (group 0x200000, `MineMaterial`: traction −1, so every contact is a wall) with no force: no mine went off, the robot never found a floor and slid across the sand in its jump state until it was out of sight (Katana from the side platform's rim: x 1975, z −1207 by frame 2000, health untouched). `MineMaterial`'s size and strength ranges read as denormals and a negative span (bytes `01 00 40 1c`, `01 00 00 80`, `01 00 00 00`, `00 00 00 00`; DesertRings' copy is the same), so `ProcessForce`'s checks pass any force of a type in its mask 0x7a00003f; its hook `MineFieldProcessForce` attacks the force's actor, and `AttackerBuildAttack` pops it up (reaction PopUpHeadHit, 20 damage) with momentum towards the arena's centre (sideways off the side platforms' ends, towards the cross's inner corner near the outer jump pads). Every fighter now has its own `FighterGroundCollisionForce`; the same jump now sets off the mines and the juggle lands Katana back on the stage (488, 27, −142) at 81% health, as Jaguar already did. BladePit's and FirePit's pits, Iceberg's water, Volcano's lava and outer edge, Canyon and PowerPlant end the same way for Jaguar and Katana (destroyed by their attack volumes), and the seeded fights measured (Jaguar v Katana in all seven arenas, Katana v Mantis and Pyros v Warlord in Desert, 3000 frames; four screenshots of a Desert session) are unchanged.

336. **A copied effect's modifiers are resolved from the generator, as World::referent does** [Katana.DEObjects `BProjectile` (`ExplosionState`); Core (disc) `DE_CParticleGenerator::NewInstance` (via `AssureInstanceCaller` 0x701d51e0), `DE_CParticleModifier` spawning flag; FINDINGS 262, 275, 279]: Katana's blade `ProjectileActor` draws `BladeQuads` and `Stream` from its child `Cone`; their generator `QuadGenerator` lists `TravelingParticleSpawner`, whose copy hangs under `ProjectileActor`. `ExplosionState` turns spawning off through `FindChildByName("TravelingParticleSpawner").SpawningOff()` on the projectile's own copy, hides `VisualMesh`, runs the blast (m_BlastDurration frames, then 70) and calls `Die()`. Each reference is resolved from the object that holds it, by the copy rule (a reference names the copy made in the same copy operation, else the newest non-system instance): the actor's renderer, the renderer's generator, the generator's modifiers. Ours looked for a modifier only below the drawing actor and otherwise took the data's own object, so the exploded projectile kept spawning a particle every 11 ms (life 500 ms, about 45 alive, the oldest always near age 1) and its blade glow hung over Katana until it died; it now takes the resolved copy and stops. The same rule gives `SurfaceHit`'s `SurfaceHitParticleSpawner` and Katana's `ParticleBuildupTarget` (`HeadParticleBuildSpawner`, `ParticleOriginMagnet`) their own copies instead of the data's shared one, which changes those effects' rand() use: seeded fights where they spawn diverge from before.
337. **The glow target starts with every opaque mesh in black** [Direct3D (disc) `DE_CRendererDirect3D::UpdateGlowMaps` 0x20026ce0, `DE_CClipVolume::DrawLayer(IDirect3DTexture9 *, IDirect3DSurface9 *, EPlacement, ulong, int)` 0x20029ee0, `DE_CD3DRenderMeshElement::InitialSetup` 0x2002e550; FINDINGS 92]: `UpdateGlowMaps` calls that `DrawLayer` with layer 9 and flag 0x400. Into the cleared 256 × 256 target and its depth it first draws every layer-0 shader whose flags (+0x38) have bit 4, with ZWRITEENABLE 1, ZFUNC LESSEQUAL, CULLMODE NONE, LIGHTING 0, alpha blend with SRCBLEND ONE / DESTBLEND ZERO and TFACTOR 0 after `PrepareForShaders(0)`: black, writing depth. Then `PrepareForShaders(9)` and the layer-9 shaders with flag 0x400. `InitialSetup` starts every mesh element's shader flags at 4 (then 0x24 projector-lit, | 8 vertex-lit, | 0x10 two-sided, | 0x40 / 0xc0 with placement times), so every opaque mesh pass, robots included, hides the glow behind it; particle shaders are not mesh elements and draw nothing there. Ours drew only the glow passes into the target, so FirePit's wall glow (the lava veins) came through the robots in front of it, worst in the close pre-match quotes (North American Open seed 5: Gracy's Chronos and Stacy's Warlord). Ours now draws the opaque first passes of the arena, the run-time actors and the robots (their attached meshes too, which also get their own glow passes) black with depth before the glow passes.

351. **An actor's first two contents BOOLs choose what it takes from its parent: the target marker and PowerPlant's fence posts** [Core (disc) `DE_CActor::ObjProcessContents` 0x7008fca0 (versions 2 to 7: the BOOLs into +0xd8, +0xdc, +0xe4, +0x108), `DE_CActor::InternalWorldUpdate` 0x70090ac0; scripts (2.123) Pilots `BDefaultHUD` (`Initialize`, `Render`); Pilots `CurrentTarget` (Actor, BOOLs 0 0), `CurrentTargetEffect` (BillboardedQuad mode 19, local generator, 13 × 13, `CurrentTargetProjectedImage`), `TargetProjector` (Cone) with its `New Light`; PowerPlant `SuportPositionParent` (Actor at (0, −400, 0), BOOLs 0 0) under the root at (0, −400, 0), with the 22 `WallSupportReferance` passive references at y 539.8]: `InternalWorldUpdate` builds an actor's world system from its local one turned by its parent's world system only when +0xdc is set, and its world position from its local one, turned by the parent's system when +0xdc is set, plus the parent's world position only when +0xd8 is set. Both are 1 in nearly every shipped actor; 0 0 in 30 (among them the HUD's `CurrentTarget`, PowerPlant's `SuportPositionParent` and bolt targets, the pickups' `BoltTarget`, `LandingIndicator` and `Destination`, Warlord's `GrenadeLandingIndicator` and `Danger`, Chronos's `ExplosionSphere` and `GroundHitAffector`, the fighters' position indicators), and 1 0 in 23 (Pyros's and FirePit's `FireTrails`, Force's `MeteorCallEffect`, Gargoyle's `AttackAffector` and `Whirlwind`, the picker's `Spinner`, the cinematic lasers). `BDefaultHUD` parents a copy of `CurrentTarget` to the player's fighter and every frame sets its local orientation to the target's world orientation and its local position to the floor under the target (`TestLineSegment` from 15 above down 300 against the fighting surfaces, else the target's position): with the BOOLs clear those are its world ones. Ours composed every actor with its parent in full, so the marker sat at the target's position taken through the player's frame (in one Desert fight at (909, 55, −374) with the target at (502, 28, −76)), and PowerPlant's fence posts 400 units under the floor. Ours now reads both BOOLs (`de::ActorInfo`, `GameObject::inherit_position` / `inherit_rotation`) and composes by them in `GameObject::world()`, the arena bake, the light placement and the data transforms of the view: the marker sits on the floor under the target and turns with it, and the fence posts stand between the fence bars. Two of nine seeded fights take another course (Warlord against Mantis in PowerPlant, through the bolt targets; Force against Chronos in SpaceStation, through the pickups' `BoltTarget`); the other seven are unchanged.

352. **A particle modifier's Setup assures the channels it writes: Chronos's freeze beam cone points forward** [Core (disc) `DE_CParticleGenerator::Setup` 0x701d5b00 (clears the channel flags +0x70, calls each modifier's vfunc +0x120, then sets those of the BOOLs +0x80..+0x94), `AssureChannel` 0x7008aaa0, `DE_CManualParticleSpawner::Setup` 0x701d3e40; Particles `DE_CNewParticleModifier::Setup` 0x40006cb0 / `ObjProcessContents` (+0x60 rotation, +0x64 orientation, +0x68 momentum, +0x70 colour mode), `DE_CGravity` 0x400026e0, `DE_CWind` 0x400098e0, `DE_CMomentumAndAcceleration` 0x40005af0, `DE_CInitialMomentumFall` 0x40002d10, `DE_CInitialMomentumRadiate` 0x400033f0, `DE_CRotator` 0x40009130, `DE_CSpringSpawner` 0x4000f2c0 `::Setup`; `DE_CRingParticleRenderer::DE_CInstanceInfo::Process` 0x40015c00 (the frame from the particle's orientation only when channel 1 is set, 0x40015ebd); scripts (2.123) Chronos `BChronos` (`Ground.FreezeBeam`); Chronos `FreezeBeamFlash` (`LongBeamRenderer`, mode 6, local; `ShockwaveRenderer`, Ring plane 0, world generator, `OrientationAwayFromCenter` orientation mode 1)]: a generator's channels are its own BOOLs plus those its modifiers assure: momentum for gravity, wind, momentum-and-acceleration and initial-momentum-fall; a new-particle modifier the rotation, orientation, momentum and colour channels whose mode is not 0; rotation for a rotator; orientation for a spring spawner and for a manual spawner with its orientation BOOL (+0x70); initial-momentum-radiate momentum, and orientation with its +0x74. Ours took only the BOOLs. Chronos's freeze beam (double tap forward and a punch) places `FreezeBeamFlash` at Chronos turned as he is, 21 up; its ring generator sets no orientation BOOL, but its new-particle modifier points each particle away from the centre, so the ring's frame is the particle's and the cone opens along Chronos's Z with the long beams. Ours drew the ring in world axes, opening along world Z: to his side, or behind him. 87 shipped renderers' generators gain a channel; what that changes on screen: the 12 shockwave rings with an orienting modifier (Chronos's, Force's vortex, Single Player's) now take their particles' frames, and Canyon's shell effect (mode 15) its rotation. The axial modes (6, 7, 9, 11, 16, 17) read the orientation row or the actor's system whatever the channels, so the fence, the thrusters and City's light cones are unchanged; the freeze beam's long beams already ran along Chronos's Z.

356. **Mantis's grapple: a spider planted on the victim, and why ours never exploded** [Mantis.DEObjects `BMantis` (`Ground.Grapple`, `Ground.Grapple.AttackerBuildAttack`, `Ground.Grapple.Hit`, `Ground.Grapple.TryLaunch`), `BSpider` (`Grapple`, `GrappleAttachToEnemy`, `MantisActivationStateToss`, `Explode`, `AttackAllInSphereExceptOwner`, `BuildAttack`); Fighter.DEObjects `BFighter` (`ThrowableBeginGetThrown`, `Air.GetThrown`, `ThrowableSetReleaseReaction`); Core (disc) `DE_CCreature::ObjPrepare` 0x15016050 (Creature), `DE_CScene::AddTemporal`]: the move is the generic throw (forward + both punches from close range; OMFHelp "2d-Throws"; the HAR Reference lists no special for it). `Ground.Grapple` lunges (`seqGrappleMiss`, `PropelForward(.8)`) and tests both medium punch testers from 0.22 to 0.4; a victim within 16 units starts `Ground.Grapple.Hit` (`inherit Air.NoLand`), which puts it in `Air.GetThrown` bent over (`reactStandingHeadLower`, set and timed every frame by Mantis, no follow or attach of the victim itself). At 0.1 of the 165-frame `seqGrapple` a copy of `BugProjectiles\SpiderProjectile` is made with `g_TossedSpiderExecutor`, its `SpiderStateManager` and `PickupDetector` deleted, and held in Mantis's hand (`BSpider::Grapple` → `PickupSetNewHolder`); at 0.4 `GrappleAttachToEnemy` parents it to the victim at (0, 20, 5), turned 90° about X, physics off, and starts `MantisActivationStateToss(50)`: 50 of its ticks later it explodes (`Explode(.7)`, radius 18.9, everyone but Mantis, unblockable, `m_DamageMedium`, momentum (0, 1.8, 0): the victim pops up). At 0.6 Mantis lets go (`ThrowableSetReleaseReaction(reactStandingHeadLower)` has no branch for that type: the script's own "unhandled reaction type" ERROR, the victim stays in `Air.GetThrown` until 30 frames after Mantis stops timing it, then falls) and jumps away (`IncrementMomentumLocalY(1.9)`, `IncrementMomentumLocalZ(-1.5)`, `DampenMomentum(.03)`), ending in `Air.Fall(10,0)`. The "Unknown Throw Reaction Type" logs are the script's too: `ThrowableBeginGetThrown` runs `Air.GetThrown` before the prelude type is set. Ours made the spider but it never ticked: `copy_node` copied the template's `temporal_registered`, and the template under Mantis is in the scene, so the copy never joined its 16 ms set and sat on the victim's head for ever. A copy is a new object that `ObjPrepare` adds to its set when it enters the scene; the flag is now cleared on copies, so every `NewCopy` of a prepared creature ticks (the grapple spider now explodes 50 ticks after landing, 7% to Katana).

357. **A state registers only with the creature's own state manager** [Creature (disc) `RegisterStandardStateCaller` 0x15013f50, `DE_CCreature::RegisterExecutorState` 0x15015650, `ObjPrepare` 0x15016050, `SetStateManager` 0x15015fd0, `Tick` 0x15016870; Session `DE_CStandardSessionModifier::RegisterState` 0x45016660; Core `DE_CStateManager::GetState` 0x701b1f40, `DE_CClassExecutorInstance::GenerateStatus` 0x70145930 / `ProcessStatus` 0x70145b50]: `RegisterExecutorState` does nothing unless the creature's own +0x204 is set, which only `ObjPrepare` (a `StateManager\Standard` among its properties) and `SetStateManager` (no caller in the modules or the data) set; a session modifier uses its own +0x124 likewise. Nothing walks to a parent or component owner. Ours did, so the grapple spider (its manager deleted, FINDINGS 356) registered Mantis's state while held and Katana's once attached, and `--direct`'s stats components (no manager there) registered their fighter's. A state registered between a creature's ticks is taken back at its next tick (`Tick` → `GetState` of exactly +0x1d4), and `ProcessStatus` zeroes every local that is not `replicate`: when the spider's explosion paid Mantis energy, `NotifyOfChange` registered Mantis from the spider's tick, Mantis took it back and `Ground.Grapple.Hit`'s `Frame` fell to 0, replaying the last 165 frames. `register_state` now uses only the object's own manager. (Separately, `GenerateStatus` writes only the main executer's frames; ours also writes the frames of an outside call in progress, which no longer matters here.)

358. **`IsStandingState` and `IsAirState` follow `inherit`** [OMF (disc) `DE_COMF2Fighter::IsStandingState` 0x35029ad0, `IsJumpingState` 0x35029c60, `DE_CFighterPhysics::Tick` 0x35039970; Core `DE_CClassCompiledMethod::DoesInherit` 0x7013d4f0]: both ask whether the state (+0xf0) `DoesInherit` the class's public `Ground` / `Air` method, and `DoesInherit` steps to the method's `inherit` target (+0x78, the VMC2 `v78` method index) when it has one, else to its nearest enclosing state. A move nested under `Ground` that declares `inherit Air.NoLand` (Mantis's `Ground.Grapple.Hit` and `Ground.Attack.RightKickLight`, Katana's `Ground.Grapple.Hit` and `Ground.SuperRisingBlade.Leap`, Pyros's `Ground.Attack.LeftKickHeavy`) is an air state, not a standing one. Ours walked only the enclosing methods, so the physics' walk-off check (a standing fighter that leaves the floor is put back on it if it is within 2 units below) pulled those moves' upward momentum back onto the floor every tick: Mantis never hopped back off its victim, Katana's grapple back-flip and Rising Blade leap stayed on the ground. They now leave the floor as scripted, and hits on those moves get the airborne reactions. Seeded AI fights diverge from the first such move (Mantis–Katana at frame 1270, Katana–Jaguar 1280, Gargoyle–Mantis 1140, Pyros–Warlord 1650, Chronos–Force 2230); the other robots' throws and Jaguar's overhead throw are unchanged apart from Katana's back-flip.
